# WorldBuilder.Terminal — Command Fidelity Audit (bug-fixing reference)

> ## ✅ FIX STATUS (2026-06-11)
> The **53 CRITICAL + HIGH** findings were fixed by the `eyetest-followup`/terminal-audit
> ultracode workflow (14 file-disjoint clusters) and individually marked **✅ FIXED** in the
> headings below. The 9 CRITICALs and all 44 HIGHs are done; **F75** is **⚠ PARTIALLY FIXED**
> (the 7 named stdout sites converted, but ~18 more `Console.WriteLine` calls reachable from
> `analyze-dungeon-catalog/-topology`, `analyze-landblock-patterns`, `compute-vanilla-baseline`
> still pollute stdout — finish the canonical sweep). A build-blocker introduced by **F234**
> (CS0165 unassigned `weenieType`, `WeenieTemplateDefinition.cs`) was corrected; **WorldBuilder.Shared
> + WorldBuilder.Terminal now build clean (0 errors).** The **190 MED/LOW** findings are NOT yet addressed.


**Purpose:** root-to-tip technical-fidelity verdict for every one of the 205 callable Terminal commands. "Root to tip" = JSON arg parsing in `JsonCommandProcessor.cs` → `CommandEngine*` logic → underlying service/DAT/DB layer → response shape, cross-checked against `help` metadata, `docs/agent_api_reference.md`, and (where applicable) retail DAT semantics / ACE schema. A command passes only if it is **utterly precise and accurate**; anything less gets a finding below describing exactly what must change.

**How this doc is maintained:** an autonomous /loop audits commands in batches, flips checklist statuses, and appends findings. Statuses: `PENDING` → `PASS` | `FINDINGS (Fn..)` | `BLOCKED (reason)`.

**For the fixing agent:** each finding has an ID `F<n>`, severity (CRITICAL = wrong results/data corruption; HIGH = wrong results in common paths or misleading errors; MED = edge-case inaccuracy, stale/wrong help metadata, response-shape lies; LOW = polish), exact file:line, what is imprecise, and what must be done to make it precise. Findings are self-contained — do not assume the fixer read the audit transcript.

**Companion doc:** `terminal_command_inventory_2026-06-10.md` (same dir) — full command/args/handler table.

## Executive summary (audit complete, 2026-06-10)

All 204 checklist rows audited (203 dispatch commands + quit/exit) across 17 batches. **243 findings: 9 CRITICAL, 44 HIGH, 153 MED, 37 LOW.** 35 commands earned an unqualified PASS.

### The 9 CRITICALs (fix first)

| ID | Command(s) | One-liner |
|---|---|---|
| F12 | apply-population | LB-local coords stored in world-space `Origin` — every placement lands at the wrong world position |
| F30 | chorizite-hash-string | hash matches neither Chorizite nor retail PJW — wrong for nearly every input ≥3 chars |
| F57 | export (via remove-building/copy-building) | building delete decrements NumCells without compacting EnvCells — live interior cells fall out of range / get overwritten |
| F72 | export-towns-csv | Vector3 doesn't survive STJ round-trip (`{}`) — every row computed from (0,0,0) |
| F163 | placement-add-dungeon/-remove/-set-scope | dungeon placement edits never persisted — evaporate at session end |
| F215 | transact | placement-* ops allow-listed but zero rollback coverage; project.Save() mid-batch makes "rolled back" permanent |
| F216 | transact | rollback silently skips docs not loaded at batch start; committed batches omit them from journal/tiles/diff |
| F233 | weenie-save/-insert | STJ never populates get-only Lists — weenie-save WIPES all scalar properties under success:true |
| F234 | weenie-template-apply | contract says merge; implementation is delete-and-replace + weenie.type overwrite |

### Recurring defect classes (fix once, sweep all sites)

1. **ushort LbKey wrap** — unvalidated lbX/lbY/world coords cast through `(ushort)`/LbKey silently target the wrong landblock. Canonical: F9; instances F18, F35, F47, F53, F66, F71, F76, F85, F90, F95, F111, F156, F162, F186, F190, F203. Fix: route every coord through the existing `Lb()`/`ValidateLbLocalCoord` helpers.
2. **stdout pollution in --stdin mode** — progress printed to Console.Out corrupts the one-JSON-per-line protocol. Canonical: F75; instances F89, F94 (adjacent), F107, F109, F120, F194. Fix: stderr everywhere.
3. **Tile-cache non-invalidation outside transact** — direct mutations never dirty tiles; cache also bleeds across `load` (F118) and `mark-tiles-clean` splits state (F131). Canonical: F112; instances F143, F157, F179.
4. **Silent-success masquerade** — failures reported as success:true with empty/zero/fabricated payloads (missing data, swallowed MySqlException, reflection no-ops, stale mtime-picked reports). Largest class: F21, F37, F40, F42, F48, F50, F88, F96, F97, F98, F134, F139, F141, F173, F191, F209, F235, etc.
5. **Brush geometry** — radius ×12+1 vs docs (F123), row/col 254 exclusion (F122), smooth flattens-to-mean vs documented neighbor-average (F213).
6. **help/api-ref drift** — ~60 commands missing from agent_api_reference.md or with wrong args/contract: F11, F23, F34, F44, F56, F82, F102, F116, F128, F161, F182, F200, F214, F227, F239.
7. **Hex-rejection inconsistency** — id args that accept only decimal where the ecosystem speaks 0x-hex: F81, F100, F106, F146, F237.
8. **Non-atomic "transactions"** — F215/F216 above, plus dungeon-placement non-persistence F163 and validators that lazily create+persist phantom docs (F96, F225).

### Reading order for the fixing agent

CRITICALs (table above) → HIGH by family (transact/export/placement, then obj-import F148/F149, road F184, rotate-object F189, enrich no-ops F62/F63, benchmark destructiveness F16, spell F206/F207, import-texture F105, skybox oracle F172, mark-tiles-clean F131, load cache-bleed F118, brush radius F123, sweep staleness F50/F229, pairings undercount F74) → recurring-class sweeps (above) → MED/LOW per command.

## Findings

<!-- Append findings as ### F<n> — <command> — <SEVERITY>: <one-line title>, body: Where (file:line) / What is imprecise / What must change. -->

### F1 — ace-db-ingest-creatures / -housing / -npcs / -spawns / -weenie-index — MED: no-DB error tells JSON-mode agents to run a command that does not exist in JSON mode
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Creature.cs:15-19` (`RequireAceDbConnector`), surfaced by all five `ace-db-ingest-*` JSON commands (and others) when `Project.AceDb` is unset.
- **What is imprecise:** The error is `"ACE DB is not configured. Run 'ace-db connect' first."` — but `ace-db connect` exists only in the interactive REPL (`TerminalRepl.cs:187`, `["ace-db"] = HandleAceDb`). The JSON dispatch table in `JsonCommandProcessor.cs` `BuildCommandHandlers()` has `ace-shard-db-connect` but NO command that configures the WORLD ACE DB. A JSON-mode agent following the guidance hits `Unknown command: 'ace-db'` / `'ace-db connect'`. (Engine support already exists: `CommandEngine.AceDbConnectAsync` at `CommandEngine.cs:8022`.)
- **What must change:** Register a JSON command `ace-db-connect` (args `host, port?, database?, user?, password?`, mirroring `CmdAceShardDbConnect` at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3001` but delegating to `AceDbConnectAsync`) plus optionally `ace-db-status`; add both to the `help` metadata and `docs/agent_api_reference.md`. Then change the `RequireAceDbConnector` message to name the JSON command (e.g. `"ACE world DB is not configured. Run 'ace-db-connect' (JSON) or 'ace-db connect' (REPL) first."`). If adding the command is out of scope, at minimum reword the error to say the connection must be configured via the REPL `ace-db connect` or the project file, so JSON agents aren't sent to a nonexistent command.

### F2 — ace-db-ingest-npcs — MED: response field `talkerCount` does not count talkers, and the documented selection formula is stale
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2772` (`talkerCount = r.TalkerCount`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.SiteIngest.cs:87-89` (`otherCount = roster.Count - vendorCount` passed as `TalkerCount`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandResults.cs:1410-1416` (`IngestNpcRosterResult.TalkerCount`); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1967`.
- **What is imprecise:** (a) The roster is `_weenieIndex.Entries.Where(e => e.IsNpc)`, where `IsNpc = WeenieType==12 (Vendor) ∪ RadarBlipColor==8 ∪ NpcLooksLikeObject ∪ NpcInteractsSilently` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.WeenieIndex.cs:96-112,189`). The value reported as `talkerCount` is simply "every non-vendor NPC" — there is no talker signal anywhere in this path (the IsTalker emote-category filter was deliberately removed). (b) `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1967` still documents the selection as `WhereType(Vendor=12) ∪ Where(Type=10 ∧ IsTalker)`, which is not what the code does (the union is not restricted to Type=10 and IsTalker no longer exists).
- **What must change:** Rename the response field and record component to what it is — e.g. `otherNpcCount` / `IngestNpcRosterResult.OtherNpcCount` — in `CommandResults.cs:1413`, `CommandEngine.SiteIngest.cs:89`, and `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2772`; update `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1964-1969` to state the actual rule: "every WeenieIndex entry with `isNpc` true, where isNpc = WeenieType 12 (Vendor) OR RadarBlipColor=8 (Yellow) OR NpcLooksLikeObject OR NpcInteractsSilently; `vendorCount` = entries with WeenieType 12, `otherNpcCount` = the rest."

### F3 — ace-db-ingest-weenie-index — MED: agent_api_reference.md describes a removed `isTalker` side query and omits three joined properties
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1951`; actual code `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.WeenieIndex.cs:61-205`.
- **What is imprecise:** The doc says the ingest stamps "`isTalker` (has emote category 5 or 6)". No such query or field exists — it was replaced by the NPC-signal union (`weenie_properties_int` RadarBlipColor=95 value=8 UNION `weenie_properties_bool` types 83/90 value=1) that stamps `IsNpc`. The doc also lists the joined side-table values as "(Name, Title, Setup DID, Icon DID, PaletteBase DID, CreatureType, Level)" but the SQL additionally joins ClothingBase DID (type 7), PaletteTemplate (int 3), and Inscription (string 16).
- **What must change:** Rewrite `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1951` to: side queries stamp `isServerManaged` (DISTINCT weenie_Class_Id in `landblock_instance`) and `isNpc` (RadarBlipColor=Yellow(8) ∪ NpcLooksLikeObject(bool 83) ∪ NpcInteractsSilently(bool 90), plus WeenieType=Vendor(12) applied at row-build time); and list the full join set (Name, Title, Inscription, Setup/Icon/PaletteBase/ClothingBase DIDs, CreatureType, Level, PaletteTemplate).

### F4 — ace-shard-db-connect — MED: same-as-world-DB safety guard is a raw string compare, bypassable by host aliases; `db`/`pass` arg aliases undocumented
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:8084-8092` (guard); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3001-3011` (wrapper); help metadata `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2434`.
- **What is imprecise:** The HARD-CONSTRAINT-3 guard ("a biota write never lands in the world DB") compares `world.Host`/`Port`/`Database` to the requested values with `string.Equals(..., OrdinalIgnoreCase)`. `localhost` vs `127.0.0.1` vs the machine's FQDN are different strings for the same server, so pointing the shard at the world DB through an alias passes the guard and connects "successfully" — exactly the configuration the docstring says is rejected. Separately, the wrapper accepts undocumented aliases `db` (for `database`) and `pass` (for `password`) that appear in neither the `help` args (`host, port?, database?, user?, password?`) nor any doc.
- **What must change:** Normalize hosts before comparing — at minimum map `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1` to one canonical token (better: `Dns.GetHostAddresses` both sides and compare address sets, falling back to string compare on resolution failure) while keeping the port+database equality. Either document the `db`/`pass` aliases in the help args string or delete the aliases.

### F5 — add-object — MED: help args omit `snap`/`scaleX/Y/Z`, and the API reference presents quaternion components as independently optional when they are all-or-nothing
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2349` (help entry: `args = "lbX, lbY, modelId, x, y, z, qw?, qx?, qy?, qz?, scale?"`); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:766-769` (param table rows `qw`=default 1.0, `qx`/`qy`/`qz`=default 0.0, each marked individually optional); actual parser `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3657-3671` (`ParseQuaternion`) and `1868-1885` (`CmdAddObject`).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) The code accepts `snap` (bool, default false) and per-axis `scaleX`/`scaleY`/`scaleZ` overriding `scale`; the help args string lists none of them (the API reference does document them). (b) `ParseQuaternion` throws `"Quaternion requires all of qw, qx, qy, qz (or none for identity)."` when only a subset is supplied — so `{"qw":0.707}` alone is an error, contradicting the doc table that gives each component its own default. (c) Neither help nor the doc table mentions that the supplied quaternion is normalized before storage, nor that `x`/`y` must lie inside the named landblock's 192 m square (`ValidateLbLocalCoord`, `CommandEngine.cs:10359-10367`, throws otherwise) — the doc just says "World X position".
- **What must change:** Update the help entry to `args = "lbX, lbY, modelId, x, y, z, qw?+qx?+qy?+qz? (all or none), scale?|scaleX?+scaleY?+scaleZ?, snap?"`. In `docs/agent_api_reference.md` add a note row/footnote: "qw/qx/qy/qz must be supplied together or not at all (identity); the quaternion is normalized on input", and on `x`/`y`: "must lie within the target landblock's bounds [lbX·192, lbX·192+192); the command errors otherwise".

### F6 — add-object — LOW: Setup-vs-GfxObj classification tests bit 0x02000000 instead of the DID type byte
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1316` (`bool isSetup = (modelId & 0x02000000) != 0;`).
- **What is imprecise:** DAT type is the top byte (GfxObj=0x01, Setup=0x02). The bit test classifies any DID whose bit 25 is set — 0x03xxxxxx (Animation), 0x06xxxxxx (Texture), 0x0Axxxxxx (Sound), etc. — as "Setup", so a wrong-type id like `0x06003F65` produces the misleading error `modelId 0x06003F65 is not a Setup in the loaded DAT` instead of "not a model id". (Cross-file inconsistency: `AnalyzeLandblockPatterns` at `CommandEngine.cs:4546` correctly uses `(objId & 0xFF000000) == 0x02000000`.)
- **What must change:** Replace with a type-byte check: `uint didType = modelId >> 24;` — `0x01` → GfxObj, `0x02` → Setup, anything else → throw `ArgumentException($"add-object: modelId 0x{modelId:X8} is neither a GfxObj (0x01...) nor a Setup (0x02...)")`.

### F7 — analyze-dungeons — MED: `topStarterCandidates[].sampleDungeonNames` is always an empty array; the API reference example shows it populated
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1482-1487` (`AnalyzeDungeons` calls `_dungeonService.AnalyzeRooms(dats)` with no resolver); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Dungeon/DungeonRoomAnalyzer.cs:100,161` (`dungeonNameResolver?.Invoke(sampleLbs) ?? new List<string>()`); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:993` (example `"sampleDungeonNames": ["Dungeon A", "Dungeon B"]`).
- **What is imprecise:** `DungeonRoomAnalyzer.Run` only fills `DungeonNames` when given a `dungeonNameResolver`, and no caller in the JSON path supplies one, so every response carries `"sampleDungeonNames": []` while the docs promise names. The field is dead weight that the example actively misrepresents.
- **What must change:** Either (preferred) pass a resolver from `CommandEngine.AnalyzeDungeons` that maps lb keys through the loaded town gazetteer (`_townGazetteer`, see `CommandEngine.WeenieIndex.cs:178` for the existing lbKey→name lookup pattern), or change the docs example to `"sampleDungeonNames": []` with a note "currently always empty — no name resolver is wired in terminal mode" and drop the field from the response until wired.

### F8 — analyze-dungeon-topology — MED: counts every landblock containing EnvCells as a "dungeon" (building interiors included), and per-LB analysis errors are never surfaced in the result
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Dungeon/DungeonTopologyAnalyzer.cs:70-95` (loop: any `LandBlockInfo` with `NumCells > 0` is analyzed as a dungeon) and `:64,85-90` (`errors` counter goes only to `Console.Error`, not into `TopologyReport`); response `totalDungeonsAnalyzed` via `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2048-2060`.
- **What is imprecise:** Retail outdoor towns implement cottage/shop interiors as EnvCells (e.g. Holtburg: 123 cells across 12 buildings), so `totalDungeonsAnalyzed` is inflated by thousands of building-interior landblocks and `classificationCounts` is polluted with their 1-3-cell "Single"/"Linear" graphs — the numbers do not describe dungeons. Additionally, when `AnalyzeSingleDungeon` throws, the failure increments a local `errors` counter that is logged but absent from `TopologyReport`/the JSON response, so callers cannot tell a clean run from one that silently skipped landblocks (`analyze-dungeon-catalog` by contrast does return `errors`).
- **What must change:** (a) Distinguish true dungeons from building interiors: skip (or count separately as `buildingInteriorLandblocks`) any landblock whose `LandBlockInfo.Buildings.Count > 0` — those EnvCells belong to outdoor buildings, while pure dungeons have cells but no outdoor buildings. (b) Add an `int Errors` component to `TopologyReport` and `AnalyzeDungeonTopologyResult` (`CommandResults.cs:606-612`) and emit it in `CmdAnalyzeDungeonTopology`. Also update the help description if (a) changes the population semantics.

### F9 — analyze-landblock-patterns — MED: minX/minY/maxX/maxY are not range-checked; values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and double-count landblocks
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3410-3414` (raw `GetValue<uint>()` with no bounds check, unlike the shared `Lb()` helper at `:3632` which rejects > 254); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4383-4386` (loop `for lbX = minX..maxX`) and `:10241` (`LbKey` = `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)`).
- **What is imprecise:** Passing e.g. `maxX: 300` runs rows 0..300; for rows ≥ 256 the `(ushort)` cast wraps (`lbX=300` → key row 0x2C), so rows 0x00-0x2C are scanned twice and their objects are double-counted in adjacency, slope, orientation, and cluster statistics — a silently wrong result rather than an error. Similarly `lbY > 254` bleeds into the X byte.
- **What must change:** In `CmdAnalyzeLandblockPatterns`, validate all four args ≤ 254 (and `minX <= maxX`, `minY <= maxY`), throwing `ArgumentException("'maxX' must be 0..254; got …")` consistent with the `Lb()` helper; or clamp to 254 in `CommandEngine.AnalyzeLandblockPatterns` defaults.

### F10 — analyze-landblock-patterns — MED: slope distribution uses a flat 2-units-per-height-index approximation while the real region height table is loaded and then ignored
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4377` (`var ht = GetHeightTable();` — `ht` is never referenced again in the method) and `:4522-4524` (`float heightDeltaWorld = maxDelta * 2f; // approximate: 2 world units per height index`).
- **What is imprecise:** Terrain heights are byte indices into the region's LandDefs height table, which is not a uniform 2.0-unit ramp. Computing the slope angle from `indexDelta * 2` misestimates the world-space height delta wherever the table is non-linear, shifting objects between the flat/gentle/moderate/steep buckets — so `slopeDistribution` in the response is systematically inaccurate even though the correct table is already in scope. (Same approximation is duplicated at `:4958` in the building-pairings scan.)
- **What must change:** Use the loaded table: `float heightDeltaWorld = MathF.Abs(ht[hCenter] - ht[hAdj]);` taking the max over the four neighbors (i.e. index `ht[]` with each vertex's height byte before differencing), then `slopeDeg = atan2(heightDeltaWorld, 24)` as now. Apply the same fix to the copy at `CommandEngine.cs:4958`. If the approximation is kept deliberately, the response field must be renamed/documented as approximate — but the table lookup is a two-line fix and strictly more accurate.

### F11 — analyze-dungeon-catalog / analyze-dungeon-topology / analyze-landblock-patterns / ace-shard-db-connect / ace-shard-db-status — MED: commands missing from docs/agent_api_reference.md
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (no occurrence of any of the five command names; verified by search). All five exist in the dispatch table (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:177,178,257,258,266`) and in `help` metadata (`:2356,2357,2434,2435,2444`).
- **What is imprecise:** The agent API reference claims to be the protocol reference but has no entry — request shape, defaults, or response schema — for these five callable commands. Agents working from the doc cannot discover `ace-shard-db-connect`'s `database`→`ace_shard` default or the analyzers' `outputPath?` behavior.
- **What must change:** Add documented sections: `analyze-dungeon-catalog` (args `outputPath?`; response `{success, totalLandblocksScanned, totalCellsScanned, uniqueRoomTemplates, errors, classificationCounts[], outputPath}`), `analyze-dungeon-topology` (args `outputPath?`; response `{success, totalDungeonsAnalyzed, totalCellsAnalyzed, classificationCounts[], outputPath}`), `analyze-landblock-patterns` (args `minX?/minY?/maxX?/maxY?` default 0..254, `outputPath?`; response `{landblocksAnalyzed, totalObjectsAnalyzed, elapsedMs, slopeDistribution, orientationBias, clusterSummary, topAdjacencyPairs[≤50], outputPath}`), and `ace-shard-db-connect`/`ace-shard-db-status` (mirroring the help strings at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2434-2435`, including the ace_shard default and world-DB rejection rule). Keep them consistent with whatever renames come out of F2/F8.


### F12 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — apply-population — CRITICAL: objects are stored with LB-local coordinates in the world-space `Origin` field, so every placement lands at the wrong world position
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3471-3499` (`ApplyPopulation`: `Origin = new System.Numerics.Vector3(localX, localY, localZ)`); contrast `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/LandblockDocument.cs:25` (`public Vector3 Origin; // World-space position`) and `CommandEngine.cs:1307-1341` (`AddObject` validates/stores WORLD coords via `ValidateLbLocalCoord`).
- **What is imprecise:** The plan's `localX`/`localY` are LB-local (0..192; default 96). The code correctly converts to world coords for the height sample (`worldOffsetX + localX` at :3478-3479) but then stores the RAW LOCAL values into `StaticObject.Origin`, which the document model and exporter treat as world-space (DAT load does `Offset(obj.Frame.Origin, lbId)` local→world at `LandblockDocument.cs:79`; export reverses with `ReverseOffset(obj.Origin, lbId)`). Every object placed by `apply-population` into any landblock other than 0x0000 is therefore positioned at the wrong world location (clustered near the map origin in the doc model; driven negative/out-of-range on export), while `z` is the terrain height of the CORRECT world position — a mixed-frame vector. `objectsPlaced`/`landblocksModified` report success anyway.
- **What must change:** Store world coordinates: `Origin = new Vector3(worldOffsetX + localX, worldOffsetY + localY, localZ)` (reuse the already-computed `worldX`/`worldY` from the height-snap block, computing them unconditionally, not only when `heightLookup != null`). Mirror `AddObject` semantics exactly. Add a regression test that applies a 1-object plan to lbX=169/lbY=180 and asserts the staged `StaticObject.Origin.X` is in `[169*192, 169*192+192)`.

### F13 — apply-population — MED: plan fields are applied with no validation — lb wrap, wrong-type setupIds, nonexistent DIDs, and silent z=0 fallback
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3441-3443` (lb packing), `:3490` (`bool isSetup = setupId >= 0x02000000;`), `:3486-3499` (no DAT existence check), `:3476-3484` (height-lookup catch → z stays 0).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) `lbX`/`lbY` come straight from the plan as Int32 with no range check; `(uint)((lbX << 8) | lbY)` then `GetLandblockDoc((ushort)lbId)` wraps for values > 255 (and produces garbage keys for negatives), silently placing objects in the wrong landblock. (b) `isSetup = setupId >= 0x02000000` classifies every DID ≥ 0x03000000 (Animation, Palette, Texture, Sound…) as a Setup — same defect family as F6; the correct test is the type byte (`setupId >> 24 == 0x02`). (c) Unlike `add-object` (which rejects DIDs absent from the DAT, `CommandEngine.cs:1316-1322`), no existence check is done — junk DIDs are staged and counted in `objectsPlaced`. (d) When `heightLookup` is null or throws, the object silently gets z=0 with no per-object or summary indication.
- **What must change:** Validate `0 <= lbX/lbY <= 254` (skip + count invalid placements in `objectsSkipped`, or fail the plan with a row-indexed error). Replace the `>=` test with a type-byte check and skip-and-count DIDs that are neither 0x01 nor 0x02 prefixed. Check `dats.TryGet<Setup>/<GfxObj>` like `add-object` and count missing DIDs as skipped. Add a `heightSnapFailures` (or similar) count to `ApplyPopulationResult` so z=0 fallbacks are visible.

### F14 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — auto-paint (and generate-terrain autoPaint) — HIGH: painted terrain-type indices do not mean what the code and response claim — "water" paints BarrenRock, "rock" paints Ice, "snow" paints PatchyDirt
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3974-3983` (`AutoPaintTerrain` height bands: `h<10 → type 0` counted as `waterVerts`, `<25 → 3` sand, `<80 → 1` grass, `<180 → 2` rock, `else → 8` snow) and the duplicated bands in `GenerateTerrain` at `:3873-3882`; ground truth `external/DatReaderWriter/DatReaderWriter/dats.xml:183-217` (`TerrainTextureType`: 0=BarrenRock, 1=Grassland, 2=Ice, 3=LushGrass, 8=PatchyDirt, 0x0A=SandYellow, 0x0F=Snow, 0x10-0x14=Water*); correct sibling mapping `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WorldGen/BiomeMapper.cs:15-42` (uses `TerrainTextureType.WaterDeepSea/WaterShallowSea/...` enum values).
- **What is imprecise:** Against the retail region terrain-texture table the bands paint: ocean floor = BarrenRock (0), beaches = LushGrass (3), mid-elevation = Grassland (1, the only correct one), mountains = Ice (2), peaks = PatchyDirt (8). The response fields `waterVertices`/`sandVertices`/`rockVertices`/`snowVertices` and the help description "Re-paint all terrain types from heightmap" describe an intent the painted bytes do not implement; the sibling `worldgen` path (BiomeMapper) uses the correct enum values, so the two generators produce semantically different worlds.
- **What must change:** Map the bands to the retail enum: water → `0x14` (WaterDeepSea) or `0x12` (WaterShallowSea), sand → `0x0A` (SandYellow), grass → `0x01` (Grassland), rock → `0x00` (BarrenRock) or `0x0E` (SemiBarrenRock), snow → `0x0F` (Snow), cliff override → `0x00`/`0x0D` instead of `2`. Apply identically to both copies (`AutoPaintTerrain` and `GenerateTerrain`), or better, extract one shared band mapper. If the current indices are somehow deliberate for a custom region, the response fields and help text must instead be renamed to the real type names — but BiomeMapper proves the retail mapping is the project convention.

### F15 — auto-paint — MED: per-type vertex counters are pre-override, span unchanged landblocks, and do not reconcile with `verticesPainted`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3974-4011` (`AutoPaintTerrain`).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) Band counters (`waterVerts`…`snowVerts`) are incremented for EVERY vertex of every loaded landblock, but `landblocksWritten`/`verticesPainted` count only landblocks where types actually changed — so the per-type counts can sum to far more than `verticesPainted` and describe the scan, not the paint. (b) The cliff override (`:3986-4000`) rewrites `types[idx] = 2` AFTER the band counter was already incremented for the original band, and does not increment `rockVerts` — so a grass-band vertex painted as cliff is reported under `grassVertices` AND `cliffOverrides`, and `rockVertices` undercounts the final type-2 population. The response therefore does not describe the final painted distribution.
- **What must change:** Count final types: after the cliff-override pass, tally `types[i]` per band only for landblocks that are actually written (inside the `if (anyChange)` branch), and keep `cliffOverrides` as a separate informational counter. Alternatively rename the fields `scannedWaterVertices` etc. and document the pre-override semantics — but final-type counting is the honest fix.

### F16 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — benchmark — HIGH: permanently mutates up to 50 populated landblocks (4500 terrain writes + 2000 junk object placements) with no restore, no confirm gate, and no warning in help/docs
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4037-4258` (`RunBenchmark`: `SetHeight` ×2000 at :4077-4092, `AddObject(lb.x, lb.y, 0x020000A7, …)` ×2000 at :4119-4133, `SetLandblockHeightmap` ×500 at :4183-4195); help metadata `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2407` ("Run speed test suite (terrain, objects, validation, bulk)"); `docs/terminal_repl_commands.md:115`.
- **What is imprecise:** The "speed test" performs real edits against the first 50 populated landblocks and never restores them: heights are overwritten with synthetic ramp values, and 2000 `0x020000A7` static objects are appended to the landblock documents. `BenchmarkMode` only suppresses the persistence queue during the run (`TerrainDocument.cs:111-115` — dirty tracking still works), so the corrupted state persists in the session and is saved by any later edit/export. Nothing in the help string, REPL doc, or response discloses this; `fresh-start` by contrast requires `confirm:true` for destructive action. Additionally, AddObject exceptions are swallowed (`:4123-4125`) yet the iteration still counts toward `operations`/`opsPerSec`, so the reported object-placement throughput can include no-op failures.
- **What must change:** Either (preferred) snapshot the touched landblocks (heights + static-object lists) before the run and restore them in the `finally` block, or refuse to run without `"confirm": true` and document "benchmark is destructive: it edits the first 50 populated landblocks and does not restore them" in help + docs. Track AddObject failures in a counter, exclude them from the ops denominator (or report `failedOps`), and surface the counter in the response.

### F17 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — bulk-place-objects — HIGH: no modelId existence check and no coordinate-frame/bounds validation, unlike add-object; success=true even when every object fails
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4320-4357` (`BulkPlaceObjects`); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3314-3333`; contrast `AddObject` at `CommandEngine.cs:1311-1322` (`ValidateLbLocalCoord` + DAT existence check); help `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2440`.
- **What is imprecise:** (a) `x`/`y` must be WORLD coordinates inside the named landblock's 192 m square (that is what `StaticObject.Origin` means — `LandblockDocument.cs:25` — and what `add-object` enforces), but bulk-place-objects accepts any finite values: LB-local coords (0..192) or coords belonging to a different landblock are silently staged, producing objects that render at the wrong place or corrupt the export's `ReverseOffset` math. No doc anywhere states the frame (the command's only doc presence is the `transact` example with `objects: [/*…*/]` elided). (b) `modelId` is never checked against the DAT, so nonexistent DIDs are "placed" and counted in `placed`. (c) The wrapper returns `success = true` unconditionally — a batch where all N objects error still reads as success (with `errors` populated, but agents key off `success`). (d) `errorMessages` is silently capped at 10 with no truncation indicator (`errorMsgs.Count < 10` at :4334/4350).
- **What must change:** In `BulkPlaceObjects`, per object: validate the type byte (0x01/0x02) and `dats.TryGet<Setup>/<GfxObj>` existence, and call `ValidateLbLocalCoord(lbX, lbY, x, y, "bulk-place-objects")`, counting violations as per-object errors (keep batch semantics — don't abort the batch). Set `success = r.Errors == 0` in the wrapper (or add an explicit `allPlaced` field and document `success` as "command ran"). When messages are truncated, append `$"(+{errors - 10} more)"`. Document the command (args incl. the objects[] element shape — `modelId` hex-string, world-frame `x`/`y`/`z`) in `docs/agent_api_reference.md`.

### F18 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — bulk-paint-replace — HIGH: lbList/rect coordinates are unvalidated; values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and silently repaint the WRONG landblocks
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5052-5069` (`CmdBulkPaintReplace` uses raw `U(...)`, not the bounds-checking `Lb()` helper at `:3632`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Transplant.cs:327-328` (`LbKey(lbX, lbY)`); `CommandEngine.cs:10241` (`(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)`).
- **What is imprecise:** This is the write-path twin of F9: a rect like `maxLbX: 300` iterates rows 0..300, and rows ≥ 256 wrap (`lbX=256` → key row 0x00), so low-numbered landblocks get their terrain types REWRITTEN twice/wrongly with no error; `lbY > 255` bleeds into the X byte. Same for out-of-range `lbList` entries. Additionally, a rect with `minLbX > maxLbX` (or minY > maxY) produces an empty list and the engine error reads `"Provide lbList or a rect (minLbX/minLbY/maxLbX/maxLbY)."` — misleading, since a rect WAS provided, just inverted.
- **What must change:** In `CmdBulkPaintReplace`, validate every lb coordinate ≤ 254 (both the rect bounds and each `lbList` entry) with field-named `ArgumentException`s matching the `Lb()` helper convention, and validate `min ≤ max` for the rect with a specific message (`"'minLbX' (300) must be <= 'maxLbX' (172)"`-style). No engine change needed beyond that.

### F19 — bsp-build — MED: reports built=true (and stages the entry) when BspGenerator silently no-ops; command documented nowhere
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10840-10848` (`BspBuild` calls `BspGenerator.Build(gfx)` then unconditionally `SetEntry` + `Built: true`); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/BspGenerator.cs:50-61` (`Build` RETURNS SILENTLY when `Polygons` is null/empty, `VertexArray.Vertices` is null, or no polygon plane can be computed); docs: no entry in `help` metadata (deliberate-diagnostic is fine) but also none in `docs/agent_api_reference.md`, `docs/terminal_repl_commands.md`, or any method doc — only the auto-generated inventory row.
- **What is imprecise:** For a GfxObj with no polygons/vertices (or all-degenerate planes), `Build` does nothing, yet the command responds `success:true, built:true, polygonCount:0` and stages the UNMODIFIED GfxObj into `PortalDatDocument` — a no-op write that dirties the export pipeline and a response that lies about a BSP existing. `found:true/built:true` is indistinguishable from a real build.
- **What must change:** Make `BspGenerator.Build` return `bool` (false on each early-return), or in `BspBuild` check `gfx.PhysicsBSP != null && gfx.DrawingBSP != null` after the call; on no-op return `Built: false` with `Error: "GfxObj 0x… has no polygons/vertices to build a BSP from."` and skip `SetEntry`. Add a one-line doc entry (args `gfxObjId`; response `{success, gfxObjId, found, built, polygonCount, error}`) to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` or the diagnostics method doc.

### F20 — cell-portal-graph-sweep — MED: ParseLbIdScalar silently misroutes common lbId spellings to landblock 0x0000
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4558-4571` (`ParseLbIdScalar`); consumed by `CmdCellPortalGraphSweep` (`:4480-4495`) and `CmdPvsVisibilitySnapshot` (`:4529`); engine normalizes `rawLb & 0xFFFF_0000` (`CommandEngine.CellPortalGraph.cs:153`).
- **What is imprecise:** The word-form→LB-high shift (`v <<= 16`) only fires for HEX STRINGS with ≤ 4 hex digits. Two reasonable inputs bypass it: (a) a numeric JSON value (`"lbId": 43444`, i.e. 0xA9B4) goes through `(uint)entry.GetValue<long>()` unshifted; (b) a zero-padded hex string `"0x0000A9B4"` has `hex.Length == 8` so it is not shifted. Both produce lbHigh `0x00000000`, and the sweep then scans landblock 0x0000 — typically 0 EnvCells — returning `success: true, envCellCount: 0` with no hint the requested LB was never examined. (For `pvs-visibility-snapshot` the same inputs yield "cell not found", which at least fails loudly.)
- **What must change:** In `ParseLbIdScalar`, apply the shift on the VALUE not the spelling: for any input (string or number) with `v != 0 && v <= 0xFFFF`, shift `v <<= 16`; values > 0xFFFF are taken as full IDs. (A bare cell-low word can never be a valid full LB id, so this is unambiguous for the sweep path.) Alternatively, reject word-form numerics with `ArgumentException("lbId 0xA9B4 looks like an LB word — pass 0xA9B40000")`. Either way the silent LB-0x0000 path must go.

### F21 — cell-portal-graph-sweep — MED: silent reflection-failure path can report a clean sweep with 0 cells, and `success` conflates command failure with content findings
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.CellPortalGraph.cs:311-331` (`EnumerateEnvCellIdsInRange`: `yield break` when the `Tree` field or `GetFilesInRange` method is not found via reflection), `:156-161` (range comment vs code), `:220-224` ("Cross-LB portal" comment); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4498` (`success = r.OrphanedCellCount == 0 && r.AsymmetricPortalCount == 0`).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) If DRW API drift removes/renames `DatDatabase.Tree` or `GetFilesInRange`, the enumerator silently yields nothing; every LB reports `cellCount: 0` and the sweep returns `success: true` — a hard internal failure presented as a clean pass (`GetPortalsList` by contrast throws on drift via the `.Id` check at :324, but only if entries are reached). (b) `success` is also false when the sweep RAN CORRECTLY but found orphans/asymmetry, with no `error` field to distinguish "tool broke" from "content has findings" — callers must know to inspect counts. (c) Two stale comments: the doc-comment/method header say the walk range starts at `lbHigh | 0x0100`, but the code passes `lbHigh | 0x0001` and relies on the suffix filter at :329; and the `!cells.TryGetValue` skip at :220-224 is labeled "Cross-LB portal: target is in another landblock" — impossible, since `otherCellId = lbHigh | otherWord` is by construction in the SAME LB; the skip actually covers portals to outdoor LandCells (suffix < 0x0100, excluded from `cells`) or genuinely missing records.
- **What must change:** (a) In `EnumerateEnvCellIdsInRange`, throw `InvalidOperationException("DRW API drift: DatDatabase.Tree/GetFilesInRange not found")` instead of `yield break` for the two reflection-miss branches. (b) Keep `success` as the validation verdict if desired, but document it (method doc + a `findingsClean` alias field), or better: `success = true` when the sweep executed, plus explicit `clean: bool`. (c) Fix both comments: state the actual range start and relabel the skip "portal target outside the EnvCell range for this LB (outdoor LandCell or missing record)" — and consider counting such unresolvable targets (suffix ≥ 0x0100 but absent) as a finding instead of silently skipping.

### F22 — chorizite-decode-surface-chunk — MED: INDEX16 palette indices read as signed, documented EMPTY status never emitted, dead/false fast-mode constant
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.TextureParity.cs:641` (`ReadInt16LittleEndian` for PFID_INDEX16 palette index), `:96-99` + `:283` (EMPTY documented and counted but never produced — the solid branch at `:380-406` always returns PASS), `:178-187` (`FastModeSubset` array is dead code; comment claims a 50-seed subset from seeds.json) vs `:189-207` (`ResolveFastModeIds` actually samples 81 IDs by stride), `:192` (`using var dat2 = dat;` disposes the caller's `using` database a second time).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) PFID_INDEX16 indices are unsigned on the wire; `ReadInt16LittleEndian` makes any index ≥ 0x8000 negative, turning a valid decode into an `IndexOutOfRangeException` reported as a record FAIL with a misleading reason (latent — retail palettes are small, and the shipped 6,152/6,152 PASS shows it hasn't fired — but it is wrong as written). (b) The `TextureRecordResult.Status` contract documents `EMPTY` ("Base1Solid with ColorValue=0") and `DecodeChunkCore` counts it as PASS, but `DecodeOneSurface` has no path returning EMPTY — the contract lies. (c) `FastModeSubset` is never referenced; its comment ("50 entries total") and the XML doc on `ResolveFastModeIds` disagree with the actual 81-stride sampler. (d) `using var dat2 = dat;` in `ResolveFastModeIds` aliases and double-disposes the caller-owned database — harmless today, but a disposal-ownership bug.
- **What must change:** (a) Use `BinaryPrimitives.ReadUInt16LittleEndian` at :641 (mirror PFID_R5G6B5 at :656). (b) Either emit `Status: "EMPTY"` from the solid branch when `ColorValue` is null/zero, or delete EMPTY from the record doc-comment and the `case "EMPTY"` arm. (c) Delete the dead `FastModeSubset` field and correct the sampler comment to "81 stride-sampled Surface IDs". (d) Remove the `using var dat2 = dat;` line — the caller owns the handle.

### F23 — apply-population / auto-paint / benchmark / bulk-place-objects / cache-ontology — MED: help-advertised commands with no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (no section for any of the five; `bulk-place-objects` appears only inside the `transact` example at :1171 and the op-alphabet list at :1213, with the objects[] element shape elided). All five are advertised in `help` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2393, 2397, 2407, 2440, 2443`).
- **What is imprecise:** The agent API reference is the JSON protocol contract (same gap class as F11), yet none of these advertised commands has a documented request/response shape. Specifically missing: `apply-population` (plan-file schema: `placements[].lbX/lbY/objects[].{category,setupId,localX,localY}` — nowhere written down), `auto-paint` (response field semantics, see F14/F15), `benchmark` (destructiveness, see F16; response schema with tests/memory/gcCollections/extrapolation), `bulk-place-objects` (objects[] element shape: `modelId` hex-string via `Hex32`, world-frame `x/y/z` floats; per-object error semantics), `cache-ontology` (scan-first precondition, default path `<project_dir>/ontology_cache.jsonl`, undocumented `path` alias).
- **What must change:** Add sections to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` for each, mirroring the help strings and the response objects in `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2659-2667, 2725-2736, 2834-2869, 3314-3333, 3371-3384`, incorporating whatever renames/semantics come out of F12-F17. Document the apply-population plan JSON schema explicitly. Either document or remove the `path` alias on cache-ontology (and the `planPath`/`path` duality on apply-population).

### F24 — asset-refs / asset-used-by — LOW: `id` accepts only a JSON string; a numeric id fails with an opaque coercion error
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4881-4882` and `:4897-4898` (`node["id"]?.GetValue<string>()`); contrast `ParseUIntField` (`:944-953`) and `ParseLbIdScalar` (`:4558`) which accept both forms.
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"asset-refs","id":0x02000306-as-number}` (i.e. `"id": 33555206`) throws `InvalidOperationException` from JsonNode ("An element of type 'Number' cannot be converted…") surfaced verbatim as the error — no field name, no guidance — while sibling DID-taking commands accept numerics. Help/docs only ever show string form, so this is edge polish, not a wrong result.
- **What must change:** Accept both kinds: if `node["id"]` is a number, format via `((uint)id)`; if a string, keep `ParseRegionHexU32`. Or keep string-only but catch the kind mismatch and throw `ArgumentException("'id' must be a hex string like \"0x02000306\"")`. Apply to both wrappers (and `surface-fingerprint`'s `id` at :4924, which shares the pattern).

### F25 — chorizite-decode-texture-chain-chunk — MED: command is byte-identical to decode-surface-chunk — the documented chain-detail/MIP/mean-comparison behavior does not exist, and its progress sidecar overwrites the surface-chunk's
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.TextureParity.cs:146-161` (doc claims "records the full chain shape … MIP level selection"; class doc `:34-40` additionally claims it "Compares the mean RGBA against the in-chunk fast path output"), `:209-212` + `:331-335` (`recordChainDetails` parameter is threaded through `DecodeChunkCore` → `DecodeOneSurface` and then **never read** — `ChainKind`/`SurfaceTextureId`/`RenderSurfaceId`/`PaletteId` are populated unconditionally for both commands), `:292` (`chunkLabel = $"surface_{startId:X8}_{endId:X8}"` regardless of which command ran).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) The only difference between `chorizite-decode-texture-chain-chunk` and `chorizite-decode-surface-chunk` is the `command` string echoed in the progress JSON; no MIP-level field exists anywhere in `TextureRecordResult`, and no mean-RGBA comparison is performed — the W4.B contract the doc-comments describe is unimplemented. (b) Because `chunkLabel` is `surface_…` for both commands, running texture-chain after surface-chunk on the same range silently overwrites `progress/surface_<start>_<end>.json` (and vice versa), destroying the other command's sidecar. (c) All F22 defects (signed INDEX16 read at `:641`, phantom EMPTY status, dead `FastModeSubset`, double-dispose in `ResolveFastModeIds`) apply verbatim to this command since it shares `DecodeChunkCore`/`DecodeOneSurface`.
- **What must change:** Either implement the differentiated behavior (gate `ChainKind`/chain-ID fields on `recordChainDetails`, add the MIP-selection field, and implement or delete the mean-RGBA cross-check claim) or collapse the two commands and update the doc-comments to say they are aliases. Prefix `chunkLabel` with the command (e.g. `texchain_{startId:X8}_{endId:X8}` when `recordChainDetails` is true) so the two sidecars cannot clobber each other. Apply the F22 fixes once in the shared core.

### F26 — chorizite-dump-enum-values — MED: unknown `enumName` returns success=true with count=0 instead of an error; vendored-file absence silently drops ObjectDescriptionFlag
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Chorizite.cs:185-196` (name resolution: allowlist filter, then assembly fallback — no else/throw), `:199-217` (loop silently skips unresolved names), `:229-231` (`ParseAcProtocolFlagsEnum` returns null when the .generated.cs is missing → entry dropped with no diagnostic); wrapper `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:379-399` (always `success = true`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"chorizite-dump-enum-values","enumName":"Bogus"}` returns `success:true, count:0, enums:[]` — indistinguishable from a real empty enum and easy for a caller to treat as "no members". Similarly, in a checkout without `external/chorizite/Chorizite.ACProtocol`, a full dump quietly returns 64 enums instead of 65 with nothing flagging the missing `ObjectDescriptionFlag` (the doc-comment at `:81-94` promises it is part of the empty-default set).
- **What must change:** In `ChoriziteDumpEnumValues`, when `enumName` is non-empty and resolves to nothing, throw `ArgumentException($"Unknown enum '{enumName}'. Known: …")` (the wrapper's existing exception path surfaces it). For the full dump, surface skipped allowlist entries (e.g. add a `missing: string[]` to the result and response) instead of silently omitting them.

### F27 — chorizite-dump-layout-tree — LOW: undefined UIStateId values serialize as bare decimal strings; the intended 0x-hex fallback is dead code
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.UiSpriteExtract.cs:308` (`kvp.Key.ToString() ?? $"0x{(uint)kvp.Key:X8}"`) and `:342` (same pattern for `DefaultState`).
- **What is imprecise:** `Enum.ToString()` never returns null — for a state value not defined in `DRW.Enums.UIStateId` it returns the decimal digits (e.g. `"1234"`), so the `??` hex branch can never execute. The `States` dictionary then carries a mix of symbolic names and bare decimal keys, while every other ID in the dump is 0x-hex — a shape inconsistency for consumers keying on IDs.
- **What must change:** Replace with `Enum.IsDefined(kvp.Key) ? kvp.Key.ToString() : $"0x{(uint)kvp.Key:X8}"` (same for `DefaultState` at `:342`) so undefined values render in the documented hex form.

### F28 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — chorizite-extract-ui-textures / chorizite-decode-texture-chain-chunk — HIGH: JPEG RenderSurfaces use header Width/Height instead of decoded payload dims — spurious FAILs and lying width/height fields
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.UiSpriteExtract.cs:806-831` (`DecodeUiRenderSurfaceToRgba8` PFID_CUSTOM_RAW_JPEG branch overwrites its **local** `width`/`height` with `img.Width`/`img.Height` but never returns them) vs `:746-749` (caller emits PNG and builds the record with `rs.Width`/`rs.Height`); same pattern in `CommandEngine.TextureParity.cs:553-567` (decode) vs `:454-460` (`EmitPng(..., rs.Width, rs.Height)` + record `Width: rs.Width, Height: rs.Height`).
- **What is imprecise:** Retail JPEG-format RenderSurfaces can carry header dims that differ from the JPEG payload — including 0×0 (the documented lifestone case; see memory `project_holtburger_white_objects_lighting_2026-05-30` / trevis "JPEG-dims-in-payload"). For such records: in `chorizite-extract-ui-textures`, `EmitPngFile` throws (`Refusing to write zero-byte PNG: 0x0`) or `Image.LoadPixelData` throws on the length mismatch, and the record is reported FAIL with a misleading reason even though the decode succeeded; in `chorizite-decode-texture-chain-chunk`/`-surface-chunk`, the swallowed `EmitPng` means no PNG is written and the PASS record reports the wrong `width`/`height` (the pixel sha/mean are computed from the real payload, so the dims field actively contradicts the data).
- **What must change:** Have both decoders return the effective dims (e.g. `out int decodedWidth, out int decodedHeight`, or return a `(byte[] rgba, int w, int h)` tuple), and use those for `EmitPngFile`/`EmitPng` and for the `Width`/`Height` fields of `UiTextureRecord`/`TextureRecordResult`. Keep `rs.Width`/`rs.Height` only if separately surfaced as `headerWidth`/`headerHeight`.

### F29 — chorizite-extract-ui-textures — LOW: `requestedCount` reports the post-dedupe count, not the number of DIDs requested
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.UiSpriteExtract.cs:735` (`foreach (var did in dids.Distinct())`) and `:789` (`RequestedCount: records.Count`); response field `requestedCount` in `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:599-616`.
- **What is imprecise:** Passing `["0x06000165","0x06000165"]` yields `requestedCount: 1`. The name promises the request size; the value is the distinct-processed size. Callers reconciling `requestedCount == pngCount + failCount` get a true equation, but callers reconciling against their input array length see a silent shrink.
- **What must change:** Set `RequestedCount: dids.Count` and add a separate `processedCount`/`distinctCount` (records.Count), or rename the response field to `distinctCount`. Keep `pngCount + failCount == records.Count` intact.

### F30 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — chorizite-hash-string — CRITICAL: implements a rotate-XOR hash that matches neither the cited Chorizite StringHashExtensions nor the retail/DRW PJW hash — wrong output for nearly every input ≥ 3 chars
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Chorizite.cs:365-381` (`h = (uint)(((h << 4) | (h >> 28)) ^ (uint)signed)` per byte, single `h &= 0x0FFFFFFF` after the loop); doc-comment `:352-362` cites `external/chorizite/DatReaderWriter.Extensions/DatReaderWriter.Extensions/StringHashExtensions.cs` as the source. Wrapper `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:420-431`.
- **What is imprecise:** The cited vendored reference computes `result = c + (result << 4)` (signed-byte **addition**, not XOR) with an in-loop conditional fold (`if ((result & 0xF0000) != 0) result = (result ^ ((result & 0xF0000000) >> 24)) & 0x0FFFFFFF`), and the canonical DRW `StringBase.GetHashCode` (external/DatReaderWriter/DatReaderWriter/Types/StringBase.cs:39-45) uses the classic PJW form (`hash = c + (hash << 4)`, fold on `hash & 0xF0000000`). The Terminal's rotate-XOR agrees with both only while no add-carry/fold occurs (≤2 ASCII chars). Verified numerically: "abc" → vendored 0x00006783 / PJW 0x00006783 / Terminal 0x00006743; "Strength" → 0x0B8C4B08 / 0x0B8C4B08 / 0x0343802D; "BootSpot" → 0x0669A3F4 / 0x0669A3F4 / 0x09814680. Every EnumMapper/StringTable key lookup driven by this command is wrong. (Also note: the vendored Extensions file itself folds on `0xF0000` where DRW StringBase folds on `0xF0000000` — they diverge from each other on long/high-bit inputs, e.g. "MeleeDefense" 0x9F80AE95 vs 0x0F80AE05.)
- **What must change:** Reimplement `ChoriziteHashString` as a literal port of the cited `StringHashExtensions.ComputeHash` (long accumulator, Windows-1252 sbyte add, in-loop conditional fold, `(uint)` final cast) so the command actually matches its stated oracle, then validate at least one hash against a real key in `client_portal.dat`'s EnumMapper/StringTable (per [[feedback_ground_in_real_wire_data]]). If that validation shows the vendored `0xF0000` fold mask is an upstream bug (DRW StringBase's `0xF0000000` PJW form is the classic AC hash), match the DAT-validated form and document the upstream divergence in the doc-comment. Fix the doc-comment's "4-bit rotate-fold / 28-bit accumulator XOR" description to describe the real algorithm.

### F31 — chorizite-list-dat-records — MED: unfiltered enumeration of a custom-named DAT silently returns 0 records, and per-type enumeration failures are swallowed
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.DatParity.cs:311-332` (unfiltered walk gates each type row on `DatMatches(resolved, row.DatFile)`), `:405-413` (`DatMatches` = filename substring test for "portal"/"cell"/"local"), `:318-322` (`catch { continue; }` drops a whole type's records on any enumeration exception with no trace).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) `{"command":"chorizite-list-dat-records","datPath":"/tmp/copy_of_portal.dat"}` (no `typeName`) returns `success:true, recordCount:0` because no `DatFile` label substring-matches the filename — an empty result that lies about a perfectly readable DAT. (b) If `GetAllIdsOfType<T>` throws for one type mid-walk (corrupt range, DRW drift), that type vanishes from the output with `success:true` and no indication, so a parity validator reads "0 records of type X" as ground truth.
- **What must change:** (a) Decide the DAT family from the file header or from an explicit arg instead of the filename (or at minimum, when no row matches, throw `ArgumentException("cannot infer DAT family from filename …; pass typeName")`). (b) Replace `catch { continue; }` with collecting `(typeName, error)` pairs surfaced in the response (e.g. `enumerationErrors: [...]`), mirroring the F21 fix pattern.

### F32 — chorizite-list-dat-records — MED: `compressedSize` response field misnames the plain stored record size — AC DATs are not compressed
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.DatParity.cs:65-69` (`DatRecordSummary.CompressedSize`), `:415-442` (`TryGetCompressedSize` reads `DatBTreeFile.Size`), response field `compressedSize` in `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:748-766`; doc-comment `:29-31` repeats "compressedSize".
- **What is imprecise:** `DatBTreeFile.Size` is the record's byte length in the DAT B-tree directory; the AC DAT format stores records uncompressed. Calling it `compressedSize` implies a compression ratio/distinct decompressed size that does not exist and will mislead anyone diffing against decompressed payload lengths (which are equal). Additionally, any failure inside `TryGetCompressedSize` (reflection miss, missing entry) returns `0`, indistinguishable from a genuine zero-length record.
- **What must change:** Rename the record member and JSON field to `sizeBytes` (update the doc-comment and any consumer). Distinguish lookup failure from zero size (return `int?` null on failure and emit `null`).

### F33 — chorizite-parse-dat-record — MED: ID auto-detection checks the 0xFFFF/0xFFFE cell suffix BEFORE portal ranges (misroutes portal IDs), and the documented modder-range refusal is never enforced
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.DatParity.cs:189-217` (`ResolveDBObjType`: suffix tests at `:196-204` run before the FirstId/LastId range scan at `:205-209`; EnvCell catch-all at `:210-215`), `:50-54` (class doc: "this command refuses to parse from DAT files containing IDs in the 0x__FFxxxx modder range") + `:487-492` (comment claims the pre-flight "happens here") vs `:254` (`FindModderIdAmongIds` — never called from the list/parse paths, only from MeshParity/TextureParity).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) Any portal-DAT ID whose low 16 bits are 0xFFFF or 0xFFFE (e.g. `0x0100FFFF`, a valid GfxObj slot) auto-resolves to `LandBlock`/`LandBlockInfo`, which then resolves the CELL dat via `ResolveDatPathForType` and reports "Record … not present" (or worse, parses the wrong record if the cell DAT happens to contain that ID). Range-based resolution would have classified it correctly. (b) Unrecognized IDs with suffix 0x0001-0xFFFD fall into the EnvCell catch-all even when the ID is plainly a portal-prefix ID outside every known range, producing a misleading "not present in client_cell_1.dat" error. (c) The modder-DAT refusal promised by the class doc and the inline comment is simply absent for `chorizite-list-dat-records`/`chorizite-parse-dat-record`.
- **What must change:** In `ResolveDBObjType`, run the FirstId/LastId range scan FIRST; only fall back to suffix discrimination (LandBlock/LandBlockInfo/EnvCell) for IDs matching no portal range. For (c), either call `FindModderIdAmongIds` on the enumerated/requested IDs in both commands (rejecting with the documented message) or delete the refusal claims from the class doc at `:50-54` and the comment at `:487-492`.

### F34 — chorizite-decode-texture-chain-chunk / -dump-enum-values / -dump-layout-tree / -dump-opcodes / -dump-skill-table / -dump-world-object-taxonomy / -extract-ui-textures / -hash-string / -list-dat-records / -list-dat-types / -parse-dat-record / -resolve-sound — MED: none of the 12 batch-3 commands is documented in docs/agent_api_reference.md
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (zero occurrences of "chorizite"); the commands are dispatched at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:303-339` and absent from `help` (`:2332-2488`) — the help absence is by design (internal parity surface), but no request/response contract exists anywhere outside the source.

Callers must reverse-engineer arg names (`enumName`, `sourceRoot`, `input`, `soundTableDid`/`sound`/`datPath`, `layoutId`/`resolveSymbols`, `dids`/`outDir`, `skillTableId`, `datPath`/`typeName`, `idHex`, `startId`/`endId`/`cacheRoot`/`fastMode`/`emitPng`) and coercion rules (hex-string vs decimal vs integer JSON) from the wrappers.
- **What must change:** Add a "Chorizite parity surface" section to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` with one entry per command: required/optional args with accepted forms, defaults (layoutId 0x21000000, skillTableId 0x0E000004, DAT alias resolution to ~/ac_base_dats), response schema, and side-effect notes (dump-opcodes writes a JSON file even with no `outputPath`; decode-*-chunk writes cache/progress files under /mnt/wbterminal1 by default). Incorporate the renames from F29/F32 once fixed.

### F35 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — copy-landblock / copy-building — HIGH: src/dst landblock coords are unvalidated; values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and silently read/write the WRONG landblock
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4973-4975` (CmdCopyLandblock reads `srcLbX/srcLbY/dstLbX/dstLbY` via bare `U()`), `:5004-5007` (CmdCopyBuilding same for all four), vs the `Lb()` helper at `:3632-3637` that every other lb-taking command uses (rejects >254). Wrap site: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10241` `LbKey(uint,uint) => (ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)`, consumed at `CommandEngine.Transplant.cs:96-97` and `:165-167` with no range check.
- **What is imprecise:** This is the recurring F9/F18 defect class. `{"command":"copy-landblock","fromDat":"donor","srcLbX":300,"srcLbY":10}` silently reads donor LB `0x2C0A` (300&255=44) and, since dst defaults to src, stages writes into project LB `0x2C0A` — wrong block, success=true, no warning. For copy-building the dst coords additionally interact with `ValidateLbLocalCoord` (CommandEngine.cs:10359), which validates x/y against the UNwrapped `dstLbX*192` bounds, so a caller using dstLbX=300 with consistent world coords passes validation yet stages into the wrapped key. lbY > 255 also bleeds into the lbX byte via the OR.
- **What must change:** In `CmdCopyLandblock` and `CmdCopyBuilding`, validate all four (or five, counting optional dst) coordinates to 0..254 with field-named ArgumentExceptions — either reuse `Lb()`-style checks per field or add a `U254(node, field)` helper. Alternatively (belt-and-braces) make `CommandEngine.LbKey` throw on lbX/lbY > 254 and fix the handful of internal callers that already guarantee range.

### F36 — copy-building — MED: ad-hoc quaternion parsing — qx/qy/qz without qw are silently ignored, missing components zero-fill, and the quaternion is never normalized (unlike every other orientation-taking command)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5011-5018` (gates only on `node["qw"] != null`, defaults qx/qy/qz to 0f, constructs `Quaternion` raw) vs the shared `ParseQuaternion` helper at `:3656-3670` (all-or-nothing check, finite check, `Quaternion.Normalize`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"qx":0,"qy":0,"qz":0.707,"qw" omitted}` silently falls back to the donor's orientation with no error; `{"qw":2}` stages an unnormalized (2,0,0,0) orientation into the StaticObject (Transplant.cs:126) that downstream renderers/exporters assume is unit-length; NaN components are accepted. Help (`:2482` "qw?/qx?/qy?/qz?") implies each component is independently optional.
- **What must change:** Replace lines 5011-5018 with `var orientation = ParseQuaternion(node);` (it already returns `Quaternion?` = null when no q-field present, throws on partial/non-finite, and normalizes), and pass it through unchanged.

### F37 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — creature-get / creature-export-sql — HIGH: MySqlException is swallowed in the load path, so a dead/unreachable ACE DB yields success=true with empty overrides (and an SQL export of "-- No overrides defined.")
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.Creature.cs:113` (`catch (MySqlException) { }` — returns whatever was accumulated, possibly nothing or a PARTIAL result if the exception hit mid-way through the 6 queries), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Creature.cs:22-27` (`CreatureGetResult(true, ...)` — Success hardcoded true), `:49-59` (CreatureExportSql loads via the same swallowing path and returns `Success: true` unconditionally), `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3194-3226` (neither wrapper emits an error field).
- **What is imprecise:** An agent cannot distinguish "creature 31226 has no overrides" from "MySQL was down" or "connection dropped after the texture_map query" — all three return `{"success":true,"overrides":{...empty or partial...}}`, and creature-export-sql will happily write a file whose only content is the `-- No overrides defined.` comment. Data-loss hazard: a creature-get → edit → creature-save round-trip during a DB blip silently DELETEs all real rows on save (save replaces rows with the empty set it was fed).
- **What must change:** In `LoadCreatureOverridesAsync`, stop swallowing: either rethrow, or capture `ex.Message` into a new `Error`/`LoadFailed` member on `AceCreatureOverrides` (the XML doc "Returns an empty result (not null) on failure" must go). Then have `CreatureGetAsync`/`CreatureExportSql` set Success=false and propagate the error string, and have both Cmd wrappers serialize an `error` field when present.

### F38 — creature-save — MED: DB failure returns success=false with no error field, and the help/args metadata marks the required `fromJson` as optional
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.Creature.cs:164-166` (`catch (MySqlException) { return false; }` — reason discarded), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Creature.cs:40-46` (bool-only result; also `ObjectId == 0` → false via Creature.cs:122, indistinguishable), `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3203-3213` (response has no error member at all), help entry `:2414` (`args = "objectId, fromJson?"` — but CommandEngine.Creature.cs:41 throws when fromJson is absent, so it is required).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"success":false,"objectId":"0x00007A09","textureMapRows":12,"animPartRows":9}` is the entire failure response — the caller can't tell a SQL error from a connection failure from objectId=0, and the row counts report what was ATTEMPTED, not what was written (zero rows were). And the `?` in help lies about fromJson.
- **What must change:** Change `SaveCreatureOverridesAsync` to return `(bool Ok, string? Error)` (capture `ex.Message`; also return a distinct error for ObjectId==0), thread it through `CreatureSaveResult`, and serialize `error` in CmdCreatureSave. Fix help `:2414` to `args = "objectId, fromJson"`. Update `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1837` likewise if it implies optionality.

### F39 — clear-objects — MED: `all=true` only iterates DAT-resident LandBlockInfo, so staged-only landblocks (add-object onto an LB with no DAT-side LBI) are silently left populated
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1401-1428` (`ClearAllObjects` loops 0..254² and `continue`s unless `dats.TryGet<LandBlockInfo>` succeeds — never consults `DocumentManager.ActiveDocs`), contradicting the comment at `:10295-10299` which claims `TryGetLandblockDoc`'s ActiveDocs-first filter "mirrors the filter used by clear-objects all=true" (it doesn't — TryGetLandblockDoc checks ActiveDocs first at `:10304`).
- **What is imprecise:** Help (`:2351` "Clear all objects from one landblock or whole world") and the response (`objectsRemoved`, `affectedLandblocks`) claim whole-world coverage, but a session that did `add-object` into a virgin LB (no 0x____FFFE record in the DAT) then `clear-objects all=true` reports success with that LB untouched; the orphan objects then ride into the next `export`.
- **What must change:** In `ClearAllObjects`, before (or after) the DAT scan, iterate `_projectManager.CurrentProject!.DocumentManager.ActiveDocs` for `LandblockDocument` instances whose key wasn't already processed and call `ClearStaticObjects()` on them, accumulating into the same counters. Also fix the stale cross-reference comment at CommandEngine.cs:10298-10299 if behavior changes.

### F40 — compare-render-corners — MED: with no scanned ontology (or null doc) the command reports success=true, buildingCount=0, isClean=true instead of an error
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Validation/ValidationEngine.cs:407-410` (lbDoc==null or ontologyLookup==null → empty report, indistinguishable from a genuinely clean LB), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1897-1904` (`OntologyLookupOrNull()` returns null when `!_ontologyService.IsScanned`), `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2094-2105` (`success = true`, `isClean = r.FailedCount == 0`).
- **What is imprecise:** A validation harness run before `scan-ontology` (or in a fresh session) gets `{"success":true,"isClean":true,"buildingCount":0,...}` for EVERY landblock — a lying green light. Entries whose ontology lacks both FootprintCorners and valid bounds are also silently skipped (`:439-441`), so `buildingCount` undercounts checkable structures with no indication.
- **What must change:** In `CommandEngine.CompareRenderCorners`, throw `InvalidOperationException("Ontology has not been scanned yet. Run 'scan-ontology' first.")` when `OntologyLookupOrNull()` is null (matching classify-ontology's gate at CommandEngine.cs:3050-3052). Optionally add a `skippedNoFootprint` count to `CornerDiffReport` so buildingCount's denominator is honest.

### F41 — compare-creatures-to-retail — MED: `novelInLb`/`missingInLb` are misnamed world-wide wcid lists silently truncated to 50, and the `housing` dimension is a placeholder that never computes anything
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2804-2814` (Dim serializes `novelInLb`/`missingInLb`), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.SiteIngest.cs:196-197` (`.Take(50)` with no truncation marker; the sets are global generated-vs-retail differences, not per-landblock anything), `:166-177` (Housing: `new CompareCategoryDimension(0, housingRetail, 0.0, ...)` — generated count hardcoded 0, jaccard hardcoded 0.0, code comment admits "zero-Jaccard placeholder"), vs help `:2406` ("Jaccard similarity of project's spawn gazetteer vs. ACE creature/NPC/housing rosters") and `docs/agent_api_reference.md:2002-2007` (response shape shows housing with the same jaccard fields, no placeholder caveat).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) The field names import per-LB semantics from compare-to-retail's per_landblock rows (where `novelInLb` IS per-LB) into a global-set context — an agent reading both commands will mis-join them. (b) A >50-element difference list is silently capped, so `novelInLb.length` is not the novel count. (c) `housing.jaccard = 0.0` reads as "total mismatch" when it means "not implemented".
- **What must change:** Rename the JSON fields to `novelWcids`/`missingWcids` (or document the global semantics in agent_api_reference.md), add `novelTotal`/`missingTotal` (pre-truncation counts) to `CompareCategoryDimension`, and either report `housing.jaccard` as JSON null with a `note:"counts only — jaccard not implemented"` or compute it; fix help `:2406` and the docs response shape accordingly.

### F42 — compare-creatures-to-retail — MED: malformed/unreadable gazetteer JSON is swallowed into an empty retail set, reporting success=true with every generated wcid "novel"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.SiteIngest.cs:201-215` (`LoadWcidSetFromGazetteer`: `catch { /* malformed → empty set */ }`, non-array roots and non-numeric `wcid` properties also silently dropped) and `:158-163` (missing files likewise → empty set, which IS documented).
- **What is imprecise:** A truncated or hand-edited `creature_gazetteer.json` produces `retail:0, jaccard:0, missingInLb:[]` with success=true — indistinguishable from "the ingest was never run", steering an ML tuning loop with garbage. The documented missing-file behavior is fine; the swallowed PARSE failure is not.
- **What must change:** Distinguish the cases: in `LoadWcidSetFromGazetteer`, return a flag or throw on JsonException/IOException for an EXISTING file, and surface it in the response (e.g. per-dimension `retailSource: "ok"|"missing"|"unparseable"` or a top-level `warnings[]`). Keep missing-file → empty-set semantics as documented.

### F43 — compare-to-retail — MED: python comparator aborts on generated rows missing lb_x/lb_y, and an empty generated JSONL flips the region filter off so retail volumes report the ENTIRE world
- **Where:** `scripts/PopulationPipeline/Validation/compare_world_to_retail.py:89` (`lb = (int(row["lb_x"]), int(row["lb_y"]))` — KeyError/ValueError uncaught; contrast the retail loader's try/except at `:126-129`), `:130` (`if region and lb not in region: continue` — empty region set disables filtering entirely), `:235-251` + `:570-579` (empty region → density n=0, bbox zeros) vs `:620-623` (`volumes.retail` = full-world wcid row count).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) One malformed generated row (missing/None lb_x) tracebacks the whole run; the C# wrapper correctly reports exit!=0 (CommandEngine.cs:10936-10940) but the message is a raw python traceback rather than a row diagnostic. (b) A generated JSONL with zero valid rows returns success=true with `volumes.generated=0` against `volumes.retail=<entire retail world>`, `densityDeltaPct=0.0`, and an empty per-LB table — nonsense numbers that look parseable. The `region.n_landblocks=0` clue exists but nothing flags the inconsistency.
- **What must change:** In `load_generated`, wrap the lb extraction in the same try/except-continue used by `load_retail` (count and report skipped rows on stderr). In `main()`, after loading generated, `raise SystemExit("generated JSONL contained no valid placements")` when `m_total == 0` (or emit `success:false`-shaped JSON) instead of proceeding to a world-wide retail scan.

### F44 — classify-ontology / clear-objects / clone-dat / compare-render-corners / compare-to-retail / compute-vanilla-baseline — MED: help-advertised commands with no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md (compare-render-corners additionally has no help entry at all)
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — zero standalone sections for these six (clear-objects appears only inside the transact op-alphabet list at `:1213`; compare-to-retail is name-dropped at `:2007` only). Help entries exist at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2351, 2379, 2387, 2452, 2454`; `compare-render-corners` is dispatched at `:182` but appears nowhere in the help array (grep: only `:182` and `:2096`) nor in any doc.

- **What must change:** Add request/response sections to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` for all six (clear-objects: `lbX,lbY | all:true`, found/objectsRemoved semantics and the F39 caveat; clone-dat: portal-only + overwrite behavior; compare-to-retail: the full arg list from help `:2454` incl. `WORLDBUILDER_COMPARATOR_PY`/`WORLDBUILDER_PYTHON` env overrides and cacheDir session stickiness; compute-vanilla-baseline: default outputPath; classify-ontology: scan-ontology precondition). Add a help-array entry for compare-render-corners.

### F45 — compare-to-retail / creature-save — LOW: error messages reference CLI-style flags (`--retail-baseline`, `--from-json`) that do not exist in the JSON protocol
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10885-10887` ("Pass --retail-baseline." — JSON arg is `retailBaseline`), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Creature.cs:41` ("--from-json <path> is required." — JSON arg is `fromJson`).
- **What is imprecise:** An agent in JSON mode following the error literally will send `{"--retail-baseline": ...}` or `{"--from-json": ...}` and fail again. The strings date from the REPL surface but both methods serve the JSON dispatcher too.
- **What must change:** Reword to name the JSON fields: "Retail baseline not specified and default not found (...). Pass 'retailBaseline'." and "'fromJson' (path to an AceCreatureOverrides JSON file) is required."

### F46 — compute-vanilla-baseline — LOW: "retail/vanilla baseline" is computed from whatever project is loaded, with no vanilla-ness check and no source-DAT provenance in the output
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6786-6993` (scans `GetTerrainDoc()`/`GetLandblockDoc()` — the CURRENT project documents, including any staged edits in this session), help `:2452` ("Compute retail quality baseline metrics"), output metadata `:6941-6947` (records only generatedAt + counts — no DAT path, no hash).
- **What is imprecise:** Run against a modified project (or mid-session after WorldGen/transplants), the file written to `pipeline_data/enrichment/retail_baseline.json` is silently NOT a retail baseline, and nothing in the artifact lets a downstream comparator detect that (cf. the established "bake-source.sha256 / base DATs only" convention).
- **What must change:** Add the project's `BaseDatDirectory` path (and ideally a content signature of client_cell_1.dat, or at minimum `projectName`) to the `metadata` block, and append a warning to the help/docs text: "computes from the loaded project — run against an unmodified vanilla project for a true retail baseline."

### F47 — diff-terrain / describe-floor — MED: lbX/lbY unvalidated; values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and silently target the WRONG landblock
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2288` (`CmdDiffTerrain`: `uint lbX = U(node, "lbX"), lbY = U(node, "lbY");`) and `:1083` (`CmdDescribeFloor`: same raw `U()` pair); both feed `CommandEngine.LbKey` (`CommandEngine.cs:10241`, `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)`).
- **What is imprecise:** The recurring F9/F18/F35 defect. Neither wrapper uses the existing `Lb(node)` helper (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3632-3637`) that rejects values > 254, so e.g. `{"command":"diff-terrain","lbX":300,"lbY":50}` computes `(ushort)(76850)` = `0x2C32` and returns `success=true` with a diff of landblock 0x2C32 — and `lbY` > 255 additionally bleeds into the lbX byte (`(0<<8)|300` = 0x012C → LB 0x012C). describe-floor likewise describes the wrong dungeon. The wrapped key is only detectable if the caller cross-checks the echoed `landblock` hex against its inputs.
- **What must change:** In both `CmdDiffTerrain` and `CmdDescribeFloor`, replace the raw `uint lbX = U(node, "lbX"), lbY = U(node, "lbY");` line with `var (lbX, lbY) = Lb(node);` (exactly what `CmdDescribeLandblock`/`CmdDumpLbExpectations` already do at `:1497`/`:1630`). No engine change needed.

### F48 — diff-terrain — MED: landblock with no base-DAT terrain is reported as `found=true, hasChanges=false` — "no baseline to compare" masquerades as "no changes"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2366-2368` (`DiffTerrain`: `if (baseData == null) return new TerrainDiffResult(lbKey, lbX, lbY, true, false, 81);`); serialized at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2292-2303`.
- **What is imprecise:** `GetBaseLandblockInternal` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/TerrainDocument.cs:165-170`) returns null when `_baseTerrainCache` lacks the key — i.e. the landblock exists only as live/edited `TerrainData` with no base-DAT counterpart (generated worlds, project caches built from partial DATs). In that case every vertex is by definition "changed vs base", yet the response says `hasChanges=false, changedVertices=0, totalVertices=81`, indistinguishable from a genuinely pristine landblock. The command's help text is "Compare current terrain vs base DAT" — there is no base DAT here, and the response does not say so.
- **What must change:** Add a `bool BaseFound` component to `TerrainDiffResult` (`CommandResults.cs:368-376`), return `BaseFound: false` from the `baseData == null` branch, and serialize it in `CmdDiffTerrain` as `baseFound` (always `true` on the normal path). Document that when `baseFound=false` the change counters are meaningless. Alternatively (smaller): return `success=false` with `error = "No base-DAT terrain for 0x{lbKey:X4}; cannot diff."`.

### F49 — describe-floor — MED: the "No dungeon document" guard is dead code, so any non-dungeon LB or out-of-range floor index returns success=true with "Empty floor."
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:11815-11816` (`GetDungeonDoc(lbKey) ?? throw new InvalidOperationException("No dungeon document …")`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10327-10332` (`GetDungeonDoc` calls `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync<DungeonDocument>` — lazily creates and returns a non-null doc that loads 0 cells for surface LBs, see `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DungeonDocument.cs:140-143`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/LandblockDescriber.cs:201-206` (bands empty OR `floorIndex` out of range → `FloorDescriptionResult(..., 0, 0, 0, ..., "Empty floor.")`).
