- **What is imprecise:** (a) `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync` never returns null in practice, so the intended "this LB has no dungeon" error is unreachable; `describe-floor` on a plain surface landblock answers `success=true, floorCount=0, cellCount=0, verbal:"Empty floor."` — an agent cannot distinguish "wrong command target" from "dungeon floor that happens to be empty". (b) An out-of-range `floor` (e.g. `floor:7` of a 4-floor dungeon) takes the same branch: `success=true`, `floorIndex:7`, `zMin/zMax:0`, `"Empty floor."` instead of an error naming the valid range. (Real floors are never empty — they are defined as Z-clusters OF cells, so "Empty floor." is only ever emitted on these two error paths.)
- **What must change:** In `LandblockDescriber.DescribeFloor` (`LandblockDescriber.cs:201`), split the conflated branch: if `bands.Count == 0` throw `InvalidOperationException($"Landblock 0x{lbKey:X4} has no interior cells — describe-floor applies to dungeon/interior landblocks.")`; if `floorIndex < 0 || floorIndex >= bands.Count` throw `ArgumentException($"'floor' must be 0..{bands.Count - 1} for 0x{lbKey:X4}; got {floorIndex}")`. Delete the now-dead `?? throw` in `CommandEngine.DescribeFloor` or keep it as defense. Update the docs/agent_api_reference.md describe-floor section to note both error responses.

### F50 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — diag-run-all — HIGH: with default reporting, the result is read from the newest aggregate.json by mtime — a crashed driver silently reports a STALE previous run as this run's outcome
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/Diagnostics/RunAll.cs:242-289` (`DiagRunAll`: `aggregateJsonPath = FindLatestAggregateJson(aggregateRoot)` after the subprocess returns; `FindLatestAggregateJson` at `:342-356` picks max-mtime `<dir>/aggregate.json` under `/mnt/wbterminal1/holtburger-validator-reports/diag-run-all`); success formula at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5101` (`success = string.IsNullOrEmpty(r.DriverError) && r.RequiredFailures == 0`).
- **What is imprecise:** Nothing ties the parsed aggregate to THIS invocation. If the Node driver exits non-zero before writing aggregate.json (node OOM, validator-loop crash, disk-full mid-run), the previous run's aggregate is still the newest file → it parses cleanly, `DriverError` is set to null (`RunAll.cs:286-288`, only the timeout path sets it), and the wrapper can answer `success=true` with last week's surfaces, paths and counts presented as the result of a run that never completed. `driverExitCode` carries the real exit code but nothing flags the aggregate as stale. The same applies to an explicit `reportDir` reused across runs (`:250-253` blindly accepts a pre-existing `<reportDir>/aggregate.json`). Secondary: `ParseAggregateFile` is documented "Defensive … missing optional fields land as null" but `root.GetProperty("summary")` at `:419` throws `KeyNotFoundException` on an aggregate without a `summary` key, bubbling an opaque `"The given key was not present in the dictionary."` out of `diag-run-all`.
- **What must change:** Bind the aggregate to the run: the driver prints `Report dir: <outDir>` on stdout (`run-all-validators.cjs:645`) — parse that line from `stdoutText` and read `<outDir>/aggregate.json` only; alternatively snapshot `Directory.EnumerateDirectories(root)` before launching and only accept an aggregate in a NEW directory (or with mtime > launch time), and for explicit `reportDir` record the pre-run mtime of `aggregate.json` and require it to have advanced. When no fresh aggregate exists, keep the existing "no aggregate.json was found" `DriverError` path. Also, when `exitCode != 0` and not timed out, set `DriverError` (e.g. `$"Driver exited {exitCode}; stderr tail: …"`) so the `success` formula cannot return true. Replace `root.GetProperty("summary")` with `TryGetProperty` defaulting all counts to 0 plus a `DriverError` noting the malformed aggregate.

### F51 — diag-status — MED: corrupt or unreadable aggregate.json yields success=true with an all-zero summary — the parse error is captured in DriverError and then ignored
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/Diagnostics/RunAll.cs:368-411` (`ParseAggregateFile` read/parse failures return a result with `DriverError` set and every count zeroed); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5135-5164` (`CmdDiagStatus` serializes `success = true` unconditionally on the non-throw path and does not include `driverError` in the response shape).
- **What is imprecise:** `diag-status` against a truncated/garbage aggregate.json (killed driver, disk-full write) returns `{"success":true, "summary":{"checkedCount":0,"pass":0,"fail":0,…,"requiredFailures":0}, "surfaces":[]}` — indistinguishable from a legitimate run that checked nothing, and trivially misread as "no required failures → gate green". The only honest field, `DriverError`, is dropped on the floor. (The wrapper's catch handles the genuinely-missing-aggregate throw from `DiagStatus()` correctly; this is specifically the unreadable/malformed-file path.)
- **What must change:** In `CmdDiagStatus`, after `var r = _engine.DiagStatus();` add: `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(r.DriverError)) return Serialize(new { success = false, command = "diag-status", aggregateJsonPath = r.AggregateJsonPath, error = r.DriverError });`. Apply the same `TryGetProperty("summary")` hardening from F50 so a summary-less aggregate degrades to this error instead of a KeyNotFoundException.

### F52 — emit-render-gallery — MED: per-pick render failures — including argument-validation errors for radius/resolution — are swallowed to stderr; bad args return success=true with picksRendered=0 and no error
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.RenderGallery.cs:117-127` (per-pick `try { RenderPreview(...) } catch { Console.Error.WriteLine(...); continue; }`); `RenderPreview` validation at `CommandEngine.cs:711-716` (`radius` must be 0..16, `resolution` 64..8192 — both throw `ArgumentException`); wrapper defaults at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1027-1028`.
- **What is imprecise:** Because radius/resolution are only validated inside `RenderPreview`, an out-of-range value (e.g. `"resolution":16`, `"radius":20`) makes EVERY pick throw the same `ArgumentException`, which the loop logs to stderr and skips — the command then "succeeds": `{"success":true,"picksRendered":0,"lbsCovered":0,…}` with a manifest of zero picks and no `error` field. The same swallow hides genuine per-LB render faults: a 20-pick request that rendered 17 reports `picksRendered:17` with no record of WHICH three failed or why (stderr is not part of the JSON protocol).
- **What must change:** (1) Hoist the argument checks to the top of `EmitRenderGallery` (before the pick loop): `if (radius < 0 || radius > 16) throw new ArgumentException("radius must be in [0, 16]"); if (resolution < 64 || resolution > 8192) throw new ArgumentException("resolution must be in [64, 8192]");` so bad args surface as a normal command error. (2) Collect per-pick failures into a list of `(slug, lbHex, error)` and add a `Failures` component to `RenderGalleryResult` (`CommandResults.cs:1469`), serialized as `failures:[{slug,lb,error}]` in `CmdEmitRenderGallery`; document it in the `emit-render-gallery` section of docs/agent_api_reference.md.

### F53 — emit-render-gallery (shared ParseLbFilter) — MED: lbFilter numeric entries are unchecked-cast to ushort (wrap) and unparseable string entries are silently dropped
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1198-1215` (`ParseLbFilter`: `result.Add((ushort)item.GetValue<int>());` on the numeric branch; failed `ushort.TryParse` hex on the string branch falls through without adding or erroring). Consumers: `emit-render-gallery` (`:1031`), `emit-tile-pyramid` (`:1113`), `emit-static-site` (`:975`), and two more at `:1156`/`:1180`.
- **What is imprecise:** The F9/F18/F35 wrap family in yet another parser. `{"lbFilter":[70000]}` becomes LB `0x1170` (70000 & 0xFFFF) and the gallery happily renders the wrong landblock as an "explicit pick"; a negative number wraps likewise. A malformed hex string (`"0xGGGG"`, `"A9B"+"4 "` typos that survive Trim) is silently skipped, so a 5-element filter can quietly produce a 3-pick gallery with `success=true`. Both behaviors also leak into every other lbFilter-taking emit command.
- **What must change:** In `ParseLbFilter`, replace the numeric branch with a range check: `var v = item.GetValue<long>(); if (v < 0 || v > 0xFFFF) throw new ArgumentException($"lbFilter entry {v} out of range 0..0xFFFF"); result.Add((ushort)v);` and make the string branch throw `ArgumentException($"lbFilter entry '{s}' is not a 16-bit hex landblock key")` when `TryParse` fails, instead of falling through.

### F54 — dump-lb-expectations — MED: `out` file-write failure is stderr-only; the response stays success=true with no field saying whether (or where) the oracle file was written
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1855-1866` (`Serialize(object, string outPath)`: catch writes `[dump-lb-expectations] out write failed…` to `Console.Error` and returns the payload unchanged); call site `:1710`.
- **What is imprecise:** The wire-agent workflow this command exists for ("write to `apps/holtburger-web/oracles/0xLLLL0000.json`, then the browser fetches by URL") depends on the disk write, yet a bad directory, permissions error, or full disk produces a normal `success=true` response whose JSON body is fine while the oracle file silently never lands — the failure is only on stderr, which is not part of the JSON protocol. The response also never echoes `outPath`, so the caller can't even confirm intent.
- **What must change:** Thread the outcome into the response: have the outPath-aware `Serialize` overload report success/failure (e.g. return `(string json, string? writeError)` or accept an `out` param), and add `outPath` (echoed when provided) plus `outWritten: true|false` and `outError` (message, null on success) to the `CmdDumpLbExpectations` payload before serialization (build the anonymous object with these fields, then write). Keep `success=true` for the stdout payload but make the write failure machine-visible.

### F55 — defragment-dat — MED: the DRW Defragment extension silently omits any entry whose bytes fail to read — the "defragmented" DAT can lose files with no warning, and bytesFreed counts the loss as savings
- **Where:** `DatReaderWriter.Extensions/DatReaderWriter.Extensions/DatDatabaseExtensions.cs:35-52` (`Defragment`: `if (db.TryGetFileBytes(file.Id, out var bytes)) { …copy… }` — no else branch; return `db.Header.FileSize - newDb.Header.FileSize` at `:58`); invoked from `CommandEngine.cs:2576`; result surfaced via `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2547-2549`.
- **What is imprecise:** A source DAT with corrupt/unreadable entries — precisely the DAT an operator would reach for a defragment tool to clean up — yields `success=true` and a positive `bytesFreed` while the output is missing files relative to the source, with zero indication in the response, the result record, or even a console line. `bytesFreed` then conflates genuine free-block reclamation with dropped-file bytes.
- **What must change:** In `Defragment`, count read failures (`int skipped = 0; … else skipped++;`) and either (a) change the return to a small result struct `(int BytesFreed, int FilesCopied, int FilesSkipped)` or (b) add an `out int skippedEntries` parameter. In `CommandEngine.DefragmentDat` (`CommandEngine.cs:2546-2581`) surface it: add `FilesCopied`/`FilesSkipped` to `DefragmentDatResult` (`CommandResults.cs:400-402`), serialize them in `CmdDefragmentDat`, and when `FilesSkipped > 0` either set `success=false` or include a warning string naming the count so the output DAT is never mistaken for a complete copy.

### F56 — describe-landblock / dump-lb-expectations / diff-terrain / defragment-dat / difficulty-gradient / diag-run-all / diag-status — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md; diag-* additionally documented nowhere at all
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — zero sections for any of the seven (grep matches only name-drops inside other commands' prose at `:1221`, `:1584`, `:2017`, `:2051`). Help-array entries exist for five (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2457` describe-landblock, `:2458` dump-lb-expectations, `:2375` diff-terrain, `:2380` defragment-dat, `:2396` difficulty-gradient), but `diag-run-all`/`diag-status` (dispatched at `:374-375`) appear in NO help entry, NO api-reference section, and NO `docs/agent_api_schema.json` entry — they are discoverable only by reading source.
- **What is imprecise:** Same gap class as F11/F23/F34/F44. Specific undocumented behaviors an agent needs: describe-landblock's optional `includeFootprints` (gates `footprintWorld` polygons, `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1498`) appears nowhere; dump-lb-expectations' optional `out`, `sceneryBakeDir`, `eventsBakeDir` fields and the hardcoded `/mnt/wbterminal1/holtburger-dist-v2/{scenery,events}/` defaults live only in code comments (`:1626-1646`); defragment-dat operates on the project's pristine BASE dats (`project.BaseDatDirectory`, `CommandEngine.cs:2550`) — staged in-session edits are NOT in the output, and nothing says so; difficulty-gradient accepts `path` as an alias for `gradientPath` and defaults to `<projectDir>/../difficulty_gradient.json` (`CommandEngine.cs:3375-3378`); diag-run-all's args (`wave4Mode?`, `reportDir?`, `skipSurfaces?[]`, `parallel?`), its 2-hour timeout, the `WORLDBUILDER_DIAG_DRIVER`/`WORLDBUILDER_NODE` env overrides, and its required-failures success gate are all source-only.
- **What must change:** Add request/response sections to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` for all seven commands covering the fields above (describe-landblock can reference its existing response shape from the describe-floor section); add help-array entries for `diag-run-all` (`args = "wave4Mode?, reportDir?, skipSurfaces?, parallel?"`) and `diag-status` (`args = ""`); add both diag commands plus the five others to `docs/agent_api_schema.json`'s `commands` map; in the defragment-dat description state "defragments the project's BASE dat (read-only source) into outputPath — staged project edits are not included; export first if you need them."

### F57 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — export — CRITICAL: deleting a building decrements NumCells without deleting/compacting its EnvCells — surviving buildings' cells fall out of the loadable range and newly placed buildings overwrite live cells
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/LandblockDocument.cs:207-218` (delete path: `lbi.NumCells -= (uint)deletedCellIds.Count;` at `:213`, cells left in the DAT — the referenced "Phase 2" cleanup does not exist anywhere in the codebase, `:211` is its only mention) and `:241-247` (new-building path passes the shrunk `lbi.NumCells` into `BuildingBlueprintCache.InstantiateBlueprint`), with `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/BuildingBlueprintCache.cs:450` assigning new cell IDs as `nextCellId = (ushort)(currentNumCells + 0x0100)`.
- **What is imprecise:** Both ACE's DatLoader and the retail client enumerate a landblock's interior cells as the CONTIGUOUS range `0x0100 .. 0x0100 + NumCells - 1`. The code treats NumCells as a free-floating count: (a) deleting any building whose cells are NOT the topmost block (e.g. building A owns 0x0100-0x010F, building B owns 0x0110-0x011F; delete A → NumCells 32→16) leaves B's cells 0x0110-0x011F outside the enumerated range — B's interior silently stops loading (walk-through shell) while A's orphaned cells 0x0100-0x010F are the ones still served; (b) placing a NEW building after such a delete assigns it IDs starting at `0x0100 + 16 = 0x0110`, overwriting building B's live EnvCells in the export DAT — direct data corruption. Reachable from the terminal via `remove-building` + `copy-building`/`add-object` staged in one session then `export`. `(ushort)(currentNumCells + 0x0100)` is also unchecked against the 0xFFFD ceiling (wraps).
- **What must change:** On building delete, either physically renumber: collect the deleted cell-ID set, delete those records from the export DAT, and remap every surviving EnvCell above the gap downward (rewriting their `Id`, all `CellPortals[].OtherCellId`, `VisibleCells[]`, `BuildingInfo.Portals[].OtherCellId`/`StabList` references) so the 0x0100..NumCells range stays contiguous — or refuse: keep NumCells unchanged when the deleted block isn't the topmost contiguous block and emit a diagnostic. In `InstantiateBlueprint`, allocate new IDs from `max(existing cell suffix)+1` rather than `0x0100+NumCells`, and throw if `nextCellId + cellCount - 1 > 0xFFFD`. Add a regression test: LB with two buildings, delete the first, add a third, export, assert every surviving building's cells are inside `0x0100..0x0100+NumCells-1` and no pre-existing cell record changed.

### F58 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — export — HIGH: every sub-save failure is swallowed — `Project.ExportDats` can only return true, so `success:true` is reported even when terrain/LBI/dungeon/portal writes failed
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Models/Project.cs:327-329` (`if (!writer.TrySave(lb, portalIteration)) { /* "Log error? … or ignore." — empty block */ }`), `:486` (`lbDoc.SaveToDats(writer, portalIteration);` — returns `Task<bool>`, result discarded; `LandblockDocument.SaveToDatsInternal` returns false on LBI/LandBlock save failure at `LandblockDocument.cs:273-276, 285-289`), `:505-515` (dungeon/portal/layout `SaveToDats` results likewise discarded), `:575` (the method's only `return` is `return true;` — verified no other return path). Surfaced via `CommandEngine.cs:307-311` (`Export` → `new ExportResult(success, …)`) and `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1329-1332`.
- **What is imprecise:** The audit doc's highest-stakes command writes DAT bytes to disk and reports `success:true` regardless of how many individual landblock/EnvCell/LBI/dungeon writes failed. An agent (or the transplant pipeline, which depends on export-time blueprint instantiation) has no signal that the exported DAT is partial. `InstantiateBlueprint` failures at least degrade to "place as regular object" with a log line, but TrySave failures during terrain write and static-reposition (`:376` also unchecked) vanish entirely.
- **What must change:** Count failures in `ExportDats` (`int terrainSaveFailures`, `int docSaveFailures` — check `TrySave` and `SaveToDats(...).GetAwaiter().GetResult()` results) and return them: change the signature to return a small result record `(bool Success, int TerrainWritten, int TerrainSaveFailures, int DocsSaved, int DocSaveFailures)` (or add an `out` aggregate), with `Success = (failures == 0)`. Propagate through `HeadlessProjectManager.ExportDats` (`HeadlessProjectManager.cs:117-137`) and `CommandEngine.Export`, and serialize the failure counts in the `export` response so `success:false` + counts replaces the current unconditional true.

### F59 — export — MED: passing `iteration` equal to the base DAT's current iteration silently rewrites it to 0, and the response echoes the requested value (or null) rather than what was written
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Models/Project.cs:213-215` (`if (portalIteration == DatReaderWriter.Dats.Portal.Iteration.CurrentIteration) { portalIteration = 0; }`); per-entry effect via `DefaultDatReaderWriter.TrySave` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/DefaultDatReaderWriter.cs:87`, default `int? iteration = 0`) → DRW `TryWriteFile` sets `entry.Iteration = 0` (`external/DatReaderWriter/DatReaderWriter/DatDatabase.cs:515`); response echo at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1331-1332` (`iteration = r.Iteration` = the raw request arg).
- **What is imprecise:** An agent that reads the project's current portal iteration N and passes `{"iteration": N}` gets every written file entry stamped `Iteration=0` (lower than every existing entry) with no warning, while the JSON response claims `iteration:N`. When `iteration` is omitted, the response reports `iteration:null` even though `HeadlessProjectManager.cs:122-123` actually exported with current+1 — the caller can never learn the effective iteration. Also `Project.cs:531` (`writer.Dats.Portal.Iteration.CurrentIteration = portalIteration`) is an in-memory mutation immediately before `Dispose()` — the on-disk iteration record is only ever updated by DRW's per-write `entry.Iteration > CurrentIteration` hook (`DatDatabase.cs:819-821`), so that line is dead code; the cell DAT's own iteration record is never managed at all (`:530` TODO).
- **What must change:** Delete the `== CurrentIteration → 0` rewrite (or replace it with an explicit `ArgumentException("iteration N is already the current iteration")`); thread the EFFECTIVE iteration back through `HeadlessProjectManager.ExportDats` → `ExportResult` and serialize it (`iteration = <effective>`, never null); remove the dead `:531` assignment or write the Iteration record explicitly.

### F60 — export — MED: `reposition:true` computes terrain deltas WITHOUT export-layer composition, so ACE-DB instance heights diverge from the DAT that was just exported
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:313-400` (`ExportWithRepositionAsync`): `modifiedLbs` = `terrainDoc.TerrainData.Landblocks.Keys` only (`:335`) and `newTerrain[lbKey]` = raw `terrainDoc.GetLandblockInternal(lbKey)` (`:362-367`, which returns a fresh conversion of base doc data — `TerrainDocument.cs:127-137`). Compare `Project.ExportDats` (`Project.cs:220-311`): the DAT write composites every export layer's per-field-masked cells on top (`:280-306`) and includes layer-touched LBs in `modifiedLandblocks` (`:232-240`).
- **What is imprecise:** When the project has export-enabled terrain layers carrying height edits, the DAT receives layer-composited heights but the reposition service receives pre-layer heights — `InstanceRepositionService` then computes wrong (or zero) Z deltas, and LBs modified ONLY by a layer are skipped from reposition entirely. Result: ACE `landblock_instance` rows that float/sink relative to the exported terrain — the exact failure the reposition feature exists to prevent. Additionally, the `reposition` request flag itself is absent from the `export` parameter table in `docs/agent_api_reference.md:241-264` (the extended response fields `exportSuccess`/`repositionAttempted`/etc. are likewise undocumented).
- **What must change:** Reuse the composited snapshots: have `Project.ExportDats` expose the `oldTerrain`/`newTerrain`/`modifiedLandblocks` it already builds (it constructs exactly these at `:247-311`) — e.g. via an out parameter or by invoking the existing `OnExportReposition` hook — and make `ExportWithRepositionAsync` consume that instead of re-deriving from the raw terrain doc. Document `reposition` and the extended response shape in agent_api_reference.md.

### F61 — export — MED: VisibleCells LandCell fixup is translation-only and clamps to the same landblock's 8×8 grid — rotated or LB-edge placements keep stale outdoor visibility references
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/BuildingBlueprintCache.cs:456-460` (delta from `PositionToOutdoorCell` of donor vs new origin only — orientation ignored), `:519-534` (per-reference `Math.Clamp(... , 0, 7)`), same pattern in `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/LandblockDocument.cs:396-404` + the corresponding loop in `MoveBuildingEnvCells`.
- **What is imprecise:** The fixup's stated purpose (comment at `:511-518`) is to preserve "the cell one step north" so ACE `find_transit_cells` portal lookups succeed. Two cases silently produce wrong references: (a) a building instantiated with a different orientation than its donor — the donor's LandCell offsets rotate with the building, but the applied delta is pure translation, so every fixed-up reference points at the un-rotated neighbor; (b) a destination outdoor cell near the LB boundary where `donorCell + delta` leaves 0..7 — the correct visible cell lives in the ADJACENT landblock (not representable as a 0x0001-0x0040 local ID), and clamping substitutes the nearest same-LB cell instead, recreating the one-sided walk-through-wall symptom the fixup was added to fix, with only a debug-level trace.
- **What must change:** (a) Rotate the donor→cell offset by the yaw delta between donor and destination orientation before recomposing (`offset' = R(yawDelta)·(donorCellXY − donorOriginCellXY)`), quantized to the 24 m grid; (b) when the corrected coordinate falls outside 0..7, drop the reference (removing a VisibleCells entry is safe — it only reduces PVS) instead of clamping to a wrong cell, and count dropped refs in the export log so an operator can see edge placements that lost outdoor visibility.

### F62 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — enrich-ontology — HIGH: enrichment is dead against the shipped schema — no entry has `setupId` or `model_prefix`, so the command always returns success=true with entriesEnriched=0
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2891-3026` (`EnrichOntology`); schema at `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Resources/object_ontology_schema.json`.
- **What is imprecise:** The code's only two enrichment paths key off `entry.setupId` (`:2987`) and `entry.wcid_pool` + `entry.model_prefix` (`:2955-2960`). The shipped schema's 182 entries contain ZERO `setupId` keys and ZERO `model_prefix` keys (verified by parsing the JSON; the 28 `wcid_pool` creature-family entries have `creature_family` at the ENTRY level, not under `tags` where `:2948` looks, and no `model_prefix`). Net effect: `enrich-ontology` is a guaranteed no-op that reports success — the help text "Enrich ontology with schema names & creature families" describes behavior that cannot occur. Compounding details: (i) even if `model_prefix` were present in the documented short form ("0x0200" per the comment at `:2963`), the match `(ontEntry.ObjectId >> 16) == (prefixVal >> 16)` at `:2970` compares `0x0200` against `0x0200 >> 16 == 0` — only a full 8-digit prefix would ever match; (ii) the "Override category from curated data" block at `:3009-3014` sets `ClassificationSource = "Schema"` but never assigns `entry.Category`; (iii) the embedded-resource load at `:2897-2907` always misses because `WorldBuilder.Shared.csproj` declares no `EmbeddedResource` items — the command works only when the source-tree fallback paths at `:2910-2921` resolve (dev checkout layout).
- **What must change:** Either align the code to the real schema (iterate `entries[]`, match creature families via `wcid_pool[].wcid` against ontology `WeenieClassId` — populated by enrich-weenies — and read `creature_family`/`tags.type` from their actual locations) or align the schema (add `setupId`/`model_prefix` fields). Fix the prefix comparison to compare against the documented short form (`(ontEntry.ObjectId >> 16) == prefixVal` when the prefix has ≤4 hex digits). Make the category-override block actually assign `entry.Category = topLevel`. Embed the schema (`<EmbeddedResource Include="Lib/Resources/object_ontology_schema.json" />`) so installed binaries work. Return `entriesEnriched=0` as `success=false` with an explanatory error until the matching paths are live, so agents stop treating the no-op as done.

### F63 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — enrich-materials — HIGH: "Tag materials from texture analysis" performs no texture analysis — the only tag it can ever produce is a blanket "textured"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/OntologyService.cs:945-1014` (`EnrichMaterials`), `:1044-1058` (`ClassifyTextureById` — body is an empty if-statement, adds nothing to `materials`), `:1063-1074` (`ClassifySurfaceByRange` — adds the constant `"textured"` for any 0x08 surface id). Wrapper `CommandEngine.cs:3081-3095`; help row `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2388`.
- **What is imprecise:** The 10-entry `materialKeywords` map (stone/wood/metal/fire/water/cloth/crystal/bone/moss/bark, `:947-958`) is never read — dead code. `Surface.OrigTextureId` is fetched (`:986`) and fed into a classifier that cannot emit a tag. So `entriesEnriched` counts exactly "entries that have ≥1 parseable 0x08 surface id", every one tagged `textured` and nothing else; `MaterialTags` never contains a material. The help description and the command name both promise material classification that does not exist; downstream consumers filtering on `MaterialTags` get a constant.
- **What must change:** Either implement the classification (e.g. resolve `OrigTextureId` → `SurfaceTexture`/`RenderSurface`, run the existing keyword map against mined string-table names, or use palette/colour statistics) so the keyword map is live, or strip the dead map + `ClassifyTextureById` and rename the surfaced behavior honestly (help: "Tag entries that reference Surface (0x08) records with 'textured'"). If keeping the current behavior, also rename the response semantics in docs so `entriesEnriched` is understood as "entries with any surface data".

### F64 — enrich-canonical — MED: `entriesEnriched` undercounts — name/level/wcid/tags merges don't set the `changed` flag
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/OntologyService.cs:1297-1325` (`EnrichFromCanonical`: the Name fill `:1298-1302`, Level `:1305-1307`, WeenieClassId `:1310-1312` and tag-merge `:1315-1323` blocks all mutate the entry but never set `changed = true`; only architecture/biome/behavior/creature_family/difficulty_tier do).
- **What is imprecise:** A canonical entry that contributes only a name, level, wcid, or new tags is applied to the live ontology but not counted — the response's `entriesEnriched` (surfaced at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2698-2701`) reads lower than the number of entries actually modified, and a file consisting solely of such entries reports `entriesEnriched: 0` despite changing state.
- **What must change:** Set `changed = true` in each of the four blocks when a mutation actually occurs (name filled, level set, wcid set, ≥1 tag appended). Keep the existing semantics of not counting no-op overwrites.

### F65 — enum-parity-report — MED: the `sourceRoot` argument is a no-op that is echoed back as `choriziteSourceRoot` as if it had been used
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.EnumParity.cs:334-365` (`EnumParityReportCommand`: `chorRoot` is computed at `:335` and used ONLY in the response record `:358`; the Chorizite-side dump at `:340` calls `ChoriziteDumpEnumValues(null)`, which reflects over the compiled `Chorizite.Common` assembly — `CommandEngine.Chorizite.cs:178-183` — and its ACProtocol regex fallback reads from `DefaultChoriziteSourceRoot` unconditionally at `CommandEngine.Chorizite.cs:230`). Dispatch `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:706-736`.
- **What is imprecise:** An agent pointing `sourceRoot` at a different Chorizite checkout (e.g. to diff against a newer vendored drop) gets a report computed from the COMPILED vendored assembly + default source tree, while the response's `choriziteSourceRoot` field claims their path was the basis. The response field lies about provenance.
- **What must change:** Either remove the `sourceRoot` parameter from the JSON surface (and report `choriziteSourceRoot` as the real source: assembly identity/version + `DefaultChoriziteSourceRoot` for the regex-parsed parts), or actually honor it by threading it into the ACProtocol regex fallback and returning an error when a non-default `sourceRoot` is supplied (since the reflection side cannot be re-pointed at source).

### F66 — export-envcell-components — MED: minX/minY/maxX/maxY are unvalidated — values >254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and scan wrong landblocks (recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F53 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3516-3519` (raw `GetValue<uint>()`, defaults 0/254, no range check) → `CommandEngine.cs:5384-5386` (`for lbX = minX..maxX { for lbY = minY..maxY { ushort lbKey = LbKey(lbX, lbY); … } }`) with `LbKey` at `CommandEngine.cs:10241` = unchecked `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)`.
- **What is imprecise:** `maxY: 300` makes lbY 256..300 alias into the NEXT lbX row (bit overflow through the `<<8`), re-scanning wrong landblocks and emitting duplicate component records with wrong `landblock` fields; `maxX: 300` similarly wraps past 0xFF. `landblocksProcessed` also counts every grid coordinate visited (including aliased ones), so the response inflates silently. Same unvalidated-coordinate class as F9/F18/F35/F47/F53. (`export-raw-world-facts` and `export-training-data` share the arg pattern — flag when their rows are audited.)
- **What must change:** Validate in the wrapper: each of minX/minY/maxX/maxY must be ≤ 254 and min ≤ max, else throw `ArgumentException("minX/maxX must be 0..254 with minX<=maxX; got …")` (mirrors the `Lb()` helper used by clear-objects). No engine change needed once inputs are clamped.

### F67 — emit-tile-pyramid — MED: blank-tile detection samples only every 32nd pixel — tiles whose only content is a small object glyph can be silently dropped from the pyramid
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TilePyramidEmitter.cs:199-215` (`IsBlankTile`: alpha checked at byte indices `3, 3+128, 3+256, …` = one pixel in every 32 along the linear RGBA span), called from `SliceLbRender` at `:81` for every terrain/objects/object tile.
- **What is imprecise:** The objects-glyph layer is transparent everywhere except glyphs that can be only a few pixels wide at z<11 LB scales. A tile whose only non-transparent content is narrower than the 32-pixel sampling stride in every scanline (e.g. a 4-8 px dot/glyph in an otherwise empty 256×256 tile) is misclassified blank and never written — the object vanishes from the map at that zoom even though it was rendered. The comment's claim ("enough to catch any non-blank quadrant") is wrong for sub-stride content; the failure is silent and data-dependent.
- **What must change:** Do an exact check — scan the full alpha channel (a 256 KB span scan is ~microseconds with `Span`/SIMD, negligible next to PNG encode) or use `SKBitmap.Pixels`-level `IndexOf`-style sweep; delete the stride sampling. If perf genuinely matters, check every pixel of every 4th row plus every pixel of every 4th column (guarantees detection of any ≥4px-tall-or-wide mark) rather than a linear stride.

### F68 — emit-tile-pyramid / emit-static-site — MED: per-LB render failures are swallowed with no log and no diagnostic — missing tiles, success=true
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:12020-12035` (`RenderLbForPyramid`: bare `catch { return null; }`); all four call sites in `EmitTilePyramid` (`:11887-11938`) treat null as "skip layer silently". Contrast the doc-load pre-check at `:11873-11884`, which at least logs the first 10 skips. `emit-static-site` routes through the same code (`StaticSiteEmitter.cs:59-62`) and — despite having a `diagnostics` list that captures describe/overlay/manifest issues — never receives pyramid render failures.
- **What is imprecise:** An LB whose render throws (bad Setup, decode failure, regression) produces a hole in the terrain/objects layers of the emitted map while the response says `success:true` with merely lower tile counts the caller has no baseline for. Same swallow class as F52. Additionally `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1557` ("optional `emitObject` produces a sprite-mode tier at z≥11") is stale — since the "was z=11 floor" change (`CommandEngine.cs:11987-11993`) the sprite tier is downsampled to `minZoom`.
- **What must change:** In `RenderLbForPyramid`, catch into a counter + per-LB stderr line (mirror the `[Emit] Skip LB …` pattern, capped at 10) and thread a `renderFailures` count into `TilePyramidResult` → serialized in the `emit-tile-pyramid` response; have `EmitTilePyramid` optionally accept the StaticSiteEmitter diagnostics sink (or return the failed LB list) so `emit-static-site` adds them to `diagnostics.js` and `diagnosticCount`. Update the api-reference sentence to say the sprite tier covers maxZoom→minZoom via downsample.

### F69 — emit-tile-pyramid — MED: `dirtyOnly:true` silently renders EVERYTHING when the tile pipeline isn't initialized, and a successful emit never clears the dirty set
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:12070-12080` (`ResolveTargetLbs`: `if (dirtyOnly && _tileCache != null)` — when `_tileCache` is null the dirtyOnly request silently falls through to the full document list at `:12081-…`); `_tileCache` is only created by `GetOrCreateTilePipeline()` (tile commands), not by `EmitTilePyramid`; nothing in `EmitTilePyramid` calls `ClearDirty`/`ClearDirtyForLbs` (`TileCache.cs:139-152`) after emitting — the only caller of `ClearDirty` is `MarkTilesClean` (`CommandEngine.cs:1018-1022`).
- **What is imprecise:** The documented fast path ("re-renders after a small `transact` complete in seconds rather than re-rendering every LB", `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1566-1569`) inverts in a fresh stdin session: `{"command":"emit-tile-pyramid","dirtyOnly":true}` with no prior tile-command does a FULL-WORLD render with no indication the filter was ignored. Conversely, when the pipeline IS live, the dirty bits survive the emit, so every later dirtyOnly emit re-renders the same LBs forever unless the agent separately knows to run the (undocumented in this context) mark-clean command.
- **What must change:** In `ResolveTargetLbs`, when `dirtyOnly` is requested and `_tileCache` is null, call `GetOrCreateTilePipeline()` (the manifest persists `DirtyLbs` across sessions) instead of falling through; after a successful non-filtered dirtyOnly emit, call `_tileCache.ClearDirtyForLbs(targetLbs)` + `SaveManifest()`. Surface `dirtyOnlyEffective` (bool) or the resolved source ("dirty-set" vs "all-docs") in the response so the caller can detect the fallback.

### F70 — env-cell-vs-setup-model-chunk — MED: fastMode resume key ignores the fastModeIds list — a second run with different ids of the same count resumes stale results
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MeshParity.cs:408` (`chunkLabel = $"envcell-{startId:X8}-{endId:X8}{(fastMode ? "-fast" : "")}"` — id list not encoded), `:409-431` (progress path = `progress-{chunkLabel}.json`; resume accepted when DAT sha matches and `recordCount == ids.Count`, `TryResumeEnvCellChunk` at `:872-917` checks nothing else). Same pattern in `MeshVsObjExportChunk` (`:231-…`, shares `TryResumeChunk` `:825-867`).
- **What is imprecise:** Two fastMode invocations with the same `startId`/`endId`/`datPath` but DIFFERENT `fastModeIds` lists of equal length hash to the same progress file; the second run short-circuits and returns the FIRST run's pass/fail/drift counts and failure sample, labeled `RESUMED`, for ids it never parsed. Counts and `failures[]` then describe the wrong record set. Edge-case (the shipping validator always uses the Academy list), but the JSON surface accepts arbitrary ids.
- **What must change:** Fold an order-independent digest of the effective id list into the chunk label (e.g. `-fast-{sha256(joined sorted ids)[..12]}`) or persist the id list in progress.json and have `TryResumeEnvCellChunk`/`TryResumeChunk` compare it before resuming. Apply to both chunk commands.

### F71 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — fill — HIGH: world x/y seed coords are unvalidated — negative or beyond-world values wrap through uint/ushort casts and flood-fill the WRONG landblock
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1418-1425` (`CmdFill` reads `x`/`y` via bare `F()` — no range check); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:539-549` (`Fill`: `uint lbX = (uint)Math.Floor(x / 192f)`); wrap site `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:147` (`startLbID = (ushort)((startLbX << 8) | startLbY)`).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring F9/F18/F35/F47 class, here on WORLD coords. Verified on .NET: `x=-500` → `(uint)Math.Floor(-500/192f)` = 4294967293 → `(ushort)` pack lands the seed in LB row 0xFD; `x=60000` (past the 48 960 world edge) → lbX 312 → seed LB 0x38xx. The flood-fill then mutates real terrain in a landblock the caller never named, returns `success:true` with the wrong `landblocks` list, and persists. Negative `localX` also produces a garbage `cellX` before the `Math.Min(...,8)` clamp.
- **What must change:** In `CmdFill` (and/or at the top of `CommandEngine.Fill`), reject `x`/`y` outside `[0, 255*192)` (0..48 959.99) with field-named ArgumentExceptions before computing lbX/lbY — same guard the `Lb()` helper provides for lb-coord args. Belt-and-braces: make `TerrainAlgorithms.FloodFill` throw when `startLbX`/`startLbY` > 254.

### F72 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — export-towns-csv — CRITICAL: Vector3 positions do not survive the worldgen result JSON (System.Text.Json emits `{}` for System.Numerics.Vector3), so every output row's LandblockHex/OutdoorCellHex/TeleLoc is computed from (0,0,0)
- **Where:** Producer: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10434-10455` (`RunWorldGenInternal` serializes `towns = result.Towns` and `plannedBuildings = result.BuildingPlacements` with `JsonOpts.Indented` — no `IncludeFields`, no Vector3/Quaternion converter; `TownSite.WorldCenter`, `PlannedBuilding.WorldPosition`/`Orientation` at `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WorldGen/WorldGeneratorParams.cs:35,47-48`). Consumer: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:106-141` (`ExportTownsCsv` deserializes those properties back); sink: `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WorldGen/TownsExporter.cs:19-61` (`GetTownTelelocAnchor` → `BuildAceTeleLoc`).
- **What is imprecise:** `System.Numerics.Vector3`'s X/Y/Z are public FIELDS; STJ without `IncludeFields=true` serializes the value as `{}` and round-trips it to `(0,0,0)` (empirically verified on this machine: `{"worldPosition":{}}` → `<0, 0, 0>`). So the only JSON this command can consume (`worldgen` with `output:`) carries no positions at all: every town's anchor is (0,0,0), `BuildAceTeleLoc` clamps to lbX=lbY=0 / cell 0x000A, and the CSV's LandblockHex, OutdoorCellHex and TeleLoc columns are identical garbage for every row (Name/Size/Buildings stay correct). `success:true`, `rows:N` — nothing signals the corruption. The codebase already knows STJ can't do this (`CommandEngine.DatParity.cs:623` `Vector3JsonConverter` exists, write-only).
- **What must change:** Fix BOTH ends with one shared `JsonSerializerOptions` (e.g. a `JsonOpts.WorldGenResult` with `IncludeFields = true`, or read/write Vector3+Quaternion converters promoted from DatParity with a `Read` implementation). Use it in `RunWorldGenInternal`'s `outputJsonPath` write and in `ExportTownsCsv`'s deserialize. Then add a guard in `ExportTownsCsv`: if every parsed anchor is exactly (0,0,0) while towns exist, fail with "result JSON carries no positions — re-export with a current build" so legacy result files can't silently produce garbage CSVs. Regression test: worldgen → JSON → export-towns-csv must reproduce the CSV `TownsExporter.Write` emits from the in-memory result.

### F73 — export-towns-csv — MED: help names a producer that cannot produce the input (`generate-world` never writes a result JSON), and the file-not-found error references a CLI flag that does not exist in the JSON protocol
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2439` (help: "from a worldgen result JSON written by generate-world"); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1901` ("produced by `worldgen --output` / `generate-world`"); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:107-108` (`FileNotFoundException("--from-result <path> JSON file not found.")`).
- **What is imprecise:** `CmdGenerateWorld` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2898-2920`) calls `GenerateWorldAsync(p, apply, csvPath)` — it can write a towns CSV directly but never a result JSON. The only producer of the `{towns, plannedBuildings}` shape is the `worldgen` command's `output` arg (`RunWorldGenInternal`). An agent following help/docs runs generate-world, finds no JSON, and dead-ends. The error message's `--from-result` flag doesn't exist anywhere (same class as F45).
- **What must change:** Help + reference: "from a worldgen result JSON written by `worldgen` with `output:<path>`". Reword the exception to `"'fromResult' JSON file not found: <path>"`.

### F74 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — extract-building-pairings — HIGH: cross-bucket pairs are systematically undercounted — the global `j <= i` index filter combined with half-plane bucket offsets drops every pair whose lower-index member sits in the later bucket
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4761-4777` (offsets `dx 0..1`, `dy -1..1`, skipping `(0,-1)` — a half-plane; then `foreach (int i in indices) foreach (int j in nbrs) { if (j <= i) continue; … }`).
- **What is imprecise:** For two structures in DIFFERENT 5 m buckets A and B=(A+(0,1)) with the A-resident having the larger list index, the pair is skipped when scanning A (j<=i) and unreachable when scanning B (offset (0,-1) excluded) — counted zero times instead of once. Since list order comes from the LB scan, roughly half of all cross-bucket adjacencies vanish; `pairCounts` undercounts, edges drop below the `minCount5` threshold, and `pairsKept`/`groupCount`/`building_pairings.json` (which drives group-aware placement) all underreport. Contrast the CORRECT pattern 100 lines up at `:4443-4450` (AnalyzeLandblockPatterns: full 3×3 neighborhood + global `j <= i`).
- **What must change:** Either (a) iterate the full 3×3 neighborhood (`dx -1..1`, `dy -1..1`) and keep the global `j <= i` dedup (matching `:4443`), or (b) keep the half-plane offsets but apply `j <= i` ONLY when the neighbor bucket is the center bucket itself. Re-run extraction afterward; persisted `building_pairings.json` files generated before the fix undercount and should be regenerated.

### F75 ⚠️ **PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-06-11** — export-training-data / extract-retail-heightmaps — HIGH: unconditional Console.WriteLine progress/summary lines go to STDOUT, breaking the documented one-JSON-object-per-line protocol on every invocation
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4862, 4866, 5045, 5049, 5059` (ExportTrainingData — the `:4866` "Found N objects…" line prints on EVERY run, plus per-50-row, per-skip, per-5000 and "Complete" lines) and `:6761, 6771` (ExtractRetailHeightmaps — per-50-row + "Complete"). In stdin JSON mode `Console.Out` IS the protocol stream (`WorldBuilder.Terminal/Program.cs:99` `Console.SetOut(stdoutWriter)`); the contract is "One JSON object per line on **stdout**" (`docs/agent_api_reference.md:96`).
- **What is imprecise:** A line-paired client reads `[ExportTrainingData] Found 31507 objects across …` as the command's response and fails to parse it; even tolerant clients can interleave these lines INTO the middle of nothing-else-buffered output. Other long-runners in the same file already do this correctly via stderr (e.g. `GenerateObjectSprites` `Console.Error.WriteLine` at `:11593+`).
- **What must change:** Change all seven call sites to `Console.Error.WriteLine`. Sweep the rest of `CommandEngine*.cs` for `Console.WriteLine(` reachable from JSON handlers and convert the same way (the two commands here are the only offenders in this batch).

### F76 — export-training-data / export-raw-world-facts — MED: minX/minY/maxX/maxY unvalidated — values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast (recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F53/F66 class); raw-world-facts additionally feeds the UN-wrapped values to its SQL predicates so DAT and ACE scans can cover different regions
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3471-3474` (CmdExportTrainingData) and `:3489-3492` (CmdExportRawWorldFacts) — bare `GetValue<uint>()`, no range check; wrap site `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10241` (`LbKey`), consumed at `:4847` and `:5543`; SQL range predicates at `:5659-5663` (instances/house_portals) and `:5727-5728` (encounters) compare against the raw uints.
- **What is imprecise:** `maxY:300` makes lbY 256..300 alias into the next lbX row through the `<<8` OR — the DAT pass re-scans wrong landblocks and emits records whose `lbKey`/`landblockId` fields are wrong, while `landblocksProcessed` counts every aliased coordinate. In raw-world-facts the MySQL predicates use the unwrapped 300, so the ACE rows span a region the DAT pass didn't — one JSONL file, two silently different extents. This is the exact follow-up F66 flagged ("export-raw-world-facts and export-training-data share the arg pattern").
- **What must change:** In both Cmd wrappers validate all four values ≤ 254 (and min ≤ max) with field-named ArgumentExceptions before calling the engine — same fix shape as F66.

### F77 — fresh-start — MED: help understates the destructive scope — it also deletes every static object from all landblock documents, not just "terrain + dungeon documents"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2437` (help: "Wipes all terrain to deep sea + deletes all dungeon documents"); actual scope `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:300-325` (`ResetWorldDocumentsAsync`: active landblock docs `ClearAllStatics()`, inactive landblock docs DELETED from storage, all dungeon docs deleted) called from `CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:51-53`.
- **What is imprecise:** The confirm gate itself is correct (`confirm:true` required, default false, `success:false` + instructive error otherwise — verified at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2881-2895`), and the terrain wipe (0..254 grid, all 81 verts → type 0x14/height 0, correct `(x<<8)|y` packing) matches the claim. But an agent gating on the help string will destroy all object placements it didn't know were in scope. The response also reports only `landblocksReset`/`verticesReset` — nothing about the landblock/dungeon documents that were cleared/deleted (counts are already computed for the log line at `DocumentManager.cs:321-324`). Note `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1888` describes the full scope correctly — only the `help` metadata lies.
- **What must change:** Help string → "Wipes all terrain to deep sea, clears ALL landblock static objects, and deletes all dungeon documents (requires confirm:true)". Add `landblockDocsCleared` and `dungeonDocsDeleted` to the response (plumb the two counts out of `ResetWorldDocumentsAsync`).

### F78 — extract-retail-heightmaps — MED: exports the CURRENT project terrain document, not retail data — after any terrain edit the "retail_heightmaps" file silently contains modified heights
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6706-6714` (`ExtractRetailHeightmaps` reads `GetTerrainDoc()` — the live, editable terrain document — and writes to default path `pipeline_data/heightmaps/retail_heightmaps.jsonl`); help at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2451` ("Dump all 255×255 landblock heightmaps").
- **What is imprecise:** Command name, default filename and the ML-pipeline framing all promise retail ground truth, but the source is the session's post-edit terrain state. Run it after `fill`/`raise`/`generate-world apply` and the "retail" training baseline is contaminated with synthetic terrain — no field in the response distinguishes the cases.
- **What must change:** Either read from the base cell DAT (bypassing the project terrain overlay) so the output is genuinely retail, or rename/re-describe ("dump current project heightmaps") and add a `source: "project-terrain"` field to the response. If retail-vs-project is meant to be selectable, add a `fromBase?:bool` arg and document it.

### F79 — extract-cell-footprints — MED: with an existing cache and force:false, lbFilter is silently dead — the documented incremental request is a no-op that reports the stale cache's line count as `cellsExtracted`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:11508-11511` (`if (!force && File.Exists(outPath))` → early return `(existingLineCount, 0, 0, path)`), `:11517` (`incremental` path requires the early return NOT to fire, i.e. `force:true`); docs `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1470-1478` show `{"force":false,"lbFilter":["0xA9B4"]}` and state "when provided, the cache is updated **incrementally**" / "Without lbFilter, the full cache is rebuilt".
- **What is imprecise:** Both documented claims are false at force:false with a cache present: the lbFilter request extracts nothing (returns cached count; `dungeonsScanned:0` is the only tell), and the no-filter request rebuilds nothing. The internal merge path the code comments describe (render-dungeon auto-extract) works because that caller controls `force`; the JSON surface mismatches its own docs. Additionally the cache-hit `cellsExtracted` (raw `File.ReadAllLines().Length`) and the incremental `total += keepLines.Count` (`:11538`) both count lines that were NOT extracted this run.
- **What must change:** Make `lbFilter`-present requests take the incremental path regardless of `force` (reserve `force:true` for full rebuild), or document the force requirement in both docs and help. Add `cached: true|false` to the response and only count newly extracted cells in `cellsExtracted` (report preserved lines as `cellsPreserved`).

### F80 — generate-atlas-tiles — MED: mode and lbList are unvalidated — unknown mode returns success:true having done nothing; lbList coords wrap (negative→uint, >254→ushort LbKey) into wrong tiles; a missing lbX/lbY surfaces as a bare NullReferenceException
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4426` (mode default "lbs", never validated — `"mode":"region"`/typo falls through all three branches to `success: errors.Count == 0` = true, generated 0), `:4447-4449` (`(uint)e!["lbX"]!.GetValue<int>()` — null-forgiving deref + unchecked int→uint cast; non-object entries silently dropped by the `Where`), wrap site `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TileGenerator.cs:52` (`CommandEngine.LbKey`), `:4452-4453` (mode=all sweeps `x,y < 256` — 65 536 LBs including the nonexistent 255 row/col, vs 0..254 everywhere else).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) Silent-success no-op on bad mode (same shape as F52's bad-args-success). (b) `{"lbX":300,"lbY":10}` renders and CACHES the tile under aliased key 0x2C0A — recurring wrap class, here corrupting the tile cache keyspace. (c) `{"lbY":5}` (no lbX) errors with "Object reference not set to an instance of an object." — no field name. (d) mode=all generates and stores 511 tiles for LBs that don't exist (RenderPreview accepts 255), inflating `generated` and disk use.
- **What must change:** Validate `mode` against {lbs, regions, world, all} and error otherwise; parse lbList entries with presence checks and 0..254 range validation (index-named ArgumentException, e.g. "lbList[3].lbX must be 0..254"); cap the mode=all sweep at `x,y <= 254`.

### F81 — export-textures — MED: success formula hides partial failure, the errors list is silently truncated at 20, and minId/maxId reject hex strings unlike every other DAT-id argument
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2511-2512` (`new ExportTexturesResult(failed == 0 || exported > 0, …)`; `errors.Take(20)`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2515-2516` (`node["minId"]?.GetValue<uint>()` — a JSON string like "0x06001234" throws an STJ cast InvalidOperationException instead of parsing, while `importTexture`'s `textureId` and the chorizite/DAT commands accept hex via `U()`/`ParseUintField`).
- **What is imprecise:** 500 failed + 1 exported → `success:true`; the caller must notice the `failed` count themselves, and only the first 20 error strings survive with no "(+N more)" marker. The decimal-only `minId`/`maxId` is a precision trap for the one argument whose domain (0x06xxxxxx) is universally written in hex; the resulting error message ("An element of type 'String' cannot be retrieved…") names neither the field nor the rule.
- **What must change:** `success = failed == 0` (the per-id counts already distinguish "nothing matched" from failure); add `errorsTruncated: errors.Count > 20`. Parse `minId`/`maxId` through the same hex-tolerant path as other id args (`ParseUintField`-style: JSON number OR "0x…"/decimal string).

### F82 — export-ontology / export-setup-parts / export-textures / export-training-data / export-raw-world-facts / extract-building-pairings / extract-retail-heightmaps / generate-atlas-tiles — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (grep for any of the eight names: zero hits); all eight ARE advertised in `help` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2377-2465`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same gap class as F11/F23/F34/F43/F56 — the reference is the JSON protocol contract, yet callers must reverse-engineer arg names, defaults (export-textures 0x06000000-0x06FFFFFF; extract-building-pairings minCount5=3 and default output `<project>/building_pairings.json`; export-training-data nearbyLimit=5 and default `training_data.jsonl` in CWD; extract-retail-heightmaps default path; generate-atlas-tiles mode semantics) and response schemas from the wrappers.
- **What must change:** Add a "Data export / ML pipeline" section to `docs/agent_api_reference.md` with one entry per command: args (required/optional + accepted forms), defaults, response schema, side effects (files written, default paths relative to CWD vs project dir). Fold in the corrections from F73/F77/F78 once fixed.

### F83 — extract-cell-footprints — LOW: a cell whose hull collapses below 3 corners gets the synthetic fallback polygon but keeps `synthetic:false`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CellFootprintExtractor.cs:65-68` (`polygon = fp.Corners.Length >= 3 ? fp.Corners : SyntheticPolygonAroundOrigin(cell.Origin)`) vs `:93` (`Synthetic: false` on this path; only the unresolvable-Environment path at `:44-47` sets true).
- **What is imprecise:** The `synthetic` response count and per-line `synthetic` flag undercount: degenerate hulls (collinear vertex sets) emit the same 24u synthetic AABB as the fallback path but are reported as real geometry, so downstream renderers can't distinguish them.
- **What must change:** Set `Synthetic: true` (or a separate `degenerateHull` flag) when the `Corners.Length < 3` substitution fires.

### F84 — export-setup-parts — LOW: per-setup exceptions are swallowed with no failure count — `setupsScanned − setupsExported` conflates parse faults with TryGet type-misses
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2770-2806` (`try { if (!dats.TryGet<Setup>(id, out var setup)) continue; … } catch { /* skip individual failures */ }`).
- **What is imprecise:** A corrupt Setup record silently disappears from the JSONL with `success:true`; the response gives no `failed` count and no sample of the failing ids, so a partially unreadable DAT is indistinguishable from a smaller one.
- **What must change:** Count catches separately, surface `setupsFailed` (+ first few ids/messages) in `ExportSetupPartsResult` and the response; keep the skip behavior otherwise.

### F85 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — generate-dungeon — HIGH: lbX/lbY are unvalidated — values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and generate the dungeon into the WRONG landblock
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3387` (`uint lbX = U(node, "lbX"), lbY = U(node, "lbY");` — uses raw `U()` instead of the range-checked `Lb()` helper at `:3632`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1583` (`ushort lbKey = LbKey(lbX, lbY);`).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F53/F66/F71/F76 class, but here it MUTATES: `lbX:300` wraps `(300<<8)|y` to `0x2C00|y` and writes/stacks a dungeon document on landblock 0x2Cyy with no error; the response's `lbKey` echoes the wrapped key, so the only clue is a hex value that doesn't match the requested coords. `lbY > 255` additionally bleeds into the lbX byte.
- **What must change:** Replace the two `U()` reads with `var (lbX, lbY) = Lb(node);` — identical one-line fix to the one already applied to the terrain getters.

### F86 — generate-dungeon — MED: re-running on a landblock APPENDS to the existing dungeon document — two generations stack, with the second entrance again at the origin
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1618-1620` (`GetDungeonDoc(lbKey)` → `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync`, returns the existing doc with all prior cells) and `:1645-1650` (`dungeonDoc.AddCell(... Vector3.Zero, Quaternion.Identity ...)` — entrance always placed at the dungeon origin).
- **What is imprecise:** The natural agent workflow — iterate on seed/depth/theme for one LB — silently accumulates overlapping dungeons: the second run's entrance cell occupies the same origin as the first run's, cell numbers continue from the old count, and `cellsPlaced` reports only the new cells so the response looks like a clean regeneration. Nothing in help, docs, or the response says "appends".
- **What must change:** Either clear the document's cells before generation (matching what "generate a dungeon at lbX/lbY" means), or refuse a non-empty dungeon doc unless `replace:true` is passed, and surface `existingCells` in the response.

### F87 — generate-dungeon — MED: a child skipped for lack of parent portals (or template) drops its ENTIRE subtree silently — and the code comment claims the opposite
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1672` (`if (!visited.Add(childNodeId)) continue;` — child is marked visited BEFORE any placement attempt) and `:1701-1705` (no-portal path warns for the child, with the comment "Still enqueue for BFS so we can process its children if it gets placed elsewhere" — but the code `continue`s without enqueueing, and the visited mark guarantees it is never retried).
- **What is imprecise:** Only the immediate skipped child gets a warning; every descendant of that node is dropped with no warning at all. `nodesInGraph` vs `cellsPlaced` is the only (uncounted) signal. The same silent-subtree-drop applies to the "No suitable template" and Environment/CellStruct-load failure paths (`:1690-1727`).
- **What must change:** When a child cannot be placed, warn with the size of the orphaned subtree (BFS count of its descendants) — or defer it to a retry queue keyed off other placed cells as the comment promises; delete or correct the comment either way.

### F88 — generate-object-sprites — MED: the force:false cache short-circuit fabricates the response — claims modelsCollected/modelsRendered = old manifest line count, modelsFailed:0, and ignores spritePx/lodLevel-mismatched requests
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:11580-11586` (`if (!force && File.Exists(manifestPath) && File.Exists(atlasPath)) { int existing = File.ReadAllLines(manifestPath).Length; ... return new ObjectSpritesResult(existing, existing, 0, ...); }`).
- **What is imprecise:** Nothing was collected or rendered on this run, yet the response is shaped exactly like a render run (`modelsRendered:N, modelsFailed:0`) with no field indicating a cache hit. Worse, the cache key is only (lodLevel, nightMode) via the filename suffix: calling again with a different `spritePx` or a different `lbFilter` returns the stale atlas built from other parameters, silently. A corrupt atlas decodes to `probe == null` and reports `atlasWidth:0, atlasHeight:0` with success:true.
- **What must change:** Add `cached: true` (and the cached file's mtime) to the short-circuit response, or zero out modelsRendered and rename the count field; treat a `spritePx`/`lbFilter` differing from the cached run (persist them in a sidecar or manifest header) as a cache miss; treat `probe == null` as an error.

### F89 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — generate-settlement — HIGH: unconditional Console.WriteLine progress lines go to STDOUT on every invocation, breaking the one-JSON-object-per-line --stdin protocol
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6338-6339, 6436, 6463, 6552, 6679` (`Console.WriteLine($"[GenerateSettlement] ...")` ×6).
- **What is imprecise:** Same defect class as F75 (export-training-data / extract-retail-heightmaps), new affected path: every generate-settlement call interleaves 4-6 non-JSON lines with the JSON response stream, so a strict line-parsing agent driver fails or mis-pairs responses. All other generators in this batch correctly use `Console.Error` (e.g. GenerateDungeon `:1601`, GenerateObjectSprites throughout).
- **What must change:** Switch all six call sites to `Console.Error.WriteLine` (per the F75 fix prescription).

### F90 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — generate-settlement — HIGH: centerX/centerY beyond the world edge wrap through `(uint)(px/192f)` → ushort LbKey and write objects into the WRONG landblock — ValidateLbLocalCoord passes because it checks the UN-wrapped range
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6344-6346` (center lb), `:6627-6628` (`uint objLbX = (uint)(px / 192f);` per object), `:1312` (AddObject → `ValidateLbLocalCoord(lbX, lbY, x, y, ...)`), `:10359-10367` (validator computes `lbMinX = lbX * 192` from the raw uint — for lbX=260 the range is [49920, 50112) so a world-x of 50000 passes), `:1313` (`LbKey(lbX, lbY)` then wraps 260 → 0x04).
- **What is imprecise:** `centerX:50000` (world edge is 255×192 = 48960) silently places the whole settlement into landblock column 4 with `Origin.X ≈ 50000` — coordinates that are outside any landblock-local or world-consistent range — and reports success with plausible placedObjects. Recurring wrap class (F18/F35/F71), reachable here through float world coords rather than lb ints. (Negative centers are safe: float→uint saturates to 0 and ValidateLbLocalCoord then throws per object.)
- **What must change:** In GenerateSettlement, validate `0 <= centerX/centerY < 48960` up front (error response otherwise) and skip per-object positions whose `(uint)(p/192f) > 254` with a warning; additionally make `ValidateLbLocalCoord` reject `lbX/lbY > 254` so the engine-level AddObject can never wrap regardless of caller.

### F91 — generate-settlement — MED: the biome-inference table mislabels the Region terrain types, so forbidden/allowed-biome constraints evaluate against the wrong biomes
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6361-6371` (comment "0=Road, 1=Grass, 2=Rock, 3=Dirt/Sand, 4=Desert, 5=Snow, 6=Ice" and the switch mapping 0/1→temperate, 2→rock, 3/4→desert, 5|6→snow).
- **What is imprecise:** Region terrain types are the alphabetical retail table established in F14: 0=BarrenRock, 1=Grassland, 2=Ice, 3=LushGrass, 4=MarshSparseSwamp, 5=MudRichDirt, 6=ObsidianPlain, 8=PatchyDirt… So a settlement centered on Ice (2) is inferred "rock", LushGrass (3) is inferred "desert", and MudRichDirt (5) is inferred "snow"; `constraintViolations` and the biome warnings are then wrong in both directions. Road is not a terrain type at all (it is the separate road field). 
- **What must change:** Map indices through the actual Region table (`GetRegion()`-style `TerrainInfo.TerrainTypes[i].TerrainName`) and infer biome from the name; fix the comment.

### F92 — generate-world / generate-settlement — MED: seed 0 (the default) silently uses an unseeded RNG, yet the response echoes seed:0 as if the run were reproducible
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WorldGen/WorldGenerator.cs:17` (`var rng = p.Seed != 0 ? new Random(p.Seed) : new Random();`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6341` (identical pattern in GenerateSettlement); response echoes at `CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:96` (`Seed: p.Seed`) while generate-settlement returns no seed field at all.
- **What is imprecise:** generate-world's response `seed:0` cannot reproduce the world that was just generated/applied, and generate-settlement gives the caller nothing to replay. Contrast generate-dungeon, which maps 0 → `Environment.TickCount` and reports the EFFECTIVE seed (`DungeonGrammar.cs:119`, response `seed = graph.Seed`). Neither help nor docs mention the 0-means-random convention. The `worldgen` command shares the WorldGenerator path (note for its own audit row; not double-filed here).
- **What must change:** Derive an effective seed when 0 (e.g. `Environment.TickCount`), thread it into the RNG, and return it in both commands' responses (add a `seed` field to GenerateSettlementResult).

### F93 — generate-world — MED: `fullWorld:true` generates only landblocks 0..253 — the world's last row and column (lb x=254 and y=254, 509 landblocks) are silently never touched
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WorldGen/WorldGenerator.cs:21-22` (`int regionW = p.FullWorld ? 254 : p.Width;` ditto regionH) feeding `PackResult` whose loop runs `lbLocalX < p.Width` from StartX=0, i.e. global lb 0..253.
- **What is imprecise:** Everywhere else the world grid is 0..254 inclusive (Lb() validation, fresh-start's 255-loop, generate-terrain's `lbX <= 254`, `info`'s 255*255). A "full world" generate-world leaves a one-landblock L-shaped strip of old terrain/water along the east and north edges; `landblocksAffected` (max 64516 vs 65025) is the only clue. Affects `worldgen --fullWorld` identically (shared code).
- **What must change:** `p.FullWorld ? 255 : p.Width/Height` (vertex arrays become 2041² which the existing `vx >= verticesX` guards already handle at the seam).

### F94 — generate-world — MED: DocumentManager.SkipDatStatics is set true for the apply phase with no try/finally — an apply failure leaves it stuck on for the rest of the session
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:79-83` (`SkipDatStatics = true; await ResetWorldDocumentsAsync(); ApplyWorldGenResult(result); SkipDatStatics = false;`); same pattern in `FreshStartAsync` at `:50-52`.
- **What is imprecise:** If `ResetWorldDocumentsAsync` or `ApplyWorldGenResult` throws (e.g. a landblock doc fails to load mid-apply), the exception propagates to the dispatcher's catch — a clean error JSON — but `SkipDatStatics` remains true, so EVERY subsequent landblock-document load in the session silently skips DAT statics: list-objects, sprites, validators and exports then under-report retail objects with no error anywhere.
- **What must change:** Wrap the apply phase in `try { ... } finally { project.DocumentManager.SkipDatStatics = false; }` in both GenerateWorldAsync and FreshStartAsync.

### F95 — get-bulk-heightmap — MED: minX/minY/maxX/maxY are unvalidated — values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast into the WRONG landblocks, and minX>maxX silently returns success with zero results
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2256-2259` (raw `U()` reads, no range check); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2227-2244` (loop `for x = minX..maxX` → `GetHeightmap(x,y)` → `LbKey` wrap at `:10241`).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F53/F66/F76 class. `maxX:260` makes the loop fetch x=255..260, which wrap to lb columns 255→0xFF00|y (out of world) and 256..260→columns 0..4 — wrong data reported as if it were the requested coords; the per-entry response even self-contradicts (`lbX:256` alongside `landblock:"0x00xx"`, since HeightmapResult stores the raw uint while the key wrapped). An inverted rect (minX>maxX) yields `totalLandblocks:0, foundLandblocks:0, success:true` with no hint the request was malformed.
- **What must change:** Validate all four fields ≤ 254 (reuse the Lb() error wording) and `minX<=maxX && minY<=maxY` (error response otherwise) before looping.

### F96 — get-dungeon-info — MED: the read-only query permanently CREATES and persists an empty dungeon document for any landblock that lacks one
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1490-1497` → `GetDungeonDoc` (`:10327-10332`, `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync<DungeonDocument>`) → `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:146-151` (`isNewDoc` → `DocumentStorageService.CreateDocumentAsync(...)` writes the new doc to project storage).
- **What is imprecise:** Querying `get-dungeon-info` on a plain surface LB returns `hasDungeon:false` but leaves a persisted `dungeon_XXXX` document behind — violating the read-only contract the codebase itself documents at `TryGetLandblockDoc` (`CommandEngine.cs:10256` "so query/mutate ops don't lazy-create phantom docs"). The phantom docs then inflate `info`'s activeDocuments and are enumerated by every `ListDungeonDocIds`-driven sweep (generate-object-sprites, extract-cell-footprints, emit-static-site), each iteration loading them for nothing. A bulk LB scan via get-dungeon-info salts the project with thousands of empty docs.
- **What must change:** Add a TryGet-style dungeon-doc probe (check `ActiveDocs` + `DocumentStorageService.GetDocumentAsync` + DAT dungeon-cell presence, mirroring TryGetLandblockDoc) and use it in GetDungeonInfo; only fall through to GetOrCreate for mutating callers (generate-dungeon, snap-portal).

### F97 — get-height — MED: an in-world position over a landblock with no terrain data returns success:true with height 0.00 — "no data" masquerades as sea level
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:615-639` (GetHeight: `hl(x,y)` first, vertex metadata second); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:392-393` (`var data = terrainLookup(lbId); if (data == null) return 0f;`); wrapper `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1440-1447` (`success = r.LandblockId.HasValue` — true whenever the coords are in world range, regardless of data).
- **What is imprecise:** When `GetLandblockInternal` has no entry for the LB, the response is `{success:true, height:0.00, landblock:"0xXXXX", vertexIndex:N}` with heightIndex/terrainType/road/scenery silently omitted (WhenWritingNull) — indistinguishable from a real 0-height vertex except by noticing missing optional fields. Same masquerade class as F48. The docs (`agent_api_reference.md:504+`) document no such case.
- **What must change:** Make GetHeight surface a `found`/data-present flag (data != null) and have the wrapper set `success=false` (or `found:false` with `height:null`) when the landblock has no terrain data.

### F98 — get-region — MED: a missing/corrupt Region record silently fabricates a linear i*2 height table and swallows the terrain-types exception — the response is indistinguishable from real region data
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10254-10262` (`GetHeightTable`: `TryGet<Region>` miss → `_heightTableCache[i] = i * 2f` synthetic table, no flag); `:2393-2410` (GetRegion: `try { ... } catch { }` around the terrain-type read — any parse exception silently yields `terrainTypes:null`, which `WhenWritingNull` then omits from the JSON entirely).
- **What is imprecise:** With a non-standard portal/cell DAT the command reports `success:true, heightTable:[0,2,4,…], heightTableSize:256` — fabricated values presented as "the game's Region data" (docs' words), and the terrainTypes/terrainTypeCount fields simply vanish rather than erroring. Every consumer that follows the documented usage (`heightTable[heightIndex]`) silently computes wrong world heights; get-heightmap/get-terrain-data/get-height inherit the same fabricated table through the shared cache.
- **What must change:** GetRegion should return success:false with an explicit error when Region 0x13000000 is absent; GetHeightTable's synthetic fallback should at minimum be surfaced (e.g. `heightTableSource: "region"|"synthetic-fallback"` in get-region) and logged to stderr; drop the bare `catch { }` in favor of reporting the parse failure.

### F99 — get-object-detail — MED: Setup bounds ignore placement-frame ROTATION (origin-only offset from an arbitrary first placement frame), so boundsMin/Max/Size and maxDimension are wrong for rotated-part setups
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2277-2293` (`partOffset = dp.Frames[i].Origin` — the Frame's Orientation quaternion is never applied to the part vertices; `dp = setup.PlacementFrames.Values.FirstOrDefault()` picks whichever placement happens to enumerate first rather than the default placement id).
- **What is imprecise:** Any Setup whose parts are placed with non-identity orientation (doors, levers, multi-part furniture, most creatures) gets an AABB computed from un-rotated part meshes translated to the frame origin — the reported `maxDimension` feeds ontology scale/`aspectRatio` consumers with values that can differ by the part's full length. A 4m beam rotated 90° contributes 4m to the wrong axis.
- **What must change:** Transform each vertex by the part's full frame (`Vector3.Transform(v.Origin, frame.Orientation) + frame.Origin`) and select the placement frame deterministically (key 0 / the default placement id, falling back to the first sorted key).

### F100 — get-object-detail — LOW: surfaceIds are decimal strings while every other DAT id in the protocol is 0x-hex
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2280` and `:2312` (`surfaceIds.Add(sid.ToString())` on a uint Surface DID) → response `surfaceIds:["134218399", ...]` vs `objectId:"0x02000A23"` in the same payload.
- **What is imprecise:** Surface ids live in the 0x08xxxxxx range universally written in hex (export-textures, chorizite commands, docs); emitting `134218399` forces every consumer to special-case a base-10 conversion for exactly one field.
- **What must change:** Emit `$"0x{sid:X8}"` (response-shape change; flag in release notes since the field already ships).

### F101 — generate-terrain — LOW: malformed coastline points are silently dropped — a coastline that decays below 3 valid pairs silently disables the mask
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3348-3357` (only `JsonArray` items with `Count >= 2` are collected; strings/objects/short arrays are skipped without error; the `coastArr.Count >= 3` gate runs BEFORE filtering) and `CommandEngine.cs:3828-3830` (engine re-checks `Count >= 3` and silently sets `coastline = null`).
- **What is imprecise:** `"coastline":[[0,0],[100,0],"oops"]` generates a whole-world terrain with NO coastline applied and `hasCoastline:false` as the only signal — easy to miss in a response dominated by 65025 landblocksWritten. Given the command overwrites the entire world, a silently-ignored mask is expensive.
- **What must change:** Error on any coastline entry that isn't a 2+-element numeric array, and error (not ignore) when fewer than 3 valid points remain.

### F102 — generate-dungeon / generate-settlement / generate-terrain / get-bulk-heightmap / get-object-detail — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (generate-dungeon appears only inside the transact allow-list sentence at `:1213`; the other four have zero hits); all five ARE advertised in `help` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2373-2374, 2441-2442, 2450`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same gap class as F11/F23/F34/F44/F56/F82. Callers must reverse-engineer: generate-dungeon's defaults (depth 8, branching 2.0, minRooms 5, maxRooms 40, theme "default", validate true — `validate` is absent even from help args); generate-terrain's `autoPaint` (default true, also absent from help args) and the fact that `amplitude` is a HEIGHT-INDEX amplitude (0–255 byte), not world units; generate-settlement's template list and response schema; get-bulk-heightmap's rect semantics; get-object-detail's response fields.
- **What must change:** Add reference entries for all five with args (required/optional/defaults), response schemas, and side effects (generate-terrain overwrites all 65025 landblocks; generate-dungeon appends per F86); add `validate` and `autoPaint` to the help args strings.

### F103 — generate-world — LOW: a towns CSV with zero rows is written to disk (header-only) but the response reports townsCsvPath:null
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WorldGen.cs:87-92` (`csvRows = TownsExporter.Write(...)` always creates the file via `File.Create`) and `:103` (`TownsCsvPath: csvRows > 0 ? exportTownsCsvPath : null`).
- **What is imprecise:** With `townCount:0` (or no land for towns) the command creates `exportTownsCsv` containing only the header line, yet the response denies the file exists (`townsCsvPath:null`, and `WhenWritingNull` removes the field entirely) — a caller checking the response then leaves an unexpected file behind, or a caller checking the disk sees a "successful" export the response disowned.
