- **What must change:** Always echo the path that was written (`TownsCsvPath: exportTownsCsvPath`) and let `townsCsvRows:0` carry the emptiness — or skip writing entirely when there are no towns.

### F104 — generate-settlement — LOW: the steep-terrain check uses the flat 2-units-per-height-index approximation while the real height table is loaded in the same scope
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:6665` (`float slopeDeg = MathF.Atan2(maxDelta * 2f, 24f)` — `maxDelta` is a raw height-INDEX delta scaled by a hardcoded 2.0) — the GetTerrainHelpers() height table is available at `:6565`.
- **What is imprecise:** Same approximation class as F10. The retail LandHeightTable is non-linear in its upper range, so the ">25° slope" constraint warnings (which feed `constraintViolations` in the response) are mis-scaled wherever index spacing deviates from 2.0 world units.
- **What must change:** Compute the delta as `ht[neighbor] - ht[center]` using the loaded height table instead of `maxDelta * 2f`.

### F105 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — import-texture — HIGH: permanently mutates the project's BASE portal DAT in place, with no staging/undo, and never refreshes the project's stale read handles
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2514-2527` (`ImportTexture`: `var datDir = _projectManager.CurrentProject!.BaseDatDirectory; using var writer = new DatEasyWriter(datDir); writer.UpdateRenderSurface(...)`). `DatEasyWriter(string)` opens the directory `DatAccessType.ReadWrite` (`DatReaderWriter.Extensions/DatEasyWriter.cs:30`) and `Save` writes through a `MemoryMappedBlockAllocator` immediately. `BaseDatDirectory` is the project's pristine base layer (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Models/Project.cs:31`), which `ExportDats` copies verbatim as the export baseline (`Project.cs:194`) and which the project itself holds open `DatAccessType.Read` (`Project.cs:98`).
- **What is imprecise:** Unlike its sibling `import-render-surface` (deferred via CustomTextureStore/PortalDatDocument until `export`), `import-texture` writes the base `client_portal.dat` immediately and irreversibly: there is no undo, the "base" layer every diff/export treats as immutable is silently contaminated, and the help text ("Replace a texture from image file") gives no hint of the different persistence model. Worse, the project keeps its memory-mapped Read handles on the same files for the rest of the session and `ImportTexture` never calls `Project.ReloadDatReadersAfterExternalWrite()` (`Project.cs:110`, written precisely for "a short-lived ReadWrite connection ... wrote new portal files" — currently zero callers), so subsequent reads (`export-textures`, ontology scans, render paths) may see stale or torn data for the updated surface.
- **What must change:** Either (a) reroute `import-texture` through the same deferred path as `import-render-surface` (CustomTextureStore replacement, written at export), or (b) keep the in-place write but call `_projectManager.CurrentProject.ReloadDatReadersAfterExternalWrite()` after a successful `UpdateRenderSurface`, change the help description to state the write is immediate and permanent to the project's base DATs, and add `mode:"in-place"` (vs `deferred`) to the response so agents can distinguish the two texture commands.

### F106 — import-texture — MED: textureId rejects "0x06001234" hex strings while the sibling command accepts them (F81 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2524` (`uint textureId = U(node, "textureId")` — `U` at `:3597` only does `GetValue<uint>()`, throwing `InvalidOperationException` on any JSON string). Contrast `import-render-surface` at `:3249`, which uses `ParseUintField` (`:3265-3275`) accepting both number and 0x-hex string.
- **What is imprecise:** DAT ids are presented as 0x-hex everywhere in this protocol (the command's own response echoes `textureId = "0x{...:X8}"`), yet passing the id back in that form fails with an opaque type error. Same inconsistency class as F81 (export-textures minId/maxId).
- **What must change:** Parse `textureId` with `ParseUintField` (or `Hex32`'s string branch) so 0x-hex strings and numbers are both accepted.

### F107 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — ingest-spawn-maps / ingest-spells / ingest-recipes — HIGH: unconditional Console.WriteLine progress/summary lines go to STDOUT, breaking the one-JSON-object-per-line --stdin protocol (F75/F89 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3545,3603,3606-3607` (`IngestSpawnMaps`: "[IngestSpawnMaps] Found …", per-200-file progress, completion + output lines), `:3632,3653,3693,3696-3698` (`IngestSpells`), `:3726,3797,3800-3805` (`IngestRecipes`). Responses are emitted on the same stdout (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:80`).
- **What is imprecise:** Every successful invocation interleaves multiple non-JSON lines with the JSON response stream — same defect class as F75/F89; the spawn-maps run alone can emit dozens of progress lines for the LSD dataset.
- **What must change:** Route all `[IngestSpawnMaps]`/`[IngestSpells]`/`[IngestRecipes]` lines to `Console.Error` (the pattern `OntologyService.ImportCatalog` already uses) or behind the logger.

### F108 — ingest-spawn-maps / ingest-spells / ingest-recipes — MED: per-file/per-entry parse failures are silently swallowed — the response reports success:true with no error count
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3598-3600` (`catch { errors++; }` per spawn-map file), `:3688-3690` (per spell entry), `:3792-3794` (per recipe file). The `errors` counter is printed only to the (stdout, F107) log; `IngestSpawnMapsResult`/`IngestSpellsResult`/`IngestRecipesResult` carry no failure field and the wrappers (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2743-2746, 2754-2758, 2822-2827`) serialize none.
- **What is imprecise:** A dataset where half the files are malformed returns `success:true` with merely lower totals the caller has no baseline for — the only machine-readable signal of data loss is absent. Same swallow class as F84/F68.
- **What must change:** Add an `Errors`/`Failed` int to all three result records, populate it from the existing counters, serialize it as `errors` (or `failedFiles`) in each response, and (optionally) set `success:false` when processed==0 && errors>0.

### F109 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — import-render-surface — HIGH: success path prints "[TextureImport] …" to STDOUT on every invocation (F75/F89 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Texture/RenderSurfaceImporter.cs:139` (`TryImportRenderSurfaceReplacement`, default mode) and `:194` (`TryOverwriteUiRenderSurface`, ui mode) — both `Console.WriteLine`, both reached on every successful import via `CommandEngine.Texture.cs:35/41`.
- **What is imprecise:** One non-JSON line pollutes the stdin-protocol stream per successful import, in both modes. (The same helper's export-time writers at `:227-247` etc. also write stdout, but those fire during `export`, already covered by the F75 class.)
- **What must change:** Switch the two import-path `Console.WriteLine` calls to `Console.Error.WriteLine` (the class is shared with the Avalonia GUI, where stderr is equally visible).

### F110 — import-catalog — LOW: a mid-file parse failure leaves the live ontology partially enriched while the response claims entriesEnriched:0
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3037-3042` (whole-run try/catch returning `ImportCatalogResult(false, 0, ...)`); the loop in `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/OntologyService.cs:693-752` mutates `_entries` in place as it goes (e.g. `entry.VertexCount = vcEl.GetInt32()` at `:715` throws on a non-numeric value AFTER earlier entries were already enriched).
- **What is imprecise:** After a malformed entry midway through index.json, the in-memory ontology contains an unknown number of merged thumbnails/bounds, but the response says `success:false, entriesEnriched:0` — the session state and the report disagree, and a retry double-merges.
- **What must change:** Catch per-element inside the `ImportCatalog` loop (count failures, continue), return the true enriched count plus a failure count, and surface both in the JSON response.

### F111 — get-terrain-layers / get-tile — MED: lbX/lbY are unvalidated — values > 254 wrap through the ushort LbKey cast (F9/F18/F35/F47/F53/F66/F76/F95 class); a wrapped get-tile can serve the WRONG cached tile as success
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2497` (`CmdGetTerrainLayers`: raw `U(node,...)` → `CommandEngine.cs:2418` `LbKey(lbX, lbY)` = `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)` at `:10241`) and `:4352` (`CmdGetTile` zoom=lb → `TileGenerator.cs:52` same wrap). Both bypass the existing `Lb()` validator (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3632-3637`).
- **What is imprecise:** `lbX:300, lbY:5` silently becomes LB 0x2C05 — get-terrain-layers reports another landblock's distribution under `landblock:"0x2C05"`. get-tile is worse: on a cache hit for the wrapped key it returns that other LB's tile with `success:true`; on a miss the un-wrapped coords flow into `RenderPreview`, whose own `> 255` guard (`CommandEngine.cs:715`) only catches part of the range (255 itself passes despite the world being 0..254).
- **What must change:** Use `Lb(node)` in `CmdGetTerrainLayers` and in the zoom=lb branch of `CmdGetTile`.

### F112 — get-tile / import-heightmap — MED: terrain edits outside `transact` never invalidate the tile cache — get-tile keeps serving pre-edit tiles as success
- **Where:** invalidation exists only in `CommandEngine.OnTransactCommitted` (`CommandEngine.cs:1040-1058`), called solely from the transact path. Direct mutators — `import-heightmap apply:true` (`CommandEngine.Texture.cs:109` → `TerrainDocument.ApplyBulkImport`), and equally `raise`/`lower`/`set-landblock-heightmap`/`generate-terrain` — never call `MarkLbDirty`/`MarkAllLbTilesDirty`. `TileCache.cs:16-17` documents only the transact hook.
- **What is imprecise:** After `{"command":"import-heightmap",...,"apply":true}` modifies thousands of vertices, `{"command":"get-tile","zoom":"lb",...}` returns the stale cached JPEG with `success:true` and a pre-edit `generatedAt` — there is no dirty bit, so `list-dirty-tiles` is empty and `regenerate-dirty-tiles` is a no-op. The tile layer silently diverges from the document layer for every non-transact editing session.
- **What must change:** Have the direct edit paths report their `ModifiedLandblocks` to the tile cache: e.g. in `CommandEngine`, after any `ApplyTerrainEdit`/`ApplyBulkImport`-based command succeeds and `_tileCache != null`, call `MarkLbDirty` per modified LB (plus `MarkRegionDirty` via `ResolveRegionForLb`, and world), mirroring `OnTransactCommitted`. At minimum for this batch: wire `ImportHeightmap`'s returned `ModifiedLandblocks` through.

### F113 — help — MED: args metadata marks optional fields required — `import-heightmap` startLbX/startLbY default to 0 and `get-tile` zoom defaults to "lb"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2410` (help: `"imagePath, startLbX, startLbY, lbCountX, lbCountY, apply?"` vs parser `:3280-3281` `?? 0` defaults) and `:2459` (help: `"zoom, lbX?, lbY?, region?, includeBase64?"` vs parser `:4348` `?? "lb"`).
- **What is imprecise:** The `?` suffix is the protocol's only required/optional signal; both entries claim required-ness the parsers don't enforce. Inverse direction of F77's lie but the same metadata-precision defect.
- **What must change:** Change to `startLbX?, startLbY?` (or make them required in the parser — safer for a terrain-writing command) and `zoom?`.

### F114 — get-world-info — LOW: heightMin/heightMax are the height table's first/last entries, not its min/max
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2026-2027` (`ht[0]` / `ht[ht.Length - 1]` returned as `HeightMin`/`HeightMax`, serialized as `heightMin`/`heightMax` at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2161`).
- **What is imprecise:** Correct only while the region LandHeightTable is monotonically non-decreasing. A custom region (region-import-json can stage one) with a non-monotonic table makes both fields lie under names that promise extrema.
- **What must change:** Use `ht.Min()`/`ht.Max()` (or rename the fields `heightTableFirst`/`heightTableLast`).

### F115 — get-tile — LOW: zoom value matching is case-sensitive while every command name is case-insensitive
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4351-4358` (`zoom == "lb"` etc., ordinal case-sensitive) vs the handler dictionary's `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`:152`).
- **What is imprecise:** `"zoom":"LB"` or `"World"` returns the unknown-zoom error although the error text implies a value match.
- **What must change:** Compare with `StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase`.

### F116 — get-terrain-layers / get-tile / import-texture / import-catalog / ingest-spawn-maps / ingest-spells / ingest-recipes — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md; the documented `help` example pins a stale protocol version
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` (zero occurrences of any of the seven names; verified by search). All seven are advertised in `help` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2376, 2378, 2386, 2398-2400, 2459`). Additionally the doc's help response example at `:1653` shows `"version": "1.2"` while the code returns `"1.5"` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2488`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same doc-absence class as F82/F102 — request/response contracts (notably get-tile's three zoom modes and import-texture's persistence model) exist nowhere outside source. The stale version string also tells agents to expect an older protocol revision.
- **What must change:** Add reference sections for the seven commands (mirroring the F82 remediation format) and update the help example to the current version string (or replace it with a placeholder like `"<current>"`).

### F117 — import-render-surface — MED: the API reference claims "the source image must match the target RenderSurface dimensions" — the code auto-resizes instead
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1823` ("Both modes require the source image to match the target RenderSurface dimensions and `PFID_A8R8G8B8` format.") vs `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Texture/RenderSurfaceImporter.cs:28-32` (`LoadImageAsBgra` resizes any mismatched image to the target W×H) used by the ui mode at `:180` and by the deferred export write at `:240` (which logs "Resizing …" and proceeds).
- **What is imprecise:** Only the format half of the sentence is enforced (`:123-126`, `:174-177`). The dimensions half is wrong in both modes — a mismatched image succeeds, silently rescaled; an agent pre-validating dimensions per the doc does needless work, and one relying on a rejection to catch a wrong-asset mistake gets none.
- **What must change:** Correct the doc to "the image is resampled to the target RenderSurface's dimensions; only `PFID_A8R8G8B8` surfaces can be replaced" — or, if strict matching is intended, add the dimension check before `LoadImageAsBgra` in both Try* methods.

### F118 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — load — HIGH: the tile pipeline (TileCache + TileGenerator) is never reset when a different project is loaded — tiles, dirty set, and region anchors from the previous project keep being served and written
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:123-125` (`Load` → `InvalidateCaches()`), `:10236-10239` (`InvalidateCaches` clears only `_terrainDocCache` + `_heightTableCache`), `:961-975` (`GetOrCreateTilePipeline` returns the cached `_tileCache`/`_tileGenerator` forever once created; `TileCache` is rooted at `p.ProjectDirectory/atlas_tiles` at construction, `TileCache.cs:34`; region anchors are baked in via `SetRegionAnchors` only at creation, `:973`).
- **What is imprecise:** Session: `load A` → `get-tile`/`list-dirty-tiles` (pipeline created for A) → `load B`. Every subsequent tile command still uses A's `atlas_tiles/` directory and `manifest.json`: `get-tile` serves A's cached JPEGs as B's landblocks (or renders B's data and writes it into A's cache dir), `list-dirty-tiles` reports A's dirty set, `prune-tiles`/`tile-stats` operate on A's disk, and region tiles composite with A's `region_gazetteer` anchors even though `AutoRestoreRegions` (`:250-266`) just replaced `_regionAnchors` with B's. Wrong results presented as success.
- **What must change:** In `CommandEngine.InvalidateCaches()` (or directly in `Load` before `InvalidateCaches`), also set `_tileCache = null; _tileGenerator = null;` so the next tile command lazily rebuilds the pipeline against the new project's directory and region anchors.

### F119 — load / load-building-pairings — MED: `BuildingPairings.LoadFromJsonFile` swallows every parse error — a corrupt building_pairings.json reports success:true / loaded:true with edgeCount 0, and the auto-loader's catch block is dead code
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Pairings/BuildingPairings.cs:98-111` (missing file → empty instance; `catch { }` at `:107-109` → empty instance; the comment says "caller can warn via a higher layer" but no caller can — nothing is thrown or returned); consumers `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4806-4814` (`LoadBuildingPairings`: `success:true, error:null` whenever the file exists) and `:161-174` (`AutoRestorePairings`: the `catch (Exception)` at `:170` is unreachable, so the `load` autoRestore report shows `loaded:true, count:0, error:null` for a malformed file).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"load-building-pairings","path":"corrupt.json"}` → `{"success":true,"edgeCount":0,"groupCount":0,"error":null}` — indistinguishable from a legitimately empty pairings file. Same lie inside `load`'s `autoRestore.pairings` entry. `doc?.Pairs == null` (wrong shape) is equally silent.
- **What must change:** Make `LoadFromJsonFile` propagate failure — either let `JsonException` escape (preferred: both call sites already have catch/error plumbing that would then work) or return a `(BuildingPairings, string? error)` pair. A null/absent `Pairs` array should also be reported, not treated as success-empty.

### F120 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — ingest-weenies — HIGH: progress and summary lines print to STDOUT on every invocation, corrupting the --stdin JSON channel (F75/F89/F107/F109 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3128` (`[IngestWeenies] Found {total} weenie files…`), `:3220-3221` (per-1000 progress), `:3224-3227` (4 summary lines) — all `Console.WriteLine`, while stdin mode reserves stdout for one-JSON-per-line responses (`Program.cs:73`, `JsonCommandProcessor` responses).
- **What is imprecise:** A 28k-file LSD ingest emits ~33 non-JSON stdout lines before the response line; strict JSON readers break or have to skip garbage.
- **What must change:** Route all five sites to `Console.Error.WriteLine` (the convention the ontology loader already follows, e.g. `OntologyService.cs:1743`) or to the injected logger.

### F121 — ingest-weenies — MED: per-file parse failures are silently swallowed — the response reports success:true with no error count, and a failed run leaves a partial output file (F108 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3215-3218` (bare `catch { errors++; }`), `:3229` (success return carries `processed` but not `errors`), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandResults.cs:531-534` (`IngestWeeniesResult` has no error/skipped count field), `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2643-2647` (response mirrors the record). Secondary: on a mid-run writer exception (`:3230-3231`) the partially-written `outputPath` file is left on disk while the response's `outputPath` field points at it as if usable.
- **What is imprecise:** With 500 malformed weenie JSONs the response is `success:true, totalProcessed:27500` — the 500 drops are visible only in the stdout spam (F120). Per F108 rationale: counts silently exclude failures with no machine-readable signal.
- **What must change:** Add `errorCount` (and optionally `firstErrors[≤5]`) to `IngestWeeniesResult` and the JSON response; populate it from the existing `errors` counter. On the catch path at `:3230`, either delete the partial output file or document the partial state via a response field.

### F122 — lower — MED: the brush silently excludes landblock row/col 254 — `TerrainAlgorithms.MapSize = 254` with an exclusive `>=` test makes the world's last row/column uneditable (F93 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:16` (`public const uint MapSize = 254;`), `:60` (`if (lbX >= mapSize || lbY >= mapSize) continue;` in `GetAffectedVertices`), same pattern in `GetVerticesInRect` `:105` and `AddEdgeNeighbors` (mapSize param). Contrast: `TerrainDocument.cs:437-487` populates the base cache for 0..254 inclusive (`MAP_WIDTH + 1` loop), `Lb()` validates 0..254, `list-landblocks` defaults max 254.
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"lower","x":48864,"y":100,"radius":2}` (x inside lb 254) modifies 0 vertices on the 254 row — `success:false, verticesModified:0` with no hint why, even though the landblock exists, has terrain data, and is listed by `list-landblocks`. Affects every brush op routed through `GetAffectedVertices`: `lower`, `raise`, `smooth`, `set-height`, `paint` (sites in this batch: `CommandEngine.cs:490`).
- **What must change:** Change the constant to 255 (or the comparisons to `> 254`) so lb 254 is included: `if (lbX > 254 || lbY > 254) continue;` in `GetAffectedVertices`, `GetVerticesInRect`, and `AddEdgeNeighbors`. Audit other `MapSize` consumers (`TerrainAlgorithms.cs:388` height sampling has the same exclusion) when fixing.

### F123 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — lower — HIGH: `radius` is not in world units — the engine scales it ×12+1 (`radius*12+1` world units), while the API reference says "Radius in world units"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:31` (doc comment: "Brush radius in brush units (will be scaled: radius*12+1)") and `:38` (`float radius = (brushRadius * 12f) + 1f;`); call chain `CmdLower` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1384-1390`) → `CommandEngine.Lower` (`CommandEngine.cs:467-472`) → `ApplyTerrainEdit:490` passes the JSON value through unscaled. Docs: `docs/agent_api_reference.md:350` (raise, "Radius in world units"; lower at `:368` says "Parameters identical to `raise`"), `:317` (smooth), `:392` (set-height), `:421` (paint).
- **What is imprecise:** The documented example `{"command":"lower",…,"radius":48.0}` produces a brush of 48·12+1 = 577 world units (~3 landblocks wide), 12× the documented size — silently over-editing terrain far outside the intended area. An agent wanting a 24-unit (1-cell) brush must pass ~2.
- **What must change:** Either (a) fix the docs at all four sites to "brush units; effective world radius = radius·12+1 (≈ radius half-cells)" with a corrected example, or (b) make the code take world units (divide by 12 at the wrapper or drop the scaling in `GetAffectedVertices`) — (b) changes behavior for existing callers and must update the GUI brush path too. Pick one; today code and docs disagree.

### F124 — layout-save — MED: a pack failure is swallowed — the command returns success:true while storing an empty overlay entry that lies (`hasOverlay:true`) and silently falls back to the DAT after reload
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/LayoutDatDocument.cs:59-65` (`catch` in `SetLayout` logs and stores `Data = Array.Empty<byte>()`, still `MarkDirty()`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Layout.cs:66-78` (`LayoutSave` returns `new LayoutSaveResult(true, …)` unconditionally); read side `LayoutDatDocument.cs:77` (`entry.Data.Length > 0` means an empty entry can never be unpacked) and `CommandEngine.Layout.cs:51-58` (`layout-get` then reports `hasOverlay:true` with the layout actually served from the DAT).
- **What is imprecise:** If `Pack` throws (oversized/inconsistent LayoutDesc), the caller gets `{"success":true}`; in-session reads still work via `_objectCache`, but after restart the overlay is a 0-byte stub: `layout-list` shows `hasOverlay:true`, `layout-get` silently serves the DAT original, and export logs an error per `:151-153` while `SaveToDatsInternal` still returns true.
- **What must change:** Have `SetLayout` rethrow (or return bool) on pack failure instead of storing an empty entry, and make `LayoutSave` surface that as a failed command response. Alternatively at minimum: do not insert the empty-Data entry — leave prior state untouched on failure.

### F125 — layout-list — MED: only DAT-enumerated ids are listed — an overlay saved under an id that does not exist in the local DAT is invisible, even with overlayOnly:true
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Layout.cs:29-37` (`ids = dats.Dats.GetAllIdsOfType<LayoutDesc>()`, optionally filtered by `doc.HasStoredLayout` — the overlay's own key set is never enumerated); `layout-save` accepts any uint id (`CommandEngine.Layout.cs:66-78`, no DAT-existence check) and `layout-get` can read such an id back fine.
- **What is imprecise:** Save a new LayoutDesc as `0x16009999` (absent from DAT) → success:true; `layout-list overlayOnly:true` does not include it; the help text ("Lists every LayoutDesc id from the local DAT (or only ones with a project overlay)") and the doc line `agent_api_reference.md:1873` imply the overlay set is fully covered.
- **What must change:** Union the overlay's stored ids into the enumeration (expose the key set from `LayoutDatDocument`, e.g. `IEnumerable<uint> StoredLayoutIds`, and use `dats ids ∪ stored ids`, with `hasOverlay` per id). The `overlayOnly` branch should enumerate the stored ids directly instead of filtering DAT ids.

### F126 — layout-save — MED: `fromJson` is a filesystem path to a JSON file, but help and the API reference present it as inline JSON
- **Where:** code `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Layout.cs:67-71` (`File.Exists(jsonPath)` / `File.ReadAllText`); help `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2418` ("Saves a JSON LayoutDesc into the project's LayoutDatDocument overlay" — no hint it's a path); docs `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1875-1877` (`"fromJson":"…"` example plus prose "Save accepts a JSON LayoutDesc and stores it in the overlay").
- **What is imprecise:** An agent passing the serialized LayoutDesc inline (the natural reading, and what `layout-get` returns) gets `JSON file not found: {"id":…}` — the whole document echoed as a "path". The arg name itself ("fromJson") actively suggests inline content.
- **What must change:** Either accept both (if the trimmed value starts with `{`, parse inline; else treat as path — document the dual behavior), or fix the metadata: help description → "Saves a LayoutDesc from a JSON *file* (fromJson = path)" and api-ref example → `"fromJson":"C:\\…\\layout_0x16000010.json"`.

### F127 — load — MED: the API reference's response shape omits the entire `autoRestore` block — the most consequential part of the response is undocumented
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:221-237` (response example + field table list only projectName/projectFile/projectDir/datDirectory) vs `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1298-1321` (`autoRestore` with 8 sub-objects — ontology, pairings, townGazetteer, poiGazetteer, wcidAcpedia, spawnGazetteer, regions, weenieIndex — each `{source, filePresent, loaded, count, error}`).
- **What is imprecise:** Agents have no documented way to learn that `load` auto-restores 8 project-sidecar files, nor how to detect a failed restore (`loaded:false` + `error`). Note also the `spawnGazetteer.count` is the number of landblock buckets, not spawn records (`CommandEngine.cs:230,243` use `_spawnGazetteer.Count` on a `Dictionary<ushort, List<SpawnRecord>>`) — document that unit or sum the lists.
- **What must change:** Add the `autoRestore` object to the `load` response docs with the per-entry shape, the 8 sidecar file names (`ontology_cache.jsonl`, `building_pairings.json`, `town_gazetteer.json`, `poi_gazetteer.json`, `wcid_acpedia_join.jsonl`, `ace_spawn_records.jsonl`/`spawn_gazetteer.json` fallback, `region_gazetteer.json`, `weenie_index.jsonl`), and a note on the spawn-count unit.

### F128 — ingest-weenies / load-ontology-cache / load-building-pairings / list-dirty-tiles — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md (F116 class)
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — zero occurrences of any of the four names (verified by search). All four are advertised in `help` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2389, 2394, 2446, 2462`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same doc-absence class as F82/F102/F116 — notably ingest-weenies' LSD directory-layout expectation (`<lsdPath>/weenies/*.json`) and default output (`<lsdPath>/weenie_summary.jsonl`), load-ontology-cache's project-default path + `path` alias, and list-dirty-tiles' response shape exist nowhere outside source.
- **What must change:** Add reference sections for the four commands following the F82 remediation format (request, params, response example, field table).

### F129 — list-landblocks — LOW: `truncated` reports true when the result count exactly equals `limit` even if no further landblocks exist
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1998-1999` (`results.Count >= limit` — the loop stops *at* limit, so "exactly limit matches and nothing more" is indistinguishable from "stopped early").
- **What is imprecise:** A range containing exactly 500 landblocks with the default limit returns `truncated:true`, falsely telling agents to re-query with a larger limit.
- **What must change:** Detect overflow instead of saturation — e.g. scan until `limit + 1` hits and report `truncated = found > limit` while returning only `limit` rows, or break out with an explicit `sawMore` flag set when a row is skipped due to the limit.

### F130 — load-ontology-cache — LOW: malformed cache lines are skipped with a stderr-only notice — the response carries no skipped-line count
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/OntologyService.cs:1735-1737` (per-line `catch` → `Console.Error.WriteLine`, not counted in the returned `count`); response shape `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2673-2676` (`entriesLoaded` only).
- **What is imprecise:** A half-corrupt `ontology_cache.jsonl` loads as `success:true` with a reduced `entriesLoaded`; the drop count is only inferable by diffing against the file's line count. Errors do go to stderr (correct channel), so this is visibility polish, not protocol corruption.
- **What must change:** Count skips in `LoadFromCache` and surface them (e.g. return `(loaded, skipped)` or add an out param) through `LoadOntologyCacheResult` → response field `linesSkipped`.

### F131 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — mark-tiles-clean — HIGH: only the DirtyLbs index is cleared — per-tile `entry.Dirty` flags survive, so the cache still treats tiles as dirty
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1018-1022` (`MarkTilesClean` → `cache.ClearDirty()`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TileCache.cs:139-141` (`ClearDirty` clears only `_manifest.DirtyLbs`). The dirty state is dual-tracked: `MarkDirty` sets BOTH `DirtyLbs` and per-tile `entry.Dirty` (`TileCache.cs:106-118`), and `MarkAllLbTilesDirty` sets only `entry.Dirty` (`TileCache.cs:130-137`).
- **What is imprecise:** Help says "Force-clear all dirty bits without regenerating" (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2463`), but after mark-tiles-clean every tile whose `entry.Dirty` was set is still rejected by `TileCache.Get` (`TileCache.cs:58` `if (entry.Dirty) return null;`) — forcing regeneration on next `get-tile` — and `tile-stats` keeps reporting a non-zero `dirty` count (`TileCache.cs:241`). Meanwhile `list-dirty-tiles` shows 0, so the two surfaces contradict each other.
- **What must change:** `ClearDirty()` must also reset `entry.Dirty = false` on every `_manifest.Tiles` entry (mirror of `MarkAllLbTilesDirty`), so both representations clear together and `Get`/`tile-stats` agree with `list-dirty-tiles`.

### F132 — mesh-vs-obj-export-chunk — MED: cache-disable and fastMode fallback behavior contradict the method's own documentation
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MeshParity.cs:233-235` vs XML doc at `:208-213`; `:245-252` vs XML doc at `:216-223`. Same pattern in `EnvCellVsSetupModelChunk` (`:382-384`, `:393-399`).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) The doc states "Passing an empty string disables the cache (parse-every-record mode)", but `effCacheRoot = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(cacheRoot) ? DefaultMeshCacheRoot : cacheRoot!` coerces `""` to the DEFAULT root, and `hasCache = !IsNullOrWhiteSpace(effCacheRoot)` is therefore always true — the cache cannot be disabled. (b) The doc states "When fastMode is true, the range bounds are ignored and only the Holtburg subset is walked", but when `fastMode:true` is passed WITHOUT `fastModeIds` the code silently falls through to the full `[startId, endId)` BTree walk.
- **What must change:** Either implement the documented contract (empty-string cacheRoot → no progress.json/resume; fastMode without ids → error or empty set), or rewrite both XML-doc paragraphs to match the code. Pick one; today the doc actively misleads the validator author.

### F133 — mesh-vs-obj-export-chunk — MED: the per-record sha-keyed cache described everywhere is never written — `cachedCount` can only ever be 0 or the whole chunk
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MeshParity.cs:54-61` ("Each record's per-chunk JSON entry is keyed by sha256 … Cache root is `<root>/<sha>.json`"), `:270-273` ("The full per-record cache (sha-keyed) lives next to progress.json"), `:944-949` ("The per-record sha-cache is the canonical source of truth"), `docs/mesh-parity-method.md:63-64`. Actual writes: only `progress-<chunkLabel>.json` (`:325-327`); `CachedCount: 0` hardcoded (`:338`, `:475`).
- **What is imprecise:** No `<sha>.json` per-record file is ever created, so the "re-runs only re-parse records whose bytes changed" / "canonical source of truth for cross-port diffs" claims are fiction; the only caching is the all-or-nothing chunk resume. The response's `cachedCount` field is misleading: 0 on every live parse, `recordCount` on resume, never anything between. Bonus: `ComputeRecordSha256`'s comment (`:749-753`) says it returns a 16-char short hex while it returns the full 64-char digest.
- **What must change:** Either implement the per-record `<sha>.json` cache as documented, or strip the cache-contract paragraphs from both the XML docs and `mesh-parity-method.md` and rename/redocument `cachedCount` as "resumedRecordCount". Fix the sha-length comment.

### F134 — mesh-vs-obj-export-chunk — MED: reflection-probe failures silently degrade to an empty/all-missing sweep reported as success (F21 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MeshParity.cs:694-707` (`EnumerateIdsInRange` — `yield break` when the DRW `Tree` field or `GetFilesInRange` method is not found) and `:725-744` (`TryReadRecordBytes` — returns null when the `TryGetFileBytes` signature probe fails, turning EVERY record into status "missing").
- **What is imprecise:** After a DRW API rename, a range sweep returns `success:true, recordCount:0, passCount:0` (or all-"missing") with no hint that the oracle itself broke — exactly the silent-reflection-failure class flagged in F21 for cell-portal-graph-sweep.
- **What must change:** Throw `InvalidOperationException("DRW API drift: …")` from both probes when the member is absent (the file already does this for `DatBTreeFile.Id` at `:710-711`); never convert oracle-plumbing failure into empty-success or per-record "missing".

### F135 — mesh-vs-obj-export-chunk — MED: records whose `TryGet` fails are counted as `parseErrorCount` in the live response but as `missing` in progress.json and resumed responses
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MeshParity.cs:309-312` — `ParseGfxObjRecord`/`ParseSetupRecord` return `Status: "missing"` when `TryGet` fails (`:497-509`, `:532-544`), but the loop treats every non-"ok" status as `parseErr++`. `WriteProgressJson` (`:958-978`) histograms the same record under `missing`, and `TryResumeChunk` (`:836-840`) rebuilds `FailCount = missing + other + parseError` with `ParseErrorCount = parseError` only.
- **What is imprecise:** The same chunk reports different `parseErrorCount` depending on whether it was live-parsed or resumed; the acceptance bar in `docs/mesh-parity-method.md` ("parseErrorCount = 0") can flip across the two paths. Same skew in `EnvCellVsSetupModelChunk` (`:449-452`).
- **What must change:** In the parse loop, branch on the actual status: `"ok"` → pass, `"parse-error"` → parseErr, `"missing"` → fail (with `failures.Add`), other → fail. That makes live and resumed counters identical.

### F136 — mesh-vs-obj-export-chunk — LOW: startId/endId go through the landblock-id shift heuristic — 4-hex-digit file IDs are silently multiplied by 0x10000
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5181-5184` (CmdMeshVsObjExportChunk) using `ParseLbIdScalar` (`:4558-4571`), which left-shifts any `0x`-string of ≤4 hex digits by 16 (`v <<= 16`).
- **What is imprecise:** The shift exists for landblock keys ("0xA9B4" → 0xA9B40000 — sensible for the EnvCell sibling), but this command's range is FILE ids (0x01/0x02 prefixes); a caller passing `"0x0100"` gets 0x01000000 with no notice, while the numeric form `256` stays 256 — same logical value, different parse.
- **What must change:** Parse startId/endId for the mesh chunk with `ParseUIntField` (no shift), or document the lb-shift coercion in the command's XML doc and help; the same scalar must not mean two different numbers depending on string-vs-number JSON type.

### F137 — mine-strings — MED: per-table load failures are silently swallowed — the response carries no error/skip count (F108 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2852-2868` — `catch { /* Skip tables that fail to load */ }` around `writer.GetStringTable(tableType)`; response shape `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2594-2601` has only `tablesScanned`/`totalStrings`.
- **What is imprecise:** A corrupt language DAT or a DRW unpack exception on a real table is indistinguishable from "table not present in this DAT": `success:true` with a quietly smaller `tablesScanned`. The intent (some tables may legitimately not exist) only justifies swallowing not-found, not all exceptions.
- **What must change:** Count exception-skipped tables separately and surface e.g. `tablesFailed` (with the last error message) in `MineStringsResult` → response, distinguishing absent (`result.Success == false`) from threw.

### F138 — mine-strings — LOW: CSV escaping ignores newlines — multi-line strings corrupt the output rows
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2662-2663` (`Csv` quotes only when the value contains `,` or `"`); used by the mine-strings CSV writer at `:2872-2878`.
- **What is imprecise:** A StringTable entry containing `\n`/`\r` is emitted raw, splitting one logical record across CSV lines; downstream line-oriented parsers see phantom rows.
- **What must change:** Quote when the value contains `,`, `"`, `\n`, or `\r` (RFC 4180), i.e. extend the predicate in `Csv`.

### F139 — motion-classify-swing — MED: response reports success:true even when classification failed (failureReason set, including bad motionTableId)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:897-898` (`success = true` hardcoded); engine failure shapes at `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MotionParity.cs:229-243` (motion-table-not-in-dat), `:252-267`, `:291-306`, `:345-360`.
- **What is imprecise:** A typo'd `motionTableId` yields `success:true` with `resolvedMotionCmd:null` — agents keying on `success` treat a hard input error as a successful classification. Sibling diagnostics set success from the result (e.g. mesh chunk `success = r.FailCount == 0`).
- **What must change:** `success = r.FailureReason == null` in CmdMotionClassifySwing (the failureReason field already carries the diagnostic for the false case).

### F140 — motion-classify-swing — LOW: `SwingLinkClass.Aim` is unreachable — the enum/docs promise a missile classification the code never produces
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MotionParity.cs:59-71` (enum doc: "Aim for the missile stances") vs `:276-306` — the only classes ever assigned are Magic_Cast, Melee_High/Medium/Low, and Unknown; missile stances fall into the melee candidate walk and come back labeled `Melee_*` (or no-candidate-matched).
- **What is imprecise:** A BowCombat classification that resolves via `AttackHigh1` reports `linkClass: "Melee_High"`; nothing ever reports "Aim".
- **What must change:** Either add missile-stance candidate selection that assigns `SwingLinkClass.Aim`, or delete the enum member and correct the enum's XML doc.

### F141 — motion-table-anim-hooks — MED: a nonexistent motionTableId returns success:true with all-zero counts — indistinguishable from a real empty table
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MotionParity.cs:511-519` (silent zero-result on `TryGet<MotionTable>` failure); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5275-5276` hardcodes `success = true` and the response has no error/found field.
- **What is imprecise:** `motion-classify-swing` reports `motion-table-not-in-dat` for the same condition; this command silently fabricates an empty result, so an agent probing a typo'd DID concludes "no hooks" instead of "no such table".
- **What must change:** Add a `Found`/`FailureReason` member to `MotionAnimHooksResult`, populate it on the TryGet-miss path, and set `success = r.Found` (or emit `error`) in CmdMotionTableAnimHooks.

### F142 — motion-table-anim-hooks — MED: `hookType` emits DRW class names ("SoundHook") while the in-file docs specify enum names ("Sound") and tell validators to filter on them
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.MotionParity.cs:631` (`string typeName = rawHook.GetType().Name;`) vs XML docs at `:420-422` ("The hookType byte is the AnimationHookType enum"), `:431-434` (e.g. "Sound" / "CreateParticle" / "CallPES"), and the default-case comment `:657-663` (the validator can filter on HookType == "Sound" | "CreateParticle" | "CallPES").
- **What is imprecise:** Actual emitted values are "SoundHook", "SoundTweakedHook", "CreateParticleHook", "CallPESHook", "NoOpHook", … — a validator written from the documentation's filter example matches zero hooks.
- **What must change:** Either map the subtype to the dats.xml AnimationHookType enum name (strip the "Hook" suffix / switch on subtype) or rewrite the three doc passages to state that `hookType` is the DRW class name including the "Hook" suffix.

### F143 — move-object — MED: object mutations outside `transact` never invalidate the tile cache (F112 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1898-1907` → `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1355-1368` (`MoveObject` — no `TileCache.MarkDirty`); the only tile-invalidation call site is the transact commit path (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3950-3954`). Same gap applies to direct `add-object`/`remove-object`/`rotate-object`/`clear-objects`.
- **What is imprecise:** Tiles are rendered from terrain + objects (render-preview draws objects), so after a direct move-object the LB tile keeps serving the pre-move object position as fresh, while `list-dirty-tiles` reports nothing pending.
- **What must change:** Mirror the transact invalidation: after a successful direct object mutation, mark the affected lbKey dirty in the tile pipeline (same helper the transact path uses), per the F112 remediation.

### F144 — obj-export — MED: `triangleCount` and `partCount` misreport what was actually written to the .obj
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10747-10748` (Setup path returns `parts` only — `TriangleCount` defaults to 0 even though triangles were written; `parts` counts `setup.Parts.Count` including parts skipped as missing by `WavefrontMeshExport.TryWriteSetup`, `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WavefrontMeshExport.cs:60-63`); `:10754` (GfxObj path reports `gfx.Polygons.Count`).
- **What is imprecise:** (a) Every Setup export responds `triangleCount: 0`. (b) For GfxObjs the value is the raw polygon count, which both includes polygons the exporter skips (`StipplingType.NoPos` / invalid `PosSurface`, `WavefrontMeshExport.cs:105-107`) and undercounts fan-triangulated quads/n-gons (`:161-165`). The field name promises the .obj's triangle count; it is neither.
- **What must change:** Have `TriangulateFaces`/`WriteVerticesAndFaces` return the emitted triangle count (positions.Count/3 summed) and plumb it through `TryWriteSetup`/`WriteGfxObj` into `ObjExportResult.TriangleCount`; report `partCount` as parts actually written (skips excluded) or add a `partsSkipped` field.

### F145 — obj-export — MED: the exporter's doc claims negative-surface faces are exported with reversed winding — the code never does
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/WavefrontMeshExport.cs:28-32` (WriteGfxObj XML doc: "all renderable faces (positive and negative surfaces)… Negative-face polygons are emitted with reversed vertex order") vs `:117-124` — `EmitFace` is only ever invoked with `reversed: false, useNegUVs: false`, and grouping uses `PosSurface` exclusively (`:107-109`).
- **What is imprecise:** Double-sided/negative-surface geometry is silently exported single-sided; `reversed`/`useNegUVs` parameters (`:127-159`) are dead. The doc describes a feature that does not exist, and the round-trip via obj-import permanently drops NegSurface data.
- **What must change:** Either emit a second reversed pass for polygons with `NegSurface >= 0` (grouped under the NegSurface DID, using NegUVIndices) as documented, or correct the WriteGfxObj doc to state positive-surface-only export and note the NegSurface loss.

### F146 — obj-export / obj-import — MED: `datId` / `surfaceDid` / `gfxObjId` / `setupId` reject hex strings with an opaque error (F81/F106 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3851` (`U(node, "datId")`), `:3864-3866` (`U(node, "surfaceDid"/"gfxObjId"/"setupId")`); helper `U` at `:3597-3598` does a bare `GetValue<uint>()`.
- **What is imprecise:** Every DAT DID in this codebase's responses and docs is rendered as `"0x01000007"`-style hex, but feeding that form back throws `System.InvalidOperationException` from JsonNode with no field name — while sibling commands accept hex via `ParseUIntField`/`Hex32`. New instance of the F81/F106 hex-coercion inconsistency.
- **What must change:** Parse these four args with `ParseUIntField` (accepts numbers, decimal strings, and `0x` hex) instead of `U`.

### F147 — obj-export — LOW: a failed Setup export leaves a partial .obj file on disk
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10740-10744` — the `StreamWriter` is opened (truncating/creating `outputPath`) before `TryWriteSetup` validates the Setup exists; on failure the method returns an error but the header-only file remains.
- **What is imprecise:** `success:false` responses still mutate the filesystem; a stale partial file can be mistaken for a successful prior export.
- **What must change:** Validate existence before opening the writer (`dats.TryGet<Setup>` first — it is already re-fetched at `:10745` anyway), or delete `outputPath` on the failure path.

### F148 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — obj-import — HIGH: negative (relative) OBJ face indices resolve against buffers already polluted by the parser's own fan-triangulation — wrong geometry is imported silently
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/ObjSingleMeshImporter.cs:312-326` — for each face the parser APPENDS 3 synthetic corner copies per triangle into the shared `positions`/`normals`/`texCoords` lists mid-parse; `ParseObjIndex` (`:448-455`) resolves a negative index as `count + idx` where `count` is the inflated `positions.Count`/`texCoords.Count`/`normals.Count` at the time the NEXT face line is read (`:290-292`).
- **What is imprecise:** In Wavefront OBJ, `f -3 -2 -1` refers to the three most recently DECLARED `v` records. After the first face is parsed, the list tail is synthetic triangle copies, so every subsequent negative index points at the wrong vertex — the mesh imports without error but with scrambled geometry, then gets a BSP built over it and stored into the portal document.
- **What must change:** Track declared-element counts separately from the expansion buffers — e.g. accumulate expanded corners/triangles in dedicated lists (`expandedPositions` etc.) and keep `positions/normals/texCoords` containing only `v`/`vn`/`vt` declarations, resolving both positive and negative indices against the declaration lists only.

### F149 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — obj-import — HIGH: per-face `usemtl` surface assignment misaligns whenever faces precede the first parseable usemtl
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/ObjSingleMeshImporter.cs:324-325` (`if (hasUsemtl) faceSurfaceDids.Add(currentSurfaceDid);` — entries only start once a parseable `usemtl surface_0x…` has been seen) consumed positionally from triangle 0 at `:57-59` (`i < faceSurfaceDids.Count ? faceSurfaceDids[i] : surfaceDid`).
- **What is imprecise:** If N triangles are emitted before the first parseable usemtl (or under a Blender-style unparseable material name), `faceSurfaceDids` is N entries shorter than `triangles` but is zipped against it from index 0 — every face's surface DID shifts left by N: head faces wrongly receive the first usemtl's DID and the last N faces silently fall back to the `surfaceDid` argument. Multi-material imports get visibly wrong textures with `success:true`.
- **What must change:** Append one entry per emitted triangle unconditionally — `faceSurfaceDids.Add(hasUsemtl && currentSurfaceDid != 0 ? currentSurfaceDid : 0)` with 0 meaning "use fallback" at consumption — so list positions always correspond 1:1 with triangles.

### F150 — obj-import — MED: vertex ids 32768..65534 wrap negative through the `(short)` cast in Polygon.VertexIds
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/ObjSingleMeshImporter.cs:180` (`new List<short> { (short)ida, (short)idb, (short)idc }`); the only overflow guard is at `:149-150` (`nextId == ushort.MaxValue`).
- **What is imprecise:** Meshes with more than 32,767 unique corners produce polygons whose VertexIds are negative; both the exporter (`WavefrontMeshExport.cs:133-134`) and the BSP builder (`BspGenerator.cs:248-249`) treat negative ids as skip-this-corner, so the import "succeeds" with degenerate/dropped polygons.
- **What must change:** Throw `"Too many unique vertices"` at `nextId == 32767` (short range), or store ids without the lossy cast if the DRW Polygon type permits.

### F151 — obj-import — MED: no validation of `surfaceDid` existence or of caller-supplied `gfxObjId`/`setupId` prefix and collisions — SetEntry silently overwrites
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10767-10804` — `surfaceDid` is passed straight into `TryBuild` (no `dats.TryGet<Surface>` / 0x08-prefix check); explicit non-zero `gfxObjId`/`setupId` skip allocation entirely with no 0x01/0x02 prefix check and no existing-record check before `portalDoc.SetEntry` (`:10803-10804`). Contrast `add-object`, which verifies modelId existence (`:1316-1322`), and `bsp-build`, which enforces the 0x01 prefix (`:10820-10821`).
- **What is imprecise:** A typo'd surfaceDid yields a mesh referencing a nonexistent Surface (invisible/broken at render, `success:true`); `gfxObjId: 0x02FF0001` stamps a GfxObj into the Setup namespace; reusing a retail DID silently stages an overwrite of a base record.
- **What must change:** Validate `surfaceDid` resolves via `dats.TryGet<Surface>`; enforce `(gfxObjId >> 24) == 0x01` / `(setupId >> 24) == 0x02` when non-zero; refuse (or require an explicit `overwrite:true`) when the target id already exists in the DAT or portal document.

### F152 — obj-import — MED: BspGenerator never marks any physics leaf solid, contradicting its own algorithm doc; the reflection setter can silently no-op
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/BspGenerator.cs:24-29` (header: "The negative half-space of every splitting plane is considered solid; positive is open") vs `:87-88`, `:91-92`, `:106-107` — all three `PhysicsLeaf` call sites pass `solid: false`, so the generated tree has zero solid leaves; `SetSolid` (`:304-310`) uses `_solidField?.SetValue` and silently does nothing if none of the three probed backing-field names exists in the current DRW version.
- **What is imprecise:** Imported meshes (obj-import) and rebuilt trees (bsp-build) carry physics BSPs whose Solid flags say "everything is open space", while the documentation claims solid negative half-spaces — interior/containment tests in any consumer that honors Solid behave differently from retail-built BSPs.
- **What must change:** Decide and align: if the simplified all-open-leaf tree is the intended approximation, rewrite the header paragraph to say so explicitly; if solidity is needed, pass `solid: true` for back-side leaves reached exclusively through negative half-spaces. Independently, make `SetSolid` throw (or log once) when the reflection probe finds no field, instead of silently producing structurally different output.

### F153 — obj-import — LOW: the BSP is built twice per import
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/ObjSingleMeshImporter.cs:197` (`BspGenerator.Build(gfx)` inside `BuildGfxObj`) and again `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10800-10801` ("Build BSP so the imported mesh has physics + drawing trees").
- **What is imprecise:** Redundant O(n log n) work on every import; the CommandEngine comment implies the importer doesn't build one, inviting future divergence.
- **What must change:** Remove the second call (or the importer-internal one) and update the comment.

### F154 — obj-export / obj-import — MED: undocumented anywhere — absent from `help` and from docs/agent_api_reference.md (F116/F128 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2335-2486` (help array — no obj-export/obj-import rows); `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — zero occurrences; `docs/terminal_command_inventory_2026-06-10.md:245-246` lists both with empty args. Method docs reference them only as bake-contamination warnings (`docs/hypotheticalmethod.md:71`).
- **What is imprecise:** The arg contracts (`datId`+`outputPath`; `objPath`+`surfaceDid`+optional `gfxObjId`/`setupId` with 0x__FF auto-allocation, persistence-on-export semantics) exist nowhere outside source — notably the requirement that obj-import's staged entries only persist on the next `export`.
- **What must change:** Add help rows and an API-reference section per the F82 remediation format, explicitly documenting the auto-allocation range, the usemtl `surface_0x…` material convention, and that imports are staged in PortalDatDocument until export.

### F155 — paint — MED: terrain `type` is accepted over 0..255 though only the region table's defined types (0..32) are meaningful — undefined types are silently painted and persisted
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1411` (`ByteInRange(node, "type")` with the default `max = 255`, `:3603-3609`); stored unmasked as a full byte in `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/TerrainDocument.cs:82,100` and cast straight to the enum on export at `WorldBuilder.Shared/Models/Project.cs:322` (`(TerrainTextureType)entry.Type`). Contrast: the DAT terrain word has a 5-bit type field — the stamp round-trip masks `entry.Type & 0x1F` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/StampService.cs:83-86`), so a painted type ≥ 32 aliases mod 32 when copied via `paste-stamp` but keeps its raw value in the document. `fill` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1420`) and `road`'s `value` share the helper.
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"paint","x":…,"type":200}` succeeds, reports `terrainType: 200`, and persists a value no region texture table defines; downstream consumers disagree on what it means (document byte 200 vs stamp/DAT 5-bit alias 8), and `docs/agent_api_reference.md:421` only hints "use `get-region` to see available types" without the command enforcing it.
- **What must change:** Pass `max: 32` (or validate against the loaded Region's terrain-texture count) in `CmdPaint`/`CmdFill` `ByteInRange` calls, erroring with the valid range. The shared-brush radius/row-254/tile-cache defects are F123, F122, and F112 — not re-filed.

### F156 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — paste-stamp — HIGH: source/destination world coordinates are unvalidated — negative or beyond-world values wrap through the ushort LbKey cast and capture/paste terrain (and objects) in the WRONG landblocks (F71 class, new sites)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2243-2250` (`F()` checks finiteness only); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/StampAlgorithms.cs:63-65` (`(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)` from `Floor(world/192)`) and `:127-129` (object placement), `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/StampService.cs:61-63` (capture), `:98-100` (object capture loop), `:124` (SourceLandblockId).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F71 class on WORLD coords: `destX: -500` → `lbX = -3` → the `(ushort)` cast lands the paste in LB row 0xFD; `destY > 48 960` bleeds into the X byte. Terrain in landblocks the caller never named is rewritten and the command returns `success:true` with a plausible-looking `landblocks` list. Unlike the brush path (`TerrainAlgorithms.cs:53` clamps `gx/gy < 0`), the stamp path has no clamp at all.
- **What must change:** In `CommandEngine.PasteStamp` (`CommandEngine.cs:2093`), validate `srcMin/srcMax/dest` to `[0, 254*192+192)` (and `srcMin <= srcMax`) before capture/paste, erroring out of range. In `StampAlgorithms.ComputePaste` and `StampService.CaptureRegion`, skip (don't wrap) vertices/objects whose `lbX`/`lbY` fall outside 0..254.

### F157 — paste-stamp — MED: `includeObjects` failures are silently swallowed (objects vanish from the paste with no skip count), and the paste never invalidates the tile cache outside `transact` (F21 + F112/F143 classes)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2104-2108` (`objLookup` `catch { return Enumerable.Empty<StaticObject>(); }` — a whole landblock's objects drop out of the capture), `:2128-2135` (`catch { /* skip objects in unloaded landblocks */ }` on `AddStaticObject`); no `_tileCache` call anywhere in `PasteStamp` (`:2093-2141`) — invalidation only happens via `OnTransactCommitted` (`CommandEngine.cs:1040-1058`).
- **What is imprecise:** `objectsPlaced` can silently be lower than the stamp's object count with `success:true` and no indication which landblocks failed; a direct (non-transact) `paste-stamp` leaves `get-tile` serving stale imagery for every modified LB — the same defect class as F112 (terrain) and F143 (objects), new site.
- **What must change:** Count capture/place failures and return them (`objectsSkipped`, optionally per-LB messages). After a successful paste, mark `pasteResult.TerrainChanges.Keys` (and object LBs) dirty via the tile cache exactly as `OnTransactCommitted` does.

### F158 — paste-stamp — LOW: `blendEdges` averages height-table INDICES, not heights, and the response hardcodes `success: true` even for a zero-change paste
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/StampAlgorithms.cs:150-152` (`(stampHeight + existingHeight) / 2` on byte indices into the non-linear `LandHeightTable`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2251` (`success = true` literal — contrast `TerrainEditResult.Success` semantics at `CommandResults.cs:172-180`).
- **What is imprecise:** Where the height table is non-uniform, the midpoint index is not the midpoint height, so blended edges can step visibly; `terrainChanges: 0` still reports `success: true`, unlike every brush command where a no-op returns `success: false`.
- **What must change:** Blend in height units (`FindNearestHeightIndex((h[a]+h[b])/2, table)` — the helper already exists at `:157`), and set `success = terrainChanges > 0 || objectsPlaced > 0` (or document the divergence).

### F159 — physics-jump-formula-sweep — MED: `branchHistogram` double-counts every non-clamped case, and the "all 5 branches" coverage gate only checks 4
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.PhysicsParity.cs:243-247` — `key` is the clamped-prefix-stripped token; `hist[key]++` then unconditionally `hist[r.Branch]++`. When the branch has no `clamped+` prefix (the majority of the 1000 cases), `key == r.Branch`, so the same bucket is incremented twice. `:249-254` — `requiredBranches = { "zero", "no-weenie", "weenie-success", "weenie-fail" }` while the XML doc (`:220-223`) and the comment (`:249`) promise "all 5 branch types MUST appear" (the 5th being the clamped variant).
- **What is imprecise:** For a non-clamped-only run the histogram sums to 2× `caseCount`; mixed runs sum to something in between — any validator asserting `Σhistogram == caseCount` fails, and one asserting per-branch counts gets doubled numbers. The `notes` "All branches covered." can be true with zero `clamped+*` tokens present, contradicting the documented contract.
- **What must change:** Increment the full token only when it differs from the stripped key (`if (key != r.Branch) hist[r.Branch]++;`), and either add a clamped-coverage check (any key starting `clamped+`) to `requiredBranches` handling or correct the doc/comment to "4 base branches".

### F160 — physics-replay-trace — LOW: the response field `tickCount` is the compared-row count, not the trace's tick count, and the LB-crossing/teleport skip count exists only inside the `notes` string
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.PhysicsParity.cs:818-819` (`TickCount: comparedRows` — rows skipped at `:529-552` for LB crossings and >50 m jumps, plus the final row, are excluded); `wrapSkipCount` appears only interpolated into `notes` (`:814-816`), never as a structured field. Wrapper passes it through verbatim (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:866`).
- **What is imprecise:** A 1100-row trace with 30 skips reports `tickCount: 1069`; a consumer computing per-tick statistics or verifying capture length against `tickCount` gets a silently smaller number, and machine consumers must regex the notes to learn how many comparisons were skipped.
- **What must change:** Rename to `comparedTickCount` (or add `traceRowCount` + `skippedComparisons` fields) and emit `wrapSkipCount` as a structured response field.

### F161 — physics-jump-formula / physics-jump-formula-sweep / physics-replay-trace / prune-tiles / paste-stamp / placement-set-scope — MED: no entry in docs/agent_api_reference.md (F23/F116/F128/F154 class); the three physics commands are additionally absent from `help`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2335-2486` — the help array has rows for `paste-stamp` (`:2371`), `prune-tiles` (`:2464`), and `placement-set-scope` (`:2433`) but none for `physics-jump-formula`/`physics-jump-formula-sweep`/`physics-replay-trace` (dispatched at `:325-328`). `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — zero occurrences of `physics`, `prune-tiles`, or `placement-set-scope`; `paste-stamp` appears only inside the transact op-alphabet list (`:1213`), with no section of its own.
- **What is imprecise:** The arg contracts (e.g. `weenieFallback`'s `null`/`"NaN"` sentinel semantics, `subjectSignal` prediction-vs-pose, trace/scenario JSON shapes, `olderThan` format, the stamp source-rect inclusivity, scope aliases `classDefault|class|a|world`) exist nowhere outside source.
- **What must change:** Add API-reference sections for all six (and help rows for the three physics commands) per the F82 remediation format, documenting the sentinel and signal-selection semantics explicitly.

### F162 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — placement-add-outdoor / placement-add-dungeon / placement-list — HIGH: lbX/lbY are unvalidated — values > 255 or negative wrap through the ushort LbKey pack and target the WRONG landblock (recurring F9/F18/F35/F47/F53/F66 class, new sites)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2952-2953` / `:2967-2968` / `:2935-2936` (raw `GetValue<int>()`, no range check — contrast the shared `Lb()` helper at `:3632`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:26-28` (`(ushort)(((uint)lbX << 8) | (uint)lbY)`), `:77`, `:94`.
- **What is imprecise:** `placement-add-outdoor` with `lbX: 300` silently records the placement under LB 0x2Cxx; `lbY: 256` bleeds into the X byte; negatives produce garbage keys. The exported `obj_Cell_Id` then directs ACE to spawn the object in a landblock the caller never named, with `success:true`. Additionally in `placement-list`, providing only ONE of `lbX`/`lbY` silently disables the filter entirely (`:26-28` requires both) instead of erroring.
- **What must change:** Validate `0 <= lbX/lbY <= 254` in all three wrappers (reuse `Lb()`), and in `CmdPlacementList` reject a lone `lbX`/`lbY` with an explicit "both or neither" error.

### F163 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — placement-add-dungeon / placement-remove / placement-set-scope (dungeon paths) — CRITICAL: dungeon placement mutations are never persisted — no MarkDirty/Update event/save, so every dungeon add/remove/scope-change silently evaporates at session end
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:108-109` (`dng.InstancePlacements.Add(p);` then immediate return — no `MarkDirty()`/`ForceSave()`/storage write; contrast the outdoor branch's `project.Save()` at `:85`), `:134` (`RemoveAt` — same), `:174` (`Scope = parsed` — same; outdoor twin saves at `:162`). Persistence machinery requires either an `Update` event (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/BaseDocument.cs:44-74` — only `OnUpdate`/`ForceSave` raise it) or `IsDirty` at close (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:258-291`, `:326-343`); direct `List` mutation triggers neither.
- **What is imprecise:** `placement-add-dungeon` returns `success:true` with an index; `placement-list` and `placement-export-sql` see it for the rest of the session; after restart it is gone — silent data loss of a committed edit. (Only a NEWLY created dungeon doc persists its initial — pre-mutation, hence empty — projection via `DocumentManager.cs:167-170`.) Remove/set-scope likewise report success and roll back invisibly.
- **What must change:** After each dungeon mutation call `dng.ForceSave()` (raises Update with `IsDirty=true`, which the DocumentManager batch persists), or synchronously `DocumentManager.DocumentStorageService.UpdateDocumentAsync(dng.Id, dng.SaveToProjection())`. Add a round-trip regression test: add-dungeon → dispose/reload project → placement-list must still show the row.

### F164 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — placement-list / placement-remove / placement-set-scope — HIGH: two incompatible dungeon index spaces — list reports PER-DOCUMENT indices (duplicated across docs) while remove/set-scope consume a GLOBAL index flattened over nondeterministic ConcurrentDictionary order
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:52-63` (`for (int i = 0; …) … Index: i` — resets to 0 for every `DungeonDocument`), vs `:124-137` and `:166-177` (accumulate `total` across `project.DocumentManager.ActiveDocs` and treat `index` as global); `ActiveDocs` is a `ConcurrentDictionary` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:14,29`) whose enumeration order is unspecified and can differ between the list call and the remove call. `PlacementAddDungeon` returns the per-document index (`:109`), feeding the same confusion. Help text "by index in its respective list" (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2432`).
- **What is imprecise:** With ≥2 dungeon docs loaded, `placement-list` shows e.g. two rows both labeled `index: 0`; `placement-remove {"kind":"dungeon","index":0}` deletes whichever doc the dictionary happens to enumerate first — possibly not the one the user saw — and a later identical call can hit a different doc. Also a negative `index` on the dungeon path escapes the bounds logic and surfaces as a raw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (outdoor returns `success:false`).
- **What must change:** Pick ONE addressing scheme: either (a) make remove/set-scope take `(lbX, lbY, index)` (per-document, matching list's `landblock`+`index` output), or (b) make list emit the flattened global index over a DETERMINISTICALLY ordered (e.g. sorted by `LandblockKey`) doc sequence and use the same ordering in remove/set-scope. Reject `index < 0` explicitly on the dungeon path.

### F165 — placement-list / placement-export-sql — MED: dungeon placements are enumerated from ActiveDocs only — persisted dungeon docs not loaded in this session are invisible to list and silently OMITTED from the SQL export
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:49` (list), `:126`/`:168` (remove/set-scope), `:206` (export source-of-truth build), `:393` (WritePlacementDirectives) — all iterate `project.DocumentManager.ActiveDocs`, which only contains docs someone has loaded (`DocumentManager.cs:121-129`). Contrast `CommandEngine.cs:11641`, which sweeps storage via `ListDocumentIdsAsync("dungeon_")`.
- **What is imprecise:** A dungeon placement persisted in an earlier session (or by the GUI) is exported only if some other command happened to load that `dungeon_XXXX` doc first; otherwise `placement-export-sql` writes a `dungeon_instances.sql` missing rows and reports the smaller `dungeonCount` as success. (Today this is masked by F163 — dungeon placements never persist — but the enumeration gap is independent and becomes load-bearing the moment F163 is fixed.)
- **What must change:** In `PlacementList` and `PlacementExportSqlAsync`, enumerate `DocumentStorageService.ListDocumentIdsAsync("dungeon_")` and `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync<DungeonDocument>` each id (or at minimum union storage ids with ActiveDocs) before iterating.

### F166 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — placement-add-outdoor / placement-add-dungeon / placement-export-sql — HIGH: the default orientation is (w=0, z=1) — a 180° rotation about Z, not identity — and the SQL emitter's partial-null fallback manufactures unnormalized quaternions
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:81-82` (`AnglesW = angW ?? 0f … AnglesZ = angZ ?? 1f`), `:106` (`new Quaternion(angX ?? 0f, angY ?? 0f, angZ ?? 1f, angW ?? 0f)` — System.Numerics ctor is (x,y,z,w), so this is x=0,y=0,z=1,w=0); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Models/OutdoorInstancePlacement.cs:22` (`AnglesZ { get; set; } = 1f` with `AnglesW` defaulting 0); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.cs:222-231` — the all-null branch correctly produces identity `w=1,x=y=z=0`, but the per-component fallback (`z = record.AnglesZ ?? 1f`) and the doc comment at `:209` ("Angles default to identity quaternion (0, 0, 0, 1)") encode the same w↔z confusion.
- **What is imprecise:** The identity quaternion is w=1, x=y=z=0. (w=0, z=1) is a half-turn about +Z — every placement added without explicit angles exports `angles_w = 0, angles_z = 1` and spawns FACING BACKWARDS in ACE. The emitter's special case never rescues it because the placement models store concrete 0/0/0/1 values, not nulls. Supplying only SOME angles (e.g. just `anglesW: 1`) yields `w=1,z=1` — unnormalized garbage (F36/F47 family).
- **What must change:** Default `angW ?? 1f` and `angZ ?? 0f` at both add sites, change `OutdoorInstancePlacement.AnglesZ`'s initializer to 0 with `AnglesW = 1f` (with a JSON-migration note for existing projects), fix `GenerateInsertSql`'s per-component fallbacks (`w ?? 1f`, `z ?? 0f`) and its doc comment, and enforce all-or-none angle supply in the wrappers (error otherwise), normalizing on input like `add-object`.

### F167 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — placement-export-sql — HIGH: placements without a minted static guid get a FRESH RANDOM guid on every export with no DELETE prefix — `apply:true` twice duplicates every ClassDefault placement row, and the random guid ignores ACE's per-landblock static window
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.cs:218-220` (`idempotent` only when `StaticGuidAllocator.IsStatic(guid)`; otherwise `guid = RandomNumberGenerator.GetInt32(1, int.MaxValue)`), `:243-246` (DELETE prefix only on the idempotent path); minting happens only for `PlacementOverride`-scoped placements (`CommandEngine.Placements.cs:275-288`), and `ClassDefault` is the default scope. The correct allocator exists unused for this path: `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/StaticGuidAllocator.cs:39,63` (`0x70000000 | (lb << 12)` window, ACE DeveloperContentCommands gap-finder).
- **What is imprecise:** The doc comment calls this "the legacy auto-guid (random) path" but the consequence is undocumented and surfaces as data corruption: run `placement-export-sql {"apply":true}` twice and every Option-A placement INSERTs again under a new random guid — duplicate spawns stacking in the world DB, reported as success both times. The random range [1, 0x7FFFFFFF) also lands guids in player/dynamic ranges instead of the LB's static window 0x70000000|(lb<<12)..|0xFFF, defeating ACE's static-object addressing conventions and any later Option-B join (`landblock_instance.guid == biota.id`). Relatedly, `rowsAppliedToDb` sums DELETE+INSERT affected rows, overstating inserted placements on idempotent re-applies.
- **What must change:** Mint guids for ALL placements at export time via `StaticGuidAllocator.Allocate(lb, used)` (seeding `used` from previously minted guids in the project and, on apply, from a `SELECT guid FROM landblock_instance WHERE obj_Cell_Id >> 16 = lb`), write them back like the Option-B path does, and emit the DELETE+INSERT shape for every row so re-export/re-apply replaces instead of duplicates. Document `rowsAppliedToDb` as "statements' affected rows (incl. DELETEs)" or split the count.

### F168 — placement-export-sql — MED: landblock_instance SQL hardcodes the `ace_world` database prefix while the per-class enrichment SQL is unqualified — with a differently named world DB, one transaction writes to TWO different databases (or fails)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.cs:233-246` (`INSERT INTO \`{0}\`.\`landblock_instance\`` with `databaseName = "ace_world"` default; same for the DELETE prefix), `:252` (batch passes the default through); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:385-398` (`WritePlacementDirectives` never passes `project.AceDb.Database`); contrast `AceDbConnector.EnrichmentSql.cs:156,179,238,263,282` (unqualified `INSERT INTO \`weenie_properties_*\`` — resolved against the CONNECTION's configured database).
- **What is imprecise:** With `ace-db connect … database: "ace_world_test"`, `apply:true` writes `weenie_properties_*` into `ace_world_test` but `landblock_instance` into literal `ace_world` (or errors if it doesn't exist) — inside what the code presents as one atomic world apply (`Placements.cs:327-328`). The `SameTarget` world/shard collision guard (`:407-410`) compares configured names, not the hardcoded effective target.
- **What must change:** Thread `settings.Database` into `WritePlacementDirectives` → `GenerateInsertSqlBatch(records, settings?.Database ?? "ace_world")` for the apply path (keeping `ace_world` for the file emit, or qualifying both consistently), or strip the database qualifier entirely and rely on the connection's database like the enrichment SQL does.

### F169 — placement-export-sql — MED: `dryRun:true` still mints Option-B guids and SAVES THE PROJECT — "dryRun emits files only" is untrue for project state
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:192` (`if (dryRun) apply = false;` — the only dry-run gate), `:275-288` (BiotaEnrichmentSqlExporter.WriteFiles mints guids, write-back loop sets `op.Guid`/`dp.Guid`, `if (anyMinted) project.Save();` — all unconditional). Help text: "dryRun emits files only (no DB)" (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2436`); docs example `:1867`.
- **What is imprecise:** A caller probing the export with `dryRun:true` permanently mutates the project JSON (newly minted guids written to disk). Minted-guid stability is a deliberate design (comment `:269-274`), but doing it on a DRY RUN contradicts the advertised contract — an operator cannot preview an export without committing guid assignments.
- **What must change:** Either skip the write-back+`project.Save()` when `dryRun` (mint into the in-memory `EnrichedPlacement` copies only, so files still show the would-be guids), or amend the help/docs to state explicitly: "dryRun skips DB writes but persists newly minted placement guids to the project".

### F170 — placement-add-outdoor / placement-add-dungeon — MED: help lists originX/Y/Z as required but they silently default to 0; `cellNumber` is cast-wrapped, never validated, and never checked for coherence with the origin
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2430-2431` (help args show bare `originX, originY, originZ` among required-style names) vs `:2956-2958`/`:2971-2973` (`?.GetValue<float>() ?? 0f`); `:2955`/`:2970` (`(ushort)(node["cellNumber"]?.GetValue<int>() ?? 1)` — 65536 wraps to 0, negatives wrap high; outdoor default 1, dungeon default 0x100); no validation anywhere that the outdoor `cellNumber` (1..64 per `OutdoorInstancePlacement.cs:14` and `AceDbConnector.cs:35`) matches the cell containing `(originX, originY)`, nor that a dungeon `cellNumber` is ≥ 0x0100 — `EnrichedPlacementValidator.Validate` (`EnrichedPlacementValidator.cs:104-212`) has no cell/origin check.
- **What is imprecise:** Omitting the origin places the object at the LB corner (0,0,0) with success; an outdoor add with `originX: 150, originY: 150` and the defaulted `cellNumber: 1` emits `obj_Cell_Id = 0xLBLB0001` though the origin lies in a different 24 m cell — ACE spawns with an inconsistent cell/origin pair. `cellNumber: 65536` silently becomes 0 (an invalid cell).
- **What must change:** Mark origin args optional-with-default in help (or make them required — pick one), range-check `cellNumber` (outdoor 1..64, dungeon 0x100..0xFFFD, reject wraps), and derive-or-verify the outdoor cell from the origin (`cell = (cellX * 8 + cellY) + 1` with `cellX = (int)(originX/24)/3`-style packing per the ACE convention) — erroring or warning on mismatch, and add the coherence check to `EnrichedPlacementValidator`.

### F171 — prune-tiles — MED: a malformed `olderThan` is silently ignored (success with nothing pruned), and a parseable one is interpreted in LOCAL time against UTC timestamps
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4412-4413` — `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(olderStr) && DateTime.TryParse(olderStr, out var dt)) olderThan = dt;` — a TryParse failure leaves `olderThan` null with no error; `DateTime.TryParse` without `DateTimeStyles.AdjustToUniversal`/`AssumeUniversal` yields `Kind=Unspecified/Local`, compared at `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TileCache.cs:203` against `LastAccessedAt` values written as `DateTime.UtcNow` (`:72`, `:90`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"prune-tiles","olderThan":"yesterday"}` (or any locale-mismatched format) returns `success:true, evicted:0` — indistinguishable from "nothing was old enough". A valid `"2026-06-09 12:00"` in a UTC+10 session prunes a window shifted by 10 hours from what the operator asked for.
- **What must change:** Throw `ArgumentException("olderThan must be an ISO-8601 timestamp")` when the string is non-empty and unparseable; parse with `DateTime.Parse(olderStr, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, DateTimeStyles.AssumeUniversal | DateTimeStyles.AdjustToUniversal)` (documenting UTC), and echo the resolved cutoff in the response.

### F172 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — region-skybox-snapshot / region-day-night-curve — HIGH: DirHeading is lerped LINEARLY while the runtime it claims to oracle uses shortest-arc — the C# port reproduces the pre-fix 180° sun-jump bug
