- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Skybox.cs:464-467` (`LerpSkyTime`: `DirHeading: Lerp(...)` with the comment "Linear lerp on heading (NOT shortest-arc) … per sky.rs::lerp_angle_radians doc"); ground truth `external/holtburger/crates/holtburger-world/src/sky.rs:996-1004` (`lerp_angle_radians` normalizes the delta into (-180, 180] — shortest-arc, fixed 2026-05-20 after the "sun moved really fast and got stuck" 180° wrap bug; the function's OLD doc paragraph at sky.rs:973-977 still claims linear, which is what the C# author read). Downstream: `ProjectToDayGroupUniforms` (`CommandEngine.Skybox.cs:526-554`) turns the bad heading into a wrong `sunPosition` unit vector.
- **What is imprecise:** Whenever two consecutive SkyTimeOfDay keyframes straddle the 0°/360° heading wrap (dusk→midnight→dawn in retail Dereth DayGroups), the snapshot's `rawSkyState.dirHeading` and `uniforms.sunPosition` sweep the LONG way around — up to ~180° off the value the Rust/JS runtime actually renders. The command's stated contract ("must agree with the JS-side mapping within 1e-4", file header :33-35) is violated for every sampled game-time inside such an interval; the 24/24 validator pass simply never sampled inside one. The doc-comment in the C# file cites the stale half of sky.rs's own doc.
- **What must change:** Port the FIXED `lerp_angle_radians`: in `LerpSkyTime`, compute `delta = bHeading - aHeading; if (delta > 180f) delta -= 360f; else if (delta < -180f) delta += 360f; DirHeading = aHeading + delta * u;` (degrees, period 360 — `DirPitch` stays plain linear). Update the comment to cite sky.rs:996-1004, and delete/correct the stale "NOT shortest-arc" claim. Re-run `validate_skybox.cjs` with samples chosen inside a wrap-straddling keyframe interval.

### F173 — pvs-visibility-snapshot — MED: a nonexistent cellId returns `success:true` with zeroed sets (failure signaled only inside the `source` prose), and the shared scalar parser widens short cell hex into a bogus LB id
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.CellPortalGraph.cs:450-461` (not-found path returns a zero-count `PvsSnapshotResult` whose only failure indicator is `Source: "… not found in …"`); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4537-4550` hardcodes `success = true`. Parsing: `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4528-4529` feeds `cellId` through `ParseLbIdScalar` (`:4558-4571`), whose `v <<= 16` widening (F20) fires for any ≤4-hex-digit string — so a cell-low word like `"0x0114"` becomes `0x01140000` (an LB-high with cell suffix 0x0000, never a valid EnvCell), and a negative JSON number wraps through `(uint)entry.GetValue<long>()`.
- **What is imprecise:** A typo'd or short-form cellId is indistinguishable from a legitimately isolated cell except by reading the `source` string: the response carries `success:true, liveVisibleCount:0, datVisibleCount:0` (well — liveVisibleCount is 0 here because the root isn't counted on this path, while the found path counts the root, a further shape inconsistency). F20's aside that pvs "fails loudly" on these inputs is wrong — it fails silently with success:true. Note: F20's proposed value-based widening fix is correct for `cell-portal-graph-sweep` but must NOT be applied to the pvs `cellId` argument, where low-word values are inherently ambiguous.
- **What must change:** In `CmdPvsVisibilitySnapshot`, return `success:false` plus an explicit `error`/`found:false` field when the root cell is absent (keep cellHex/cellId for context). For `cellId` specifically, bypass the ≤4-digit `<<16` widening: require a full 32-bit id and throw `ArgumentException("cellId must be a full cell id like 0xA9B40114")` for hex strings with ≤4 digits and for any value whose low word is 0x0000/0xFFFE/0xFFFF; reject negative numerics.

### F174 — region-export-json — MED: `parts`-filtered exports keep the UNFILTERED partsMask (and an emptied terrainInfo), so the emitted document fails region-import-json's own validation
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Region.cs:250` (`ApplyPartsFilter` runs after `RegionToJsonDoc`, which copied `PartsMask` verbatim at `:444`), `:810-823` (filter nulls SkyInfo/SoundInfo/SceneInfo/RegionMisc and replaces TerrainInfo with an EMPTY `RegionTerrainDescJson` without touching `doc.PartsMask`); validator `:746-749` (`CheckPart` — "partsMask has 0x10 set but skyInfo is missing") and `:762-763` (`terrainInfo.terrainTypes must not be empty`). Response: `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4694-4710` echoes the pre-filter `partsMask` next to post-filter `counts`.
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"region-export-json","out":"x.json","parts":"sky"}` writes a document that `region-import-json` rejects with 3-4 partsMask/terrainTypes problems — the export pipeline emits files its own import declares invalid, and nothing in the response or help warns that `parts` produces a non-importable partial. The response also pairs a pre-filter `partsMask` with post-filter `counts` (e.g. `partsMask:"0x0000021F"` with `soundStbs:0`).
- **What must change:** In `ApplyPartsFilter`, clear the corresponding `doc.PartsMask` bits (`HasSkyInfo`/`HasSoundInfo`/`HasSceneInfo`/`HasRegionMisc`) when a section is removed, and either leave `terrainInfo` intact (it has no mask bit and is structurally required) or add a `"partial": true` marker that region-import-json rejects with a single clear message ("document is a parts-filtered partial export — re-export without 'parts' to import"). Make the response's `partsMask` reflect the emitted document.

### F175 — region-import-json — MED: the document's `id` field is silently ignored (hardcoded to 0x13000000), and the validation pre-probe reports only the FIRST hex/enum failure despite claiming "the first few"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Region.cs:582-583` (`JsonDocToRegion`: `Id = RegionFileId` — `doc.Id` is never read or validated), `:765-767` (`try { _ = JsonDocToRegionHexProbe(doc); } catch (Exception ex) { problems.Add(...); }` — one catch, one problem, contradicting the `:772` doc-comment "surface the first few failures with paths"; the probe's exception message also carries no field path).
- **What is imprecise:** A document with `"id": "0x13000001"` (or any other value) imports cleanly and stages over Region 0x13000000 with no warning — the one field that names what you're importing has no effect. A document with 5 bad color strings reports only the first, with a message like "Color '#xyz' must be #AARRGGBB" and no JSON path to find it.
- **What must change:** In `ValidateRegionJsonDoc`, add `if (!string.Equals(doc.Id?.Trim(), "0x13000000", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)) problems.Add($"id must be 0x13000000 (got '{doc.Id}') — only the Dereth region record is supported.")`. Restructure `JsonDocToRegionHexProbe` to try/catch per section (or per field group) appending `$"{jsonPath}: {ex.Message}"` for each failure, or fix the comment to say "first failure only".

### F176 — region-import-json / RegionRoundTripTests — MED: the "byte-identical pack parity" claim is verified only against DRW's OWN Pack output, never against the raw DAT record bytes, and all four tests silently pass (not skip) when the base DAT is absent
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Tests/RegionRoundTripTests.cs:39-60` (`JsonRoundTrip_PacksByteIdentical_ToDirectLoad` compares `PackRegion(loaded)` vs `PackRegion(jsonRoundTripped)`) and `:62-76` (self-parity of pack→unpack→pack) — neither test fetches the raw 0x13000000 record bytes from `client_portal.dat` for comparison, so a DRW Unpack/Pack infidelity (dropped field, normalized padding) is invisible to both while still corrupting exports; import gate `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Region.cs:294-305` has the same scope (self-parity only). Skips: `:41`, `:65`, `:81`, `:105` are bare `return` statements — xUnit reports PASSED, so a CI box without `~/ac_base_dats` shows green with zero coverage (the file's own header says "tests skip (not fail)" but nothing marks them skipped).
- **What is imprecise:** The checklist-level claim "byte-identical pack parity locked by RegionRoundTripTests" holds only modulo DRW wire fidelity, which is asserted nowhere against ground truth; and the lock evaporates silently on any box without the retail DAT.
- **What must change:** Add a test that reads the raw record payload for 0x13000000 (DRW exposes the BTree file entry + reader; or reuse the `EnumerateEnvCellIdsInRange`-style reflection from CommandEngine.CellPortalGraph.cs) and asserts `PackRegion(LoadRetailRegion(dat))` equals those bytes (modulo any documented container framing). Convert the four `if (datPath == null) return;` guards to `Assert.Skip`/`SkipException` (xunit 2.4: `Skip.If` via Xunit.SkippableFact) so absence is reported as SKIPPED.

### F177 — region-diff — LOW: `truncated` is true whenever the row count lands exactly on maxRows, and supplying both otherDat and otherJson silently prefers otherJson
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Region.cs:362` (`Truncated: rows.Count >= maxRows` — a diff that COMPLETED with exactly maxRows rows is reported truncated); `:337-349` (the `otherJson` branch runs first; a simultaneously supplied `otherDat` is ignored with no note).

### F178 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — remove-building — HIGH: the staged-shell match is nearest-same-model with NO distance threshold — a second remove of the same index (or a moved shell) silently deletes a DIFFERENT static; lbX/lbY are also unvalidated (F162/F9 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Transplant.cs:290-302` (`RemoveBuilding`: loops all staged statics with `Id == building.ModelId`, keeps the minimum-distance one, and removes it — `bestDist` has no epsilon gate; only the bestIdx<0 "was it already removed?" case errors, which requires NO same-model static to remain anywhere in the LB); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5036` uses `U(node,"lbX"), U(node,"lbY")` instead of the validated `Lb(node)` helper, so lbX=300 wraps through `LbKey` (`CommandEngine.cs:10241`) into the wrong landblock.
- **What is imprecise:** The DAT's `lbi.Buildings[buildingIndex]` survives until export, so re-issuing `{"command":"remove-building","lbX":…,"buildingIndex":0}` after a successful removal finds the same BuildingInfo, finds no shell at its origin, and removes the nearest OTHER static sharing the model id — e.g. the identical cottage across the street (Holtburg has duplicate building models) — reporting `success:true` with only `matchDistance` (potentially tens of meters) as a clue. Same misfire if the shell was legitimately relocated with `move-object`. Export-side NumCells/EnvCell corruption when the removal sticks is the existing **F57** (CRITICAL) — referenced, not re-filed.
- **What must change:** After the loop, require `bestDist <= 0.25f` (shells are mirrored at exactly the BuildingInfo origin) and otherwise throw `InvalidOperationException($"No staged shell for building {buildingIndex} (0x{ModelId:X8}) at its DAT origin (nearest same-model static is {bestDist:F1}m away) — already removed or moved?")`. Replace the two `U()` calls at JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5036 with `var (lbX, lbY) = Lb(node);`.

### F179 — remove-object / remove-building — MED: object mutations outside `transact` never invalidate the tile cache (F112/F143/F157 class, new sites)

### F180 — regenerate-dirty-tiles — MED: failed LBs are dropped from the dirty index anyway, `success:true` regardless of errors, and dirty world/region tiles are not rebuilt despite help's "Rebuild every tile flagged dirty"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TileGenerator.cs:164-183` (`RegenerateDirty`: per-LB try/catch collects errors, then `_cache.ClearDirty()` at `:180` unconditionally clears the WHOLE `DirtyLbs` index — including LBs whose generation just threw; only LB tiles are iterated, never the `world`/`region/*` entries whose per-tile `Dirty` flags `MarkLbDirty` set at `TileCache.cs:109-118`); wrapper `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4386-4393` returns `success = true` always (errors list also truncated to 10, though `errorCount` is honest); help `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2461` says "Rebuild every tile flagged dirty … and clear dirty bits".
- **What is imprecise:** After a partial failure, `list-dirty-tiles` reports clean, so the failed LBs can never be retried by this command (the stale-tile risk is softened — their per-tile `Dirty` flag stays set so `get-tile` lazily regenerates — but the dirty INDEX lies). World/region composites flagged dirty are left to lazy regen, contradicting the help text's "every tile". `success:true` with `errorCount:3` is the F81-class success-formula pattern.
- **What must change:** Replace `ClearDirty()` with `ClearDirtyForLbs(succeededLbs)` (the helper already exists at `TileCache.cs:148-152`). Set `success = errors.Count == 0`. Either also regenerate dirty `world`/`region/*` tiles in the same pass or amend the help text to "Rebuild every LANDBLOCK tile flagged dirty (world/region composites regenerate lazily on next get-tile)".
