### F184 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — road — HIGH: road edits never sync the duplicated landblock-edge vertices, so roads break at every LB seam
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:575-608` (`DrawRoad` maps each waypoint through `_terrainService.WorldToVertex` and writes exactly ONE `(lbId, vIndex)` per waypoint); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:451-470` (`WorldToVertex` returns a single vertex; a boundary coordinate x=192·k resolves to the HIGHER landblock's column 0); contrast `TerrainAlgorithms.cs:486-514` (`AddEdgeNeighbors`), which every brush op (`GetAffectedVertices:73`) calls precisely because edge vertices are stored duplicated in both adjacent LBs' 9×9 grids.
- **What is imprecise:** AC terrain stores seam vertices twice (column/row 8 of LB N == column/row 0 of LB N+1). A road crossing an LB boundary sets the road byte on only one of the two copies, leaving the neighbor's duplicate at road=0. The exported DATs then disagree across the seam (the client renders each LB from its own data → road gap/seam artifact at every border crossing), and render-preview's unified grid shows or hides the seam segment depending on tiling overwrite order. Roads commonly span many LBs, so this hits the primary use case. Additionally, `WorldToVertex:455` rejects `lbX/lbY >= MapSize(254)`, so the world's last row/column is unreachable by road — same defect class as F122 (new site).
- **What must change:** In `DrawRoad`, after resolving `(lbId, vIndex)` for a waypoint, decompose to `(lbX, lbY, localVX=vIndex/9, localVY=vIndex%9)` and apply the same neighbor-sync `AddEdgeNeighbors` performs (edge → 1 mirror write, corner → 3 mirror writes) into `batchChanges`, writing the same `Road` value to each duplicate. For the 254 exclusion, fix `WorldToVertex`'s bound per F122's prescription (the constant excludes the last valid LB index).

### F185 — road — MED: `value` accepts 0..255 but only 2 bits survive DAT export — value 4 silently exports as road=0 (F155 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1429` (`ByteInRange(node, "value")` — max 255); in-session storage keeps the full byte (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/TerrainDocument.cs:79-100`); export copies it into DRW at `TerrainDocument.cs:544-548`, where `external/DatReaderWriter/DatReaderWriter/Types/TerrainInfo.cs:22-25` masks the setter with `value & 0x3`. Docs: `docs/agent_api_reference.md` road section ("`value` … 1+ = road").
- **What is imprecise:** F155's rationale applies (accepted range ≫ meaningful range, silent divergence): `{"command":"road","value":4}` reports success, `get-height` reads back road=4 in-session, but the exported DAT contains road=0 — the road vanishes on export with no warning. Values 4..255 are all silently truncated mod 4.
- **What must change:** Pass `max: 3` to `ByteInRange` at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1429` (error: "'value' must be 0..3") and correct the api-ref row to document the 2-bit road field.

### F186 — road — MED: world coordinates in (−192, 0) truncate to landblock 0 instead of being rejected — a road endpoint at x=−50 silently edits LB 0x0000
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:452-455` (`int lbX = (int)(worldX / LandblockLength)` truncates toward zero, so worldX in (−192, 0) yields lbX=0 and the `lbX < 0` guard never fires; `localX` goes negative and `cellX = Round(localX/24)` clamps to 0); reached from `DrawRoad` (`CommandEngine.cs:587-590`) for every waypoint `GenerateGridPath` (`TerrainAlgorithms.cs:408-442`) emits at negative grid coords — `CmdRoad`'s `F()` only checks finiteness, not sign.
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring unvalidated-coordinate class (F156/F162 rationale), new mechanism: truncation instead of ushort wrap. `{"command":"road","x1":-50,"y1":100,"x2":-10,"y2":100}` reports success and stamps road vertices onto LB 0x0000's column 0 — terrain the caller never addressed.
- **What must change:** In `WorldToVertex`, reject before truncating: `if (worldX < 0 || worldY < 0) return null;` (or use `Math.Floor` and let the existing `< 0` guard work). Same fix protects `get-height`, which shares this helper.

### F187 — road — MED: the API reference claims "terrain-aware routing" — the path is a terrain-blind greedy grid march — and the documented success shape omits the idempotent-redraw failure mode
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:467` ("The path follows terrain-aware routing."); actual algorithm `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:408-442` (`GenerateGridPath`: diagonal-then-straight march on the 24wu grid; the `heightLookup` parameter only fills waypoint Z, which `DrawRoad` never reads); success semantics `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1431` (`success = r.VerticesModified > 0`).
- **What is imprecise:** No slope/water/terrain signal influences the route — "terrain-aware" is untrue. Separately, re-running the identical road command (all vertices already at `value`) returns `success:false` with `verticesModified:0` and NO error field; the api-ref documents only `success:true` responses, so a JSON caller reads a successful no-op as a failure.
- **What must change:** Reword the api-ref to "grid-aligned greedy path (diagonal-first); terrain is not consulted", and document that `success` is false when zero vertices changed (or add a `note`/`alreadyApplied` field distinguishing no-op from failure, matching the TerrainEditResult convention documented at CommandResults.cs:173-176).

### F188 — set-height — MED: docs/help advertise only the deprecated `height` arg and a `targetHeight` response field that does not exist
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:379-404` (request table lists `height` as required; response example contains `"targetHeight": 128`); help args `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2340` (`"x, y, radius, height"`); actual contract `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1392-1406` — canonical arg is `heightIndex` (with `height` as a documented-in-code deprecated alias), and the response field is `heightIndex`.
- **What is imprecise:** A caller following the docs gets a response without the documented `targetHeight` key (validators keying on it break), and nothing outside the source mentions `heightIndex` — the name the error message uses ("Missing 'heightIndex' field"), so the missing-arg error references a field the docs never define. (Radius semantics and the row-254 exclusion are F123/F122 — same code path, already filed.)
- **What must change:** Fix the api-ref response example to `"heightIndex"`, change the request table to `heightIndex` (byte, required) with `height` noted as deprecated alias, and update the help args string to `"x, y, radius, heightIndex"`.

### F189 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — rotate-object — HIGH: an all-zero quaternion normalizes to NaN, corrupts the stored orientation, and THEN the response serializer throws — corrupt-then-error
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3657-3670` (`ParseQuaternion` checks each component is finite but never rejects magnitude≈0; `Quaternion.Normalize(0,0,0,0)` divides by zero → all-NaN); applied at `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1377-1381` (`obj.Orientation = newOrientation; lbDoc.UpdateStaticObject(...)` — persisted); the response then serializes `FmtQ` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3726-3728`) whose `Math.Round(NaN)` System.Text.Json refuses to write, so the command surfaces as an error AFTER the document was mutated.
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"rotate-object","lbX":7,"lbY":8,"index":0,"qw":0,"qx":0,"qy":0,"qz":0}` leaves the object with a NaN orientation in the landblock document (exports as NaN bytes) while the caller sees a serialization error suggesting nothing happened. Same shared parser feeds `add-object` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1873`), where identity is substituted only when NO q-field is present.
- **What must change:** In `ParseQuaternion`, after the finite check add `if (qw*qw+qx*qx+qy*qy+qz*qz < 1e-12f) throw new ArgumentException("Quaternion magnitude is zero — provide a non-zero quaternion.");` before normalizing.

### F190 — render-dungeon — MED: lbX/lbY are unvalidated — values > 255 wrap through the ushort LbKey pack and render the WRONG landblock (F9/F111/F162 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1132` (`U(node,"lbX"), U(node,"lbY")` — raw uint, no range check, unlike the `Lb()` helper at `:3632-3637` used by sibling commands); packed at `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:11403` (`LbKey(lbX, lbY)` → `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)` at `:10241`).
- **What is imprecise:** Prior-finding rationale applies verbatim: `lbX:300` silently renders LB 0x2C00-relative keys; `lbY:300` bleeds into the lbX byte. The response's `landblock` field reports the wrapped key as if it were the request.
- **What must change:** Use `Lb(node)` in `CmdRenderDungeon` (and pass through its 0..254 errors), matching `describe-floor`'s neighbors.

### F191 — render-dungeon — MED: an out-of-range `floor` returns success:true with floorIndex:0, zeroed Z range, and a blank PNG — indistinguishable from a real floor-0 render
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/DungeonRenderer.cs:73` (`if (fi < 0 || fi >= orderedBands.Count) return EmptyPng(...)` — returns BEFORE setting `output.FloorIndexRendered`, which defaults to 0 per `:39-46`; same for the `:61` no-bands and `:94` no-footprint-overlap paths); surfaced at `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1139-1151` (`success = true` hardcoded; `floorIndex = r.FloorIndexRendered < 0 ? null : r.FloorIndexRendered` → reports 0).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"render-dungeon","lbX":0,"lbY":176,"floor":99}` answers `success:true, floorIndex:0, floorCount:4, cellsRendered:0` plus a background-only PNG. A validation harness checking floors by index reads the blank as "floor 0 rendered fine". `cellsRendered:0` is the only tell, and nothing marks it as an error.
- **What must change:** In `DungeonRenderer.Render`, set `output.FloorIndexRendered = fi` (or −2/sentinel) before the early return, and in `CmdRenderDungeon` either throw `ArgumentException($"floor {floor} out of range — {r.FloorCount} floors")` when `floor >= FloorCount`, or return `success:false` with an explicit error for all `CellsRendered == 0` early-return paths.

### F192 — render-dungeon — LOW: `resolution` is unbounded above — render-preview clamps to 8192, render-dungeon allocates resolution² RGBA for any requested value
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1134` (no check) → `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:11400-11426` (no check) → `DungeonRenderer.cs:123` (`Math.Max(64, input.Resolution)` floors only); contrast `CommandEngine.cs:713-714` (render-preview rejects outside [64, 8192]).
- **What is imprecise:** `resolution: 100000` attempts a ~37 GB SKBitmap; the sibling command's documented guard establishes the intended bound.
- **What must change:** Apply the same `[64, 8192]` validation (and error message style) in `CmdRenderDungeon` or `CommandEngine.RenderDungeon`.

### F193 — render-preview — MED: `objectCount` silently includes spawn-gazetteer glyphs, and `cliffCount` is 0 whenever overlay:false even though cliffs exist
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/RenderPreviewRenderer.cs:805` (spawn glyphs appended to the same `glyphs` list as placed objects) and `:905` (`output.RenderedObjectCount = glyphs.Count`), returned as `objectCount` (`CommandEngine.cs:840-846`, `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:1282`); cliff counting lives entirely inside `if (input.Overlay && drawTerrain)` (`RenderPreviewRenderer.cs:945-985`), so `overlay:false` returns `cliffCount:0`.
- **What is imprecise:** On a spawn-gazetteer-loaded project, `objectCount` exceeds the LB's placed static count by the number of outdoor spawns — an agent diffing it against `list-objects` totals sees phantom objects. And `cliffCount:0` under `overlay:false` reads as "no cliffs" when it means "not measured"; the field name promises a world measurement, not an overlay statistic.
- **What must change:** Either report placed-object and spawn-glyph counts separately (add `spawnGlyphCount`, keep `objectCount` = placed only — count them before `:805` appends), or rename/document the field as renderedGlyphCount. For cliffs, either always run the edge scan (drawing gated on Overlay, counting not) or return `cliffCount: null` when `overlay:false`.

### F194 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — scan-building-placements — HIGH: progress and summary lines print to STDOUT on every invocation, corrupting the --stdin JSON channel (F75/F89/F107/F109/F120 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3353-3354` (`Console.WriteLine($"[ScanBuildings] ...{x+1}/255 rows scanned...")` — 5 lines per run) and `:3358-3359` (completion + output-path lines).
- **What is imprecise:** Prior-finding rationale applies: 7 non-JSON lines interleave with the one-object-per-line stdout protocol on every scan.
- **What must change:** Switch all three sites to `Console.Error.WriteLine` (the convention OntologyService already follows).

### F195 — scan-building-placements — MED: JSONL numbers are written with current-culture formatting — on a comma-decimal locale the output file is invalid JSON
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:3336-3341` (`writer.Write(Math.Round(worldX, 1))` etc. — `TextWriter.Write(double)` uses `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture`); no invariant-culture initialization exists anywhere in WorldBuilder.Terminal startup (verified: no `DefaultThreadCurrentCulture` assignment in Program.cs).
- **What is imprecise:** Under e.g. `de_DE.UTF-8`, `worldX` 1234.5 serializes as `"worldX":1234,5` — every consumer of building_placements.jsonl gets parse failures, while the command reports success with full counts.
- **What must change:** Format explicitly: `writer.Write(Math.Round(worldX, 1).ToString(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture))` for the three world coords (the integral fields are culture-safe), or build each line with `JsonSerializer.Serialize`.

### F196 — scan-ontology — MED: per-id classification failures are tracked in report.FailedEntries but DROPPED from the JSON response — success:true with no error count (F108/F121 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Services/OntologyService.cs:95-97` (`RecordFailure` populates `report.FailedEntries` for tryget-false, exceptions, and BoundsFailed ids; stderr-only summary at `:192-194`); response projection `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2186-2192` serializes totals/categories/scales only.
- **What is imprecise:** Prior-finding rationale applies: a scan where hundreds of Setups fail to classify (DAT corruption, reader fork issues) is indistinguishable in the JSON channel from a clean scan; `totalEntries < totalSetups + totalGfxObjs` is the only implicit signal and nothing explains the gap. The aggregate `ClassifiedAs*` fields the report computes (`OntologyService.cs:185-189`) are likewise dropped.
- **What must change:** Add `failedCount = rpt.FailedEntries.Count` (and optionally the first N `(id, reason)` pairs) to the `CmdScanOntology` response object.

### F197 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — scene-edit — HIGH: dry-run (apply:false) mutates the SHARED staged Scene instance — edits silently become permanent for any previously staged scene
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Scene.cs:187-221` (fields are applied directly to `scene.Objects[index]` BEFORE the `if (apply)` gate at `:224-233`); `LoadScene:271-274` returns the staged document's cached object; `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/PortalDatDocument.cs:79-83` (`TryGetEntry` hands back the SAME instance `SetEntry:52` cached) and `:172-185` (`SyncCacheToData` re-packs every cached object into the persisted/exported bytes on save and export).
- **What is imprecise:** After one `scene-edit … apply:true` (scene now staged), every subsequent `scene-edit` on that scene with `apply:false` — advertised as "Dry-run … nothing staged" in the response note (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:4869-4871`) — mutates the live cached Scene; the next project save or `export` packs the mutation into the DATs. Compounding: an unknown-field error mid-loop (`:218`) leaves the earlier fields of the same request already applied to that shared instance. (Fresh-DAT loads are safe — the project reader uses FileCachingStrategy.Never — so the leak is specifically via the staged-document path.)
- **What must change:** In `SceneEdit`, deep-copy the scene before mutating (pack/unpack via DatBinWriter/DatBinReader, or clone the ObjectDesc list) and only `SetEntry` the copy when `apply:true`; alternatively validate ALL fields first, then apply, and have `PortalDatDocument.TryGetEntry` return a defensive copy.

### F198 — scene-diff — LOW: `truncated` reports true when the diff count lands exactly on maxRows even if nothing was cut (F177 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Scene.cs:135` (`Truncated: rows.Count >= maxRows`); `DiffJsonNodes` (`CommandEngine.Region.cs:827-858`) stops at the cap without signaling whether more diffs remained.
- **What is imprecise:** F177's rationale applies verbatim to this second call site.
- **What must change:** Same fix as F177: have `DiffJsonNodes` report overflow (e.g. return a bool or count one past the cap) and set `Truncated` only when rows were actually dropped.

### F199 — serve-render-gallery — MED: the path-containment check lacks a trailing separator — sibling directories sharing the served root's name prefix are servable
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.RenderGallery.cs:360-364` (`Path.GetFullPath(Path.Combine(rootFull, rel))` then `resolved.StartsWith(rootFull, StringComparison.Ordinal)`); `rootFull` (`:308`) carries no trailing separator.
- **What is imprecise:** Serving `/tmp/gallery` while `/tmp/gallery2` (or `/tmp/gallery-private`) exists: any request whose `..`-segments survive into `rel` resolves to `/tmp/gallery2/…`, which passes `StartsWith("/tmp/gallery")` and is served with 200. The guard exists precisely to stop traversal (its own comment cites `..%2F`), but the comparison is the classic prefix-without-separator bug — the 403 branch protects only paths that don't share the name prefix.
- **What must change:** Compare against the separator-terminated root: `var rootWithSep = rootFull.EndsWith(Path.DirectorySeparatorChar) ? rootFull : rootFull + Path.DirectorySeparatorChar; if (!resolved.StartsWith(rootWithSep, StringComparison.Ordinal) && !string.Equals(resolved, rootFull, StringComparison.Ordinal)) { 403 }`.

### F200 — render-preview / scan-ontology / scan-building-placements / scene-edit — MED: no api-ref section for the first three (F116/F128/F154/F161/F182 class); scene-edit's help `fields` list names keys the command rejects
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` has no request/response section for `render-preview` (referenced in prose at `:1311`, `:1494`, `:2017` only), `scan-ontology`, or `scan-building-placements`; help entry `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2477` advertises scene-edit fields `…/displace/scale/rotation/slope/…` while the accepted keys (`CommandEngine.Scene.cs:191-218`) are `displaceX`/`displaceY`, `minScale`/`maxScale`, `maxRotation`, `minSlope`/`maxSlope` — sending the advertised `"rotation"` or `"scale"` throws `Unknown field`.
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring docs-gap class for the three undocumented commands (render-preview is the workhorse render primitive — agents must reverse-engineer includePng/useSprites/overlay defaults from help one-liners). The scene-edit help string is actively wrong, not merely terse: it names keys that error.
- **What must change:** Add api-ref sections for the three commands (args incl. defaults — render-preview: radius 0, resolution 1024, overlay true, includePng true, useSprites false; scan-ontology: scanGfxObjs true; scan-building-placements: outputPath default `<projectDir>/../building_placements.jsonl`). Fix the help string at `:2477` to the exact key list from `CommandEngine.Scene.cs:179`.

### F201 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — set-landblock-heightmap / set-landblock-terrain — HIGH: JSON-array values are truncating-cast to byte — 300 silently becomes 44, −1 becomes 255, null becomes 0
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3690` (`result[i] = (byte)(arr[i]?.GetValue<int>() ?? 0);` inside `ParseByteArrayField`), reached from `CmdSetLandblockHeightmap` (`:2874`) and `CmdSetLandblockTerrain` (`:3307`).
- **What is imprecise:** When `heights`/`types` is a JSON array, each element is read as `int` and cast to `byte` with no range check: 256→0, 300→44, −1→255, and a `null` element silently becomes 0 — the wrong values are then written into all 81 vertices (and edge-synced into up to 8 neighbor LBs) under `success:true`. The CSV-string path for the SAME field (`ParseCsvByteArray`, `:3715`) correctly rejects out-of-range segments via `byte.TryParse` with a positioned error — the two encodings of one argument disagree.
- **What must change:** In the JSON-array branch, validate each element: read `arr[i]?.GetValue<int>()`, throw `ArgumentException($"'{fieldName}[{i}]' must be 0..255; got {v}")` when null or outside 0..255 (mirror `ByteInRange`), then cast.

### F202 — set-landblock-terrain — MED: terrain type values 32..255 are accepted and stored silently (F155 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:4295-4318` (`SetLandblockTerrain` — only `types.Length != 81` is checked; per-value range never validated before `currentData[i] with { Type = types[i] }`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same defect class as F155 (paint): valid AC terrain type indices are 0..31, but any byte (post-F201 wrap, any int) is written per-vertex with `success:true`. Bulk writes make this worse than paint — one call can poison all 81 vertices plus seam-synced neighbors.
- **What must change:** Apply the same gate prescribed by F155: reject (or explicitly flag in the response) `types[i] > 31` before building `newEntries`, with the offending index in the error message.

### F203 — snap-portal — MED: lbX/lbY are unvalidated — values > 255 wrap through the ushort LbKey pack and target the WRONG landblock's dungeon (F9/F111/F162/F190 class, new site)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2307` (`uint lbX = U(node, "lbX"), lbY = U(node, "lbY");` — `U` has no bounds check) → `CommandEngine.cs:2152` (`ushort lbKey = LbKey(lbX, lbY);`, truncating `(ushort)((lbX << 8) | lbY)` at `:10241`).
- **What is imprecise:** Every other LB-addressed command in this file goes through `Lb()` (`:3632`, rejects > 254); snap-portal alone uses raw `U()`, so `lbX:300, lbY:5` silently snaps a cell into dungeon 0x2C05.
- **What must change:** Replace the two `U()` calls with `var (lbX, lbY) = Lb(node);`.

### F204 — snap-portal — MED: targetCellNumber/targetPortalPolyId/sourceEnvId are ALWAYS parsed as hex — a decimal string like "1234" silently means 0x1234 — and an uppercase "0X" prefix or JSON-number input throws an undirected exception
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2308-2313` — `ushort.Parse(str.Replace("0x", ""), NumberStyles.HexNumber)` for all three fields; help entry `:2372` (also: `sourceCellStruct` is optional with default 0 at `:2314` but not marked `?` in the help args string, unlike every other optional arg).
- **What is imprecise:** (1) `"targetCellNumber":"1234"` is read as 0x1234 = 4660 — hex/decimal confusion with no prefix requirement, unlike `ParseUintField`/`Hex32` which treat unprefixed strings as decimal. (2) `Replace("0x","")` is case-sensitive: `"0X0100"` reaches the hex parser with the literal `X` and throws a bare `FormatException`. (3) Passing a JSON number (natural for agents) throws `InvalidOperationException` from `GetValue<string>()` with no field name.
- **What must change:** Parse the three fields with the `Hex32` helper semantics narrowed to ushort (require `0x` prefix for hex, decimal otherwise, accept both JSON string and number, name the field in every error), and append `?` to `sourceCellStruct` in the help args at `:2372`.

### F205 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — snap-portal — HIGH: the snapped cell is created with ZERO surfaces — invisible in-game by the project's own validator — behind a dead-code block whose comment claims surfaces "will be populated from the environment"
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:2201-2205` (`var surfaces = new List<ushort>(); if (sourceEnv.Cells…ContainsKey(…)) { /* comment only, empty body */ }`) → `DungeonDocument.AddCell` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DungeonDocument.cs:313-326`) stores the empty list; export writes it verbatim (`:498`); validation flags exactly this: "has no surfaces assigned (will be invisible in-game)" (`:670-674`).
- **What is imprecise:** Nothing anywhere populates surfaces from the Environment (an Environment(0x0D) defines geometry, not surface lists), so every snap-portal invocation produces a cell that the same codebase's `validate-dungeon` marks broken — yet the response reports plain success with origin/orientation/portalCount and no warning.
- **What must change:** Either copy a real surface list (e.g. default to the target cell's `Surfaces` so the new cell inherits the neighboring material set, with an optional `surfaces` arg override) or, at minimum, delete the dead block and add a `warning: "cell created with 0 surfaces — assign surfaces before export or it will be invisible in-game"` field to the response.

### F206 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — spell-copy — HIGH: auto-allocated newId ignores ACE-DB ids — the subsequent UPSERT can silently OVERWRITE an existing DB-only spell
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Spell.cs:173-182` (`AllocateNextSpellId` = max(overlay ids, DAT SpellTable ids) + 1; the connected ACE DB is never consulted) → `:146` saves via `AceDbConnector.SaveSpellAsync`, whose `ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.cs:562`) replaces every column of a colliding row.
- **What is imprecise:** Help/api-ref say "auto-allocates max+1". On a server whose `spell` table contains custom rows above the DAT/overlay max (the normal case for a modded ACE world), `spell-copy` without `newId` picks an id already taken in the DB and the UPSERT destroys that spell's 64 columns — reported as `success:true, savedToDb:true` with no hint of the collision.
- **What must change:** When ace-db is configured, include `SELECT MAX(id) FROM spell` in the allocation max; independently, before UPSERTing an AUTO-allocated id, probe `GetSpellAsync(resolvedNewId)` and fail (or re-allocate) on a hit. Explicit `newId` may keep overwrite-by-design semantics, but then the response should distinguish inserted-vs-replaced.

### F207 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — spell-get / spell-copy — HIGH: no DAT fallback — every spell id that spell-list returns by DEFAULT (source "dat") fails "not found", and spell-copy's own comment claims a dat fallback that does not exist
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Spell.cs:78-97` (`SpellGetAsync`: overlay → ace-db → throw `"Spell 0x… not found in project overlay or ACE DB."`); `:131` (comment "Source: prefer overlay, fall back to db, fall back to dat-side." — no dat branch follows); contrast `SpellListAsync` `:60-73`, which reads the DAT `SpellTable 0x0E00000E` as its default source.
- **What is imprecise:** The advertised workflow (list → get/copy) is broken end to end for a project without a configured ACE DB: spell-list shows thousands of retail spells, then spell-get/spell-copy of any of those ids errors. Wrong results in the most common path; the in-code comment additionally documents behavior that was never implemented.
- **What must change:** Add a third fallback in `SpellGetAsync`: read `SpellTable.Spells[id]` and map the DAT `SpellBase` fields onto a `SpellRecord` (source string `"dat"` in the response; fields the DAT cannot supply stay null). If a mapping is deliberately out of scope, the spell-list rows from the dat source must be marked non-retrievable and the spell-get error must say "use source db / create an overlay first" — and delete the false comment at `:131`.

### F208 — spell-delete — MED: response always claims `removedFromOverlay: true` (even when no overlay entry existed), and a MySqlException is swallowed into `deletedFromDb: false`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Spell.cs:152-171` — `doc.Remove(id)` discards the `Dictionary.Remove` result (`SpellDbDocument.cs:57-62` no-ops silently) yet `:170` returns hardcoded `true`; the `catch (MySqlException) { deletedFromDb = false; }` at `:165-167` makes a dead DB indistinguishable from "row didn't exist" (F108/F121 swallowed-error class).
- **What is imprecise:** `spell-delete` of a nonexistent id returns `{"success":true,"removedFromOverlay":true,"deletedFromDb":false}` — both flags can lie about what happened.
- **What must change:** Change `SpellDbDocument.Remove` to return the bool from `_data.Spells.Remove(id)` and plumb it into `RemovedFromOverlay`; surface DB failures as an `error`/`dbError` field (or rethrow) instead of folding them into `false`.

### F209 — spell-get / spell-save / spell-copy — MED: AceDbConnector swallows every MySqlException — a dead or misconfigured DB masquerades as "spell not found" / `savedToDb:false` under `success:true`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.cs:487-489` (`GetSpellAsync` catch → null) and `:729-731` (`SaveSpellAsync` catch → false); consumed at `CommandEngine.Spell.cs:91-96`, `:122`, `:146`.
- **What is imprecise:** Connection refused, auth failure, missing table, and genuinely-absent row all collapse to the same outputs: spell-get throws "not found in project overlay or ACE DB" (misleading error), spell-save/spell-copy return `success:true, savedToDb:false` (silent write failure — the caller believes the overlay-only state is intentional). Additionally `SaveSpellAsync` returns `ExecuteNonQueryAsync > 0`, and MySQL reports 0 affected rows for an UPSERT whose values are identical — so an idempotent re-save reports `savedToDb:false` even though the row is present and correct.
- **What must change:** Remove the blanket catches (or catch, log, and rethrow a typed exception) so DB infrastructure failures surface as command errors distinct from "row absent"; treat affected-rows 0 from the UPSERT as success (row exists with identical values) or use `conn.Open` success as the savedToDb criterion plus an explicit verify read.

### F210 — spell-list — LOW: any `source` other than "db" is silently treated as "dat" and echoed back as if honored; `limit` < 1 is silently coerced to 1
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Spell.cs:32` (`bool fromDb = source.Equals("db", …)` — no validation of other values), `:75` (`source.ToLowerInvariant()` echoed), `:42`/`:67` (`Math.Max(1, limit)`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"command":"spell-list","source":"overlay"}` (or a typo like "ddb") returns the DAT listing labeled `"source":"overlay"` — the response lies about which store was queried. `limit:0` returns 1 row instead of erroring.
- **What must change:** Validate `source` against {"dat","db"} with a directed `ArgumentException`; reject `limit < 1`.

### F211 — spell-save — LOW: the missing-argument error names a CLI flag ("--from-json <path>") that does not exist in the JSON command surface
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Spell.cs:102` (`throw new ArgumentException("--from-json <path> is required for spell save.")`), reached when the JSON arg `fromJson` is absent (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3120`).
- **What is imprecise:** An agent driving the documented JSON protocol is told to supply a flag its protocol has no syntax for; the convention everywhere else is `Missing 'field' field`.
- **What must change:** Change the message to `Missing 'fromJson' field` (mention the path-to-SpellRecord-JSON expectation in the same sentence if desired).

### F212 — surface-fingerprint — MED: match-spec numbers are parsed with CURRENT-CULTURE float.TryParse, the numeric `type` form rejects hex, and supplying both `id` and `match` silently ignores `match`
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.AssetGraph.cs:282-284` (`float.TryParse(v, out …)` for translucency/luminosity/diffuse — no `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`), `:277-278` (`uint.TryParse(v, …)` for `type` — decimal only, `"0x10"` fails both branches), `:265-274` (when a probe id is present the `match` dict is never consulted).
- **What is imprecise:** On a comma-decimal locale the documented example `{"match":{"luminosity":"0.25"}}` parses to 25 (or fails) → 0 matches reported as honest success (F195 culture class, read-side site). A flags value passed as hex silently matches nothing instead of erroring. And `{"id":…, "match":{…}}` returns the id-fingerprint matches with the match-spec dropped without notice (F177-class silent precedence).
- **What must change:** Parse all three floats and the numeric type with `NumberStyles` + `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`; accept `0x`-prefixed hex for `type` (route through `ParseRegionHexU32`); throw `ArgumentException("Provide either id or match, not both.")` when both are supplied.

### F213 — smooth — MED: docs say "averaging neighboring vertices" — the implementation blends every vertex toward the single mean of the whole brush set, which is plateau-flattening, not neighborhood smoothing
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Terrain/TerrainAlgorithms.cs:215-254` (`ComputeSmooth`: one global `avgHeight` over all affected vertices at `:223-238`, then `original + (avg − original) * strength` at `:245`); claim at `docs/agent_api_reference.md:306`.
- **What is imprecise:** With `strength:1.0` the entire brush becomes a flat plane at the set mean in one application — including a hard discontinuity at the brush rim against unsmoothed terrain — whereas the documented behavior (per-vertex neighbor averaging, i.e. a Laplacian/box kernel) preserves large-scale gradients and converges gently. Agents using smooth to de-spike terrain while keeping a slope get a terrace instead. (The brush radius "world units" claim on the same table is the already-filed F123; row/col 254 exclusion = F122; tile-cache staleness = F112.)
- **What must change:** Either implement per-vertex neighborhood averaging (blend each vertex toward the mean of its 4/8 grid neighbors, iterating once per call) or fix `agent_api_reference.md:306` to state "blends each vertex toward the average height of all vertices in the brush (strength 1.0 = flatten to that average)".

### F214 — snap-portal / tile-stats / set-landblock-heightmap / set-landblock-terrain — MED: no request/response section in docs/agent_api_reference.md (F116/F128/F154/F161/F182/F200 class)
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md` — no section for `snap-portal` or `tile-stats` anywhere; `set-landblock-heightmap`/`set-landblock-terrain` appear only inside the transact example (`:1170`) and op-alphabet prose (`:1213`), with no args/index-order/value-range/response documentation of their own. All four have help one-liners (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2372`, `:2460`, `:2408-2409`).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring docs-gap class. The bulk terrain pair especially needs a documented contract: 81 values in `x*9+y` (column-of-x) flat order matching get-terrain-data's `index` formula, accepted as JSON array or CSV string, byte range 0..255 (types 0..31), seam auto-sync to neighbors.
- **What must change:** Add api-ref sections for the four commands; for set-landblock-* explicitly state the vertex ordering (`index = gridX * 9 + gridY`, same as get-terrain-data) and both accepted encodings; for snap-portal document the hex-string id formats and the surfaces caveat (until F205 is fixed); for tile-stats document the dirtyTileCount-vs-dirtyLbCount distinction.

### F215 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — transact — CRITICAL: the three placement-* ops are allow-listed but have ZERO rollback coverage — placement-add-outdoor/-remove even call project.Save() mid-batch, so a "rolled-back" transact leaves placements permanently committed to disk
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactionEngine.cs:26-36` (AllowedOps includes `placement-add-outdoor`, `placement-add-dungeon`, `placement-remove`) vs `:39-63` (the four snapshot-scope sets — TerrainSingletonOps / LandblockTargetedOps / DungeonTargetedOps / WideLandblockOps — contain NONE of the placement trio; DungeonTargetedOps at `:54-56` holds only `generate-dungeon`). Mutation sites: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Placements.cs:86` and `:120` (`project.Save()` — writes the project file immediately), `:84` (`OutdoorInstancePlacements.Add` on the Project object, which is not a document at all), `:96-110` (`PlacementAddDungeon` mutates/creates `dungeon_XXXX` with no snapshot), `:126-135` (`PlacementRemove kind:dungeon` mutates whichever DungeonDocument the unordered `ActiveDocs` enumeration reaches — and has no lbX/lbY args, so `TryParseLbKeyFromArgs` also contributes nothing to validation scope).
- **What is imprecise:** `SnapshotScope` (`TransactionEngine.cs:319-362`) never captures `Project.OutdoorInstancePlacements` or the placement-targeted dungeon doc, and the rollback path (`:216-236`) restores only snapshotted docs + deletes `createdNow` docs. A batch like `[placement-add-outdoor, <failing op>]` therefore reports `status:"rolled-back"`, `opsRolledBack:1` — while the placement persists in memory AND on disk (Save() already ran). The "commit-or-undo, never half-applied" contract (help `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2455`, api-ref `:1163`) is simply false for 3 of the 19 allow-listed ops. Bonus imprecision: `placement-remove kind:dungeon` resolves the global index by iterating a dictionary, so the same index can delete a different placement between calls.
- **What must change:** Either (a) remove the placement trio from AllowedOps until they have snapshot coverage, or (b) add coverage: snapshot `Project.OutdoorInstancePlacements` (serialize the list) at batch start whenever the batch contains a placement op and restore it on rollback; suppress `project.Save()` inside a transaction (defer to commit); add `placement-add-dungeon` to DungeonTargetedOps so its dungeon doc is snapshotted/tracked via `maybeCreatedIds`; for `placement-remove kind:dungeon` snapshot every active DungeonDocument (WideLandblockOps-style). Also order the dungeon enumeration deterministically (e.g. by doc id).

### F216 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — transact — CRITICAL: rollback is silently incomplete for any landblock/dungeon-targeted op whose document was not loaded at batch start — the mutation survives a "rolled-back" transact, and on commit the doc is missing from the journal, tile invalidation, and transact-diff
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactionEngine.cs:364-374` (`TrySnapshot` only snapshots docs already in `dm.ActiveDocs`; the comment admits "Rollback is best-effort for ops that touch unloaded docs"), `:210-214` (`createdNow` only counts new doc ids that are in `maybeCreatedIds` — dungeon/generate-dungeon only — or `landblock_*` in wideLandblockMode), `:262` (`DocumentsTouched = snapshots.Keys` — the unloaded-doc mutation appears nowhere in the journal). Doc materialization that makes the mutation stick: `CommandEngine.cs:10286-10292` (`GetLandblockDocOrCreate` → `DocumentManager.GetOrCreateDocumentAsync`), `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:146-151` (absent docs are CREATED AND PERSISTED via `CreateDocumentAsync`).
- **What is imprecise:** Scenario: `{"ops":[{"command":"add-object","lbX":10,"lbY":10,...},{<failing op>}]}` where `landblock_0A0A` was never loaded this session. SnapshotScope finds nothing in ActiveDocs → no snapshot. add-object lazy-loads (or creates) the doc and mutates it. Rollback: not in `snapshots` → not restored; not in `createdNow` (not a dungeon id, not wide mode) → not deleted. Response says `status:"rolled-back"`, `opsRolledBack:1` — the object is still there and will be flushed to SQLite. On the COMMIT path the same doc is absent from both `documentsTouched` and `documentsCreated`, so `OnTransactCommitted` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3946-3955`) never invalidates its tile and `transact-diff` has no pre/post bytes for it (the LB never appears in `ExtractLandblockKeys`). The in-code comment calls this rare; the external contract (help `:2455` "atomically commit or rollback", api-ref `:1163` "snapshots affected document projections before any op runs") does not disclose it at all.
- **What must change:** In `SnapshotScope`, for every key in `landblockKeys`/`dungeonKeys` that is NOT in ActiveDocs, either pre-load the doc via `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync` and snapshot it, or record it in `maybeCreatedIds` so rollback deletes it and commit lists it in `documentsCreated` (distinguish "existed in storage but unloaded" — pre-load+snapshot — from "brand new" — delete-on-rollback). At minimum, the journal must list these docs in `documentsTouched`/`documentsCreated` so tile invalidation and transact-diff see them, and `opsRolledBack` must not count ops whose effects were not actually unwound.

### F217 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — transact — HIGH: validate:auto runs the TRN005 seam check on the WRONG neighbors — right/top neighbors are validated (which re-checks seams the touched LB already covers) while left/bottom seam breaks introduced by the batch are never detected
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactionEngine.cs:426-444` (auto mode validates `(lbX+1,lbY)` and `(lbX,lbY+1)` "to catch TRN005 edge mismatches"); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Validation/ValidationEngine.cs:895-937` (`CheckEdgeStitching` only ever compares the validated LB's OWN right edge (x=8) against `lbX+1` and top edge (y=8) against `lbY+1`).
- **What is imprecise:** Seam ownership is directional: the seam between (x−1,y) and (x,y) is only checked when validating (x−1,y). The touched LB's own ValidateAll already covers its right/top seams, so validating the right/top neighbors checks seams (x+1)↔(x+2) and (y+1)↔(y+2) — landblocks the batch did not touch — contributing nothing. Meanwhile an edit to the touched LB's x=0 or y=0 edge vertices breaks the seam to the LEFT/BOTTOM neighbor, which auto never validates: the batch commits clean despite introducing exactly the TRN005 regression the gate exists to catch. (api-ref `:1183` documents "touched LBs + right/top neighbors", faithfully copying the defective scope.)
- **What must change:** In the auto neighbor pass, validate the LEFT and BOTTOM neighbors — `(lbX−1,lbY)` and `(lbX,lbY−1)`, skipping coords that would underflow — whose right/top checks cover the seams back to the touched LB. Drop the right/top neighbor validation (redundant) or keep it only if `CheckEdgeStitching` is extended to check all four edges. Update api-ref `:1183` to match.

### F218 — transact — MED: validate:"all" means "every landblock document currently loaded in memory", not "everything relevant" — a terrain-only batch under validate:all can validate zero of the landblocks it edited; numeric entries in validate:{landblocks:[...]} are silently dropped
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactionEngine.cs:392-403` ("all" iterates `dm.ActiveDocs` for `landblock_*` ids only, ignoring `touchedLbKeys` entirely); `:406-416` (explicit-list mode skips any non-string array entry at `:410` with no warning, so `{"landblocks":[43028]}` validates nothing while returning an empty, passing validation array).
- **What is imprecise:** "all" is load-order-dependent: a raise/paint batch touching LBs that have terrain data but no `landblock_XXXX` object doc gets NO validation under validate:"all" — strictly weaker than the default "auto" — and conversely it grows over time as lazy-created phantom docs (F225) accumulate in ActiveDocs. Nothing in help (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2455`) or api-ref (`:1183`) says "all" = "all loaded object docs". The explicit-list silent-skip means a typo'd numeric LB id converts the validation gate into a no-op that still commits.
- **What must change:** Make "all" the union of ActiveDocs landblocks AND `touchedLbKeys` (or document the loaded-docs semantics verbatim in help + api-ref). In explicit-list mode, reject the batch in `PreflightValidate` when a `landblocks` entry is not a parseable hex string (the preflight already exists for exactly this purpose but only checks the array's presence, `TransactionEngine.cs:307-310`).

### F219 — transact — MED: with rollback_on_fail:false, a failed-but-committed batch skips tile invalidation — real committed mutations leave stale tiles (F112/F143 class, inside transact itself)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3946` (`if (result.Success && result.Status == "committed")` gates `OnTransactCommitted`); `TransactionEngine.cs:247-250` (when `rollback_on_fail:false` and an op or validation failed, `Status` stays `"committed"` and `Success` is `false` — the applied ops are permanent).
- **What is imprecise:** The user opted out of rollback, so every op that succeeded before the failure IS committed (and `CapturePostStateAndRetain` even retains its diff snapshot at `:243`), yet the tile cache is never told. `get-tile` then serves pre-edit imagery with `success:true` for every LB the batch mutated.
- **What must change:** Gate the invalidation hook on `result.Status == "committed" && result.Journal.OpsApplied > 0` (drop the `result.Success` conjunct), so any batch that actually left mutations behind dirties its tiles.

### F220 — transact — MED: a partially-failed bulk-place-objects op commits as a fully-successful op — the per-op gate trusts the inner `success` flag, and bulk-place returns success:true even when every object errored (F17 surfacing inside the atomicity gate)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactionEngine.cs:177` (`ResponseSaysSuccess` reads only the envelope `success`); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3330-3332` (`CmdBulkPlaceObjects` serializes `success = true` unconditionally, with failures only in `errors`/`errorMessages` — F17).
- **What is imprecise:** Inside a transact, "atomic" extends only to op granularity; an op that placed 3 of 10 objects (7 errors) neither trips `rollback_on_fail` nor surfaces in `reason`. The batch commits a partial placement while reporting every op `success:true`.
- **What must change:** Fix F17 at the source (bulk-place `success = r.Errors == 0`, or at least `success=false` when `placed==0`). Until then, `ResponseSaysSuccess` should treat a response with a non-zero `errors` field as a failure for transact purposes, or transact must document that sub-op partial failure is invisible to the gate.

### F221 — transact — MED: both the help one-liner and the api-ref "op alphabet" misstate the allow-list — `import-heightmap` and the three placement-* ops are accepted but undocumented, and the placement trio is precisely the non-atomic subset (F215)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2455` (help: "Allow-list: terrain edits, object placement, generate-dungeon"); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1213` (enumerates 17 of the 19 `AllowedOps` — `import-heightmap`, `placement-add-outdoor`, `placement-add-dungeon`, `placement-remove` missing; cf. `TransactionEngine.cs:26-36`). The api-ref transact table also omits the `renderMode`/`resolution` inline-diff params that the request parser honors (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3969-3970`) — they ARE documented at `:1186-1187`, so only the help args string (`"ops[] | opsFile, rollback_on_fail?, validate?, diff?"`) under-reports.
- **What is imprecise:** An agent reading the docs cannot know placement ops are transactable (and would reasonably assume they get rollback protection — see F215). The clear-objects `all:true` rejection (`TransactionEngine.cs:283-286`) is likewise documented nowhere.
- **What must change:** Update api-ref `:1213` to the literal 19-op list, flagging the placement trio's rollback caveat until F215 is fixed; mention the clear-objects all:true rejection under Failure modes; extend the help args string with `renderMode?`, `resolution?`.

### F222 — transact-diff — MED: terrain-only transactions ignore the `lbs` filter entirely — no per-LB diff, no visual — and the visual note then claims "no landblocks specified" even when the caller specified them
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactDiffEngine.cs:56-64` (`touchedLbKeys` is derived ONLY from `landblock_*`/`dungeon_*` doc ids in the snapshot; a brush/raise/road batch snapshots only the `terrain` doc, so the set is empty and `lbFilter.IntersectWith(empty)` stays empty); `:104` (terrain summary path), `:546-551` (visual returns `Note: "terrain-only batch — no landblocks specified; visual diff omitted"`).
- **What is imprecise:** The transaction engine KNOWS which LBs a terrain batch touched (its own `touchedLbKeys` drive validation) but never stores them in `TransactSnapshotEntry`, so the diff engine cannot honor `{"lbs":[[169,180]]}` — the documented "Restrict diff to specific [lbX,lbY] pairs" (api-ref `:1241`) silently does nothing for exactly the batches where an agent most wants a windowed visual, and the note's wording lies about why.
- **What must change:** Persist the transaction's touched-LB set in `TransactSnapshotEntry` and seed `touchedLbKeys` from it; alternatively, when `terrainTouched && touchedLbKeys.Count == 0 && lbFilter != null`, use the caller's `lbs` as the per-LB scope (the terrain pre/post bytes are retained, so per-LB categorical/validation diffs and the visual are all computable). At minimum change the note to state the filter is unsupported for terrain-only batches.

### F223 — transact-diff — MED: the validation half of the diff compares pre-state against the CURRENT live world, not the transaction's retained post-state — diffing an older tx after later edits mixes foreign changes into validation.added/cleared while the object diff uses snapshot bytes
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactDiffEngine.cs:408-409` (`postReport = _cmd.ValidateAll(lbX, lbY)` — live docs) vs `:204-205` (object diff hydrates `entry.PostState` bytes); `:453-455` even documents that the restore path deliberately restores "current live, not the historical post-snapshot".
- **What is imprecise:** For the most recent transaction the two coincide, but the LRU retains 32 entries precisely so older txs can be diffed; for those, `validation.added`/`cleared` (and `summary.validationDelta`) attribute later transactions' regressions/fixes to this txId, while `objects.added/removed/moved` correctly reflect only this tx. One response, two different "after" states.
- **What must change:** Compute the post report the same way the pre report is computed: swap the live doc to `entry.PostState[docId]` (and post terrain) via `ValidatePreStateViaSwap`'s mechanism, validate, restore current-live. Or document that validation deltas are "vs current state" — but then the summary field name `validationDelta` should change.

### F224 — transact-diff — LOW: unrecognized renderMode strings are silently rendered as overlay yet echoed back as if honored, and touched-LB spans wider than 33×33 are silently cropped by the radius clamp
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/TransactDiffEngine.cs:572` (only `"side-by-side"` is compared; `"overlay"`, `"after-only-with-diff"`, and any typo all take the same path), `:611-612` (`Mode: mode` echoes the caller's string verbatim); `:567` (`radius = Math.Min(16, radius)` — LBs outside the clamped window are excluded from the render with `Note: null`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"renderMode":"side-byside"}` returns an overlay image labeled `"mode":"side-byside"`. A batch spanning >33 LBs renders a partial window with no indication of cropping.
- **What must change:** Validate renderMode against the three documented values (reject otherwise, matching PreflightValidate's philosophy); when the radius clamp excludes any `perLb` entry, set `visual.note` to say which LBs were cropped.

### F225 — validate-dungeon / validate-landblock / validate-all — MED: read-only validators lazily CREATE AND PERSIST documents — validating any never-touched LB plants phantom landblock_/dungeon_ rows in storage, and validate-dungeon then reports DNG001 *error* for every non-dungeon landblock
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1871-1878` (`ValidateDungeon` → `GetDungeonDoc`), `:1880-1888` (`ValidateLandblock` → `GetLandblockDoc`), `:1928-1953` (`ValidateAll` → both); `:10286-10292`/`:10324-10332` (`GetLandblockDocOrCreate`/`GetDungeonDoc` → `GetOrCreateDocumentAsync`); `WorldBuilder.Shared/Documents/DocumentManager.cs:146-151` (absent doc → `CreateDocumentAsync` persists a new row). The codebase's own `TryGetLandblockDoc` (`CommandEngine.cs:10295-10315`) exists specifically "so query/mutate ops don't lazy-create phantom docs" — the validators don't use it.
- **What is imprecise:** (a) `{"command":"validate-landblock","lbX":200,"lbY":200}` on a virgin LB creates and persists `landblock_C8C8`; validate-dungeon/validate-all additionally create `dungeon_C8C8`. These phantom docs then inflate transact's `validate:"all"` scope (F218) and ActiveDocs forever. (b) Because GetDungeonDoc always materializes an empty doc, `validate-dungeon` on any outdoor LB returns `isValid:false` with DNG001 "Dungeon has no cells." at ERROR severity — indistinguishable from a genuinely broken empty dungeon — while `validate-all` on the same LB skips the dungeon check entirely (`ValidationEngine.cs:835` requires `Cells.Count > 0`), so the two commands contradict each other on the same input.
- **What must change:** Route the validators through `TryGetLandblockDoc` (and an equivalent `TryGetDungeonDoc` that checks ActiveDocs + storage existence without creating); when no dungeon exists, validate-dungeon should return a distinct info-level "no dungeon document for this landblock" (e.g. DNG000) instead of fabricating an empty doc and flagging it DNG001.

### F226 — validate-all — MED: composition is lossy and the docs miscount it — empty dungeons that validate-dungeon flags as DNG001 pass validate-all clean, doc-load failures silently drop whole validator families, and api-ref says "all four validators" when five run
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/Validation/ValidationEngine.cs:835` (`dungeonDoc.Cells.Count > 0` gate — DNG001 unreachable via validate-all); `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:1934-1948` (each doc fetch wrapped in `try { } catch { }` — a throwing `GetTerrainHelpers` silently removes ALL TRN checks from the combined report, with `isValid:true` still possible); `docs/agent_api_reference.md:1141` ("Runs **all four validators**" — dungeon, landblock, terrain, building-shells, building-portals = five; the validate-* quick table at `:190-194` and TOC at `:43-47` likewise omit validate-building-shells).
- **What is imprecise:** An agent treating validate-all as a superset of the individual commands (the docs say "recommended validation command") gets strictly fewer diagnostics in two real cases, with no marker in the response that a family was skipped.
- **What must change:** In validate-all, when a validator family is skipped (doc fetch threw, dats null, heightTable null) emit an info diagnostic naming the skipped family; align the empty-dungeon behavior with whatever F225's resolution picks (skip-with-info or DNG001) so the two commands agree; fix api-ref `:1141` to "all five" and add validate-building-shells to the TOC/table.

### F227 — validate-building-shells — MED: no section in docs/agent_api_reference.md — absent from the TOC, the quick table, and the Validation chapter; the BSH code family is documented nowhere (F116/F128/F154/F161/F182/F200/F214 class)
- **Where:** `docs/agent_api_reference.md:43-47` (TOC lists the other four validators), `:190-194` (quick table — same omission), Validation chapter `:1050-1148` (sections for dungeon/landblock/terrain/portals/all only). Help row exists (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2362`).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring docs-gap class; the BSH009 group-Z-divergence check (and its dependency on loaded pairings) is source-only.
- **What must change:** Add a `### validate-building-shells` section (request `lbX`,`lbY`; BSH001-BSH009 code summaries; note that BSH009 fires only after `load-building-pairings`), and add the command to the TOC + quick table + the validate-all composition list.

### F228 — wave4-status / wave4-sweep — MED: in neither `help` nor docs/agent_api_reference.md — args (mode/target/concurrency/reset), the WORLDBUILDER_WAVE4_SWEEP/WORLDBUILDER_NODE env overrides, the 6-hour timeout, and the hardcoded /mnt/wbterminal1 cache/report roots are all source-only (F23/F116/F182 class)
- **Where:** dispatched at `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:344-345`; the help array (`:2335-2486`) has no wave4 rows (grep `name = "wave4` → zero hits); `docs/agent_api_reference.md` has zero occurrences of "wave4". Source contract: `CommandEngine.Wave4.cs:136-147` (timeout + roots), `:297-304` (args), `:404`/`:528` (env vars).
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring docs-gap class — an agent cannot discover the valid `target` enum (`all|surface|texture-chain|mesh|env-cell`) or the concurrency [1,32] bound without reading source; invalid values throw ArgumentException as the whole response's `error`.
- **What must change:** Add help rows (`wave4-status` args `""`; `wave4-sweep` args `"mode?, target?, concurrency?, reset?"`) and api-ref sections documenting the enums, defaults (fast/all/4/false), timeout, cache/report roots, and both env overrides.

### F229 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — wave4-sweep — HIGH: the response is built from the newest sweep-report.json on disk by mtime, not from THIS run — a driver that dies before writing a report presents a STALE prior run's counts/paths as this run's outcome, and a corrupt report's parse error is overwritten with driverError=null
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Wave4.cs:368` (`FindLatestSweepReportJson(Wave4ReportRoot)` after the subprocess exits — no correlation of the report dir to this run's timestamp/slug), `:384-391` (`return parsed with { ExitCode = exitCode, ElapsedMs = ..., DriverError = timedOut ? "..." : null }` — unconditionally replaces `ParseSweepReport`'s read/parse `DriverError` from `:458`/`:471` with null when not timed out).
- **What is imprecise:** Two lying paths: (1) orchestrator crashes mid-run after a previous successful sweep → exitCode≠0 is reported, but `sweepReportJsonPath`/`summary.{chunkCount,passed,failed,...}` describe the OLD run (the record doc at `:106` even calls it "the freshly written sweep-report.json"), and the wrapper's `success = string.IsNullOrEmpty(driverError) && failed==0` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5402`) can come out TRUE on a crashed run. (2) Driver succeeds but writes an unparseable report → counts all zero, driverError null, success true.
- **What must change:** Snapshot `FindLatestSweepReportJson` BEFORE launching and treat the run's report as found only if the path/mtime differs afterwards (or have the orchestrator print its report path on stdout and parse that); when no NEW report exists, return the existing "no sweep-report.json was found" DriverError branch (`:369-382`). Stop overwriting `parsed.DriverError` — merge: keep the parse error when present, append the timeout note when timed out. Fold `exitCode != 0` into the wrapper's `success` computation.

### F230 — wave4-sweep — MED: `success:true` for an all-INFRA sweep — the wrapper's success predicate ignores InfraChunks and the orchestrator exit code, contradicting the command's own exit-code contract (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=INFRA)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:5402` (`success = string.IsNullOrEmpty(r.DriverError) && r.FailedChunks == 0`); contract at `CommandEngine.Wave4.cs:108` (`ExitCode ... 0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=INFRA`).
- **What is imprecise:** A sweep where every chunk returned INFRA (siblings not wired, IO errors) exits 2 and validated nothing, yet the JSON envelope says `success:true` with `summary.passed:0` — an agent keying off `success` concludes the parity sweep passed.
- **What must change:** `success = string.IsNullOrEmpty(r.DriverError) && r.ExitCode == 0` (or additionally `&& r.FailedChunks == 0 && r.InfraChunks == 0`), so the envelope agrees with the documented exit-code semantics.

### F231 — wave4-status — LOW: chunk counts don't reconcile (unreadable progress.json is counted in chunkCount but in neither completed nor in-flight), and "most-recent failure" degrades to filesystem-enumeration order whenever failures lack finishedAt
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Wave4.cs:184-196` (`chunkCount++` precedes the read/parse `continue`s, so corrupt files leave `completedChunks + inFlightChunks < chunkCount` with no "unreadable" bucket); `:222-226` (`if (lastFailWhen == null || ...)` — while `lastFailWhen` stays null, EVERY subsequent FAIL overwrites the label regardless of recency, so the reported "last failure" is just the last directory enumerated).
- **What is imprecise:** The record doc (`:78-83`) defines CompletedChunks as "status != INFLIGHT", which unreadable chunks violate silently; the LastFailure fields claim recency they don't have.
- **What must change:** Add an `unreadableChunks` counter (or count parse failures as completed-with-unknown-status explicitly) so the buckets sum to chunkCount; in the last-failure selection, prefer entries WITH finishedAt over entries without, and only fall back to enumeration order when no failure has a timestamp.

### F232 — validate-terrain / validate-all — LOW: `cliffThreshold` is unvalidated — NaN silently disables every TRN002 cliff check (all comparisons false), and zero/negative values flag every vertex pair
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:2110` and `:2126` (`node["cliffThreshold"]?.GetValue<float>() ?? DefaultCliffThreshold` — no finiteness/positivity check, unlike the `F()` helper's `IsFinite` guard at `:3590-3595`); consumed at `ValidationEngine.cs:530`/`:542` (`delta > cliffThreshold`).
- **What is imprecise:** `{"cliffThreshold": NaN}` (or a JSON null-ish coercion upstream) yields a clean terrain report that an agent reads as "no cliffs", with no error. Same value also flows into transact's validation step via `engine.ValidateAll(lbX, lbY)` defaults only — but the standalone command accepts the caller's raw float.
- **What must change:** Validate with the existing pattern: reject non-finite values and values ≤ 0 with `'cliffThreshold' must be a positive finite number; got {v}` (mirror `FloatInRange`, `JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3611-3621`).

### F233 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — weenie-save / weenie-insert — CRITICAL: System.Text.Json never populates AceWeenieSnapshot's get-only List properties, so weenie-save silently WIPES every scalar row of the target weenie (and weenie-insert creates property-less weenies) no matter what the input JSON contains
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceWeenieSnapshot.cs:47-53` (all 7 scalar lists are `{ get; }` only); deserialize sites `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Weenie.cs:24-26` (save) and `:45-47` (insert) using `JsonOpts.CaseInsensitive` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/JsonOpts.cs:23-25` — no `PreferredObjectCreationHandling`); destructive sink `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.Weenie.cs:459-470` (`ReplaceRowsAsync` = unconditional `DELETE … WHERE object_Id` then insert the — always empty — list).
- **What is imprecise:** STJ ignores collection properties without setters unless `JsonObjectCreationHandling.Populate` is opted into (verified empirically on this machine with the exact class shape: `Ints.Count == 0` after deserializing `{"Ints":[{"Type":1,"Value":42},…]}`). So `{"command":"weenie-save","classId":N,"fromJson":…}` deserializes ClassId/WeenieType (settable) but ZERO rows for ints/int64s/bools/floats/strings/dataIds/instanceIds — then SaveWeenieScalarsAsync deletes every existing scalar row in all 7 `weenie_properties_*` tables and inserts nothing, commits, and returns `success:true, ints:0, …` (counts honestly say 0, but the destruction already happened and nothing says "your file's rows were dropped"). If the JSON omits `weenieType`, `weenie.type` is additionally UPDATEd to 0. The natural round-trip — `weenie-snapshot full:true` → edit → `weenie-save` — hits this every time (the snapshot serializes the lists fine on the way OUT; getters are enough for writing). weenie-insert is the same defect minus pre-existing data: it always creates a weenie with `totalScalarRows:0`.
- **What must change:** Give the 7 list properties setters (`{ get; set; }`), or mark them `[JsonObjectCreationHandling(JsonObjectCreationHandling.Populate)]`, or use a dedicated `JsonSerializerOptions { PreferredObjectCreationHandling = Populate }` at both deserialize sites. Then add a guard in `WeenieSaveScalarsAsync`'s caller: if ALL 7 lists are empty, refuse with "snapshot JSON contains no scalar rows — refusing to wipe class_Id N (pass an explicit flag to force)". Regression test: weenie-snapshot(full) → weenie-save must be a no-op on row counts.

### F234 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — weenie-template-apply — CRITICAL: the only stated contract says "Existing scalars not in the template are left untouched", but the implementation is a full delete-and-replace — applying a 3-property template destroys every other scalar property of the weenie and overwrites weenie.type
- **Where:** Claim: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10663-10666` (XML doc on `WeenieTemplateApplyAsync`). Behavior: `:10693-10700` builds a snapshot containing ONLY the template's rows, `:10709` passes it to `SaveWeenieScalarsAsync`, which deletes ALL rows per table before inserting (`AceDbConnector.Weenie.cs:459-470`) and UPDATEs `weenie.type` to the template's `WeenieType` (`:170-180`) — which itself silently defaults to 1 when the bundle omits/garbles `weenieType` (`WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/WeenieTemplateDefinition.cs:60-61`).
- **What is imprecise:** "Applies a template's scalar properties … existing scalars not in the template are left untouched" describes a merge; the code performs replacement. An agent scaffolding properties onto an existing weenie loses every int/float/string/did/iid row the template doesn't re-state, plus gets `weenie.type` rewritten (to 1, for a typed bundle with a typo'd `weenieType` key). `scalarsApplied` reports only what was written, not what was destroyed.
- **What must change:** Either implement the stated merge (load the existing snapshot via `LoadWeenieSnapshotAsync`, overlay the template rows by `(table, type)` key, save the union; preserve existing `weenie.type` unless the template explicitly sets one) — or fix the XML doc and the response to say "replaces ALL scalar properties" and add a `replaced:true` field. The merge is what the wording promises; prefer it.

### F235 ✅ **FIXED 2026-06-11** — weenie-save / weenie-insert / weenie-delete / weenie-snapshot / weenie-template-apply — HIGH: every weenie DB primitive swallows MySqlException (F209 class), and the save/insert/delete responses have no error field at all — DB-down, auth failure, duplicate class_Name, and "weenie absent" are all indistinguishable
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.Weenie.cs:252-254` (`SaveWeenieScalarsAsync` catch → false), `:324-326` (`InsertWeenieAsync` catch → 0), `:446-448` (`DeleteWeenieAsync` catch → false), `:154-156` (`LoadWeenieSnapshotAsync` catch → null, which `CommandEngine.cs:10603` then reports as `"Weenie {classId} not found."`). Response shapes: `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3057-3063` / `:3070-3076` / `:3081-3085` serialize result records (`CommandResults.cs:1313-1325`) that have NO Error member.
- **What is imprecise:** Same defect class as F209, new surface. A dead/misconfigured DB produces `{"success":false,"command":"weenie-save","classId":"0x…","ints":0,…}` — identical to "class_Id doesn't exist". For weenie-insert it also hides the unlocked `SELECT MAX(class_Id)` allocation race (`:270-275` — no `FOR UPDATE`; a concurrent insert's duplicate-key explosion surfaces as a bare `newClassId:"0x00000000"`), and a duplicate `class_Name` (unique in ACE schema) the same way. weenie-snapshot actively lies: infrastructure failure reported as row absence.
- **What must change:** In all four connector methods, stop catching `MySqlException` blanket (or rethrow as a typed error); plumb an `Error` string through `WeenieSaveScalarsResult`/`WeenieInsertResult`/`WeenieDeleteResult` and emit it in the three wrappers. In `WeenieSnapshotAsync`, distinguish `null` (row absent) from thrown (DB failure) — only the former may say "not found". For insert, use `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (or `INSERT … SELECT COALESCE(MAX…)`) so allocation is race-safe.

### F236 — weenie-delete — MED: the per-table catch swallows ALL MySqlExceptions, so a mid-wipe failure still COMMITS a partial delete; `weenie_properties_emote_action` is deleted by a column it doesn't have; and deleting a nonexistent classId still wipes+commits orphan property rows while answering success:false
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/AceDbConnector.Weenie.cs:428-434` (`try { … } catch (MySqlException) { /* table may not exist on older schemas */ }` inside the loop), table list `:414-427` (includes `weenie_properties_emote_action` at `:422`), commit `:443` unconditional, return `deleted > 0` `:444`.
- **What is imprecise:** (1) The catch is meant for missing tables but eats lock-wait timeouts, permission errors, etc. — those tables keep their rows while the rest are deleted AND committed, defeating the documented "single transaction so a partial failure rolls back" claim (`:404-406` and api-ref:1860 "transactional helper"). (2) In ACE's schema `weenie_properties_emote_action` keys on `emote_Id` (FK → `weenie_properties_emote.id`), not `object_Id` — see e.g. `external/WeenieViewer/EmoteScript/EmoteScript.Tests/Scripts/cow.sql:6` and `external/melt/Source/misc/aceDatabaseUtilities.cs:2251` — so that DELETE throws "Unknown column" on EVERY run and is swallowed; emote_action rows only disappear via the FK ON DELETE CASCADE from the emote delete, and on a schema without that cascade they'd be silently orphaned. (3) When the weenie row doesn't exist, all property deletes still run and commit (cleaning any orphans) yet the response is a bare `success:false` — a mutating "not found".
- **What must change:** Restrict the inner catch to `MySqlErrorCode.NoSuchTable` (1146) / `BadFieldError` (1054 — and for 1054 drop emote_action from the list entirely, relying on the cascade, with a comment) and rethrow anything else so the transaction rolls back. Check weenie existence FIRST and return early (no mutation) when absent.

### F237 — weenie-snapshot / weenie-template-apply — MED: `classId` rejects "0x…" hex strings (opaque InvalidOperationException) while weenie-save / weenie-delete accept them for the SAME field name (F81/F106 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3597-3598` (`U()` — bare `GetValue<uint>()`), used at `:3799` (CmdWeenieSnapshot) and `:3838` (CmdWeenieTemplateApply); contrast `ParseUintField` `:3265-3275` (hex-or-decimal) used at `:3054`/`:3079` for the same `classId` field.
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring inconsistent-coercion class (F81, F106). `{"command":"weenie-snapshot","classId":"0x00007A1A"}` throws STJ's kind-mismatch InvalidOperationException (no field name, no hint), while the identical argument works on weenie-save/weenie-delete.
- **What must change:** Use `ParseUintField(node, "classId")` at both call sites (drop `U()` here; audit its other callers for the same fix opportunity).

### F238 — weenie-template-list / weenie-template-apply — MED: bundle parsing silently drops malformed templates and rows — a template missing `title`, a bool row written as `"value":1`, or a mistyped `weenieType` vanish or default (to 1) with no warning, so templateCount/scalarsApplied under-report invisibly
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Shared/Lib/AceDb/WeenieTemplateDefinition.cs:33-46` (`TryParseOne` false → template skipped, no diagnostics), `:52-53` (missing id/title → reject), `:60-61` (`weenieType` missing OR non-uint → silently 1), `:84-93`/`:106-118` etc. (each Read*Props `continue`s past rows whose `type`/`value` don't parse at the expected JSON kind — e.g. bools require literal true/false).
- **What is imprecise:** An author's typo'd bundle yields `success:true` with quietly smaller counts; on template-apply that means fewer scalars written than the bundle states (and per F234, everything else destroyed). Nothing in either response distinguishes "template has 3 ints" from "template has 9 ints, 6 unparseable".
- **What must change:** Have `ParseBundle` collect per-template/per-row skip reasons (e.g. `warnings: ["template[2]: missing title", "ints[4]: value not an int32"]`) and surface them in both commands' responses; treat an explicitly present but unparseable `weenieType` as an error, not default-1.

### F239 — weenie-snapshot / weenie-template-list / weenie-template-apply / worldgen / worldgen-analyze-buildings / worldgen-scan-retail-towns — MED: six dispatched commands appear in neither `help` nor docs/agent_api_reference.md (F82/F116/F227/F228 class)
- **Where:** Dispatch `WorldBuilder.Terminal/JsonCommandProcessor.cs:278-283`; the help array `:2334-2486` and `docs/agent_api_reference.md` contain none of the six (the api-ref's only nearby mention is export-towns-csv pointing AT "worldgen --output", `:1898-1901` — a command the reference never defines). Args (`full`, `bundlePath`/`templateId`, the 14 worldgen params, `outputPath`, `apply`) are source-only. Additionally `CmdWorldGen` (`:3744-3766`) exposes neither `SeaLevelIndex` nor `MountainScale` (`WorldGeneratorParams.cs:21-22`) — the only two generator params unreachable from JSON, an undocumented silent omission.
- **What is imprecise:** Recurring docs-absence class. Worst concrete effect: F72's CSV-export chain is documented as consuming "the JSON shape produced by `worldgen --output`" while `worldgen` itself is undocumented, so the producing half of that workflow is unguessable.
- **What must change:** Add the six entries to the help array and an api-ref section each (weenie-snapshot: classId/full + counts shape; template-list/apply: bundle JSON schema incl. the array-or-object root; worldgen: all params with defaults + `apply` + `outputPath` and a pointer to export-towns-csv; the two scanners: outputPath + response shapes). Either wire `seaLevelIndex`/`mountainScale` into CmdWorldGen or document their fixed defaults.

### F240 — worldgen — LOW: when `apply:true` succeeds but the `outputPath` JSON write fails, the response is `success:false` even though the project documents were already mutated — a naive retry double-appends every building and decoration
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.cs:10434-10461` (write-failure return: `Success:false, Applied: applied` with error "Applied OK, but JSON write failed: …"); append-only apply at `:10496-10515` (`AddStaticObject` per building/decoration — no idempotency key).
- **What is imprecise:** `applied:true` and the error text do carry the truth, but `success:false` is the field agents branch on; re-running the identical command duplicates all placed statics (terrain overwrite is idempotent; the appends are not).
- **What must change:** Return `success:true` with a `warnings:[…]` entry for the failed JSON write (the generation+apply DID succeed), or at minimum document that `applied:true && success:false` means "do not retry with apply".

### F241 — weenie-save / weenie-insert — LOW: missing-`fromJson` errors tell the caller to pass a `--from-json <path>` CLI flag that does not exist in the JSON protocol (F45 class)
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.Weenie.cs:20` and `:41` (`"--from-json <path> is required…"`), surfaced verbatim by `CmdWeenieSave`/`CmdWeenieInsert` (`JsonCommandProcessor.cs:3053-3076`).
- **What is imprecise:** Same class as F45 (compare-to-retail / creature-save). The JSON field is `fromJson`.
- **What must change:** Reword both to `"Missing 'fromJson' field (path to AceWeenieSnapshot JSON)"`.

### F242 — chorizite-wire-pack-message / chorizite-wire-unpack-message — MED: the in-source "Header-mode contract (CRITICAL…)" comment contradicts the code on both counts — payload mode for game actions does NOT include {sequence, actionType} and for top-level messages does NOT include the opcode; a sibling comment also miscounts the event header as 16 bytes
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WireConformance.cs:47-51` (claims payload mode = "{sequence, actionType, payload}" for actions and "{opcode, fields}" for top-level) vs the implementation: `:234-240` (payload mode routes actions/events through `EmitPayloadOnlySubclass`, which strips those very bytes at `:343-347` — 8 for actions, 12 for events — and calls bare `instance.Write` with NO opcode for top-level); `:324` ("16 bytes for Ordered_GameEvent header") vs `:345` (`=> 12`).
- **What is imprecise:** The external doc `docs/wire-conformance-method.md:100-110` describes the CODE's behavior correctly (payload = body only, no envelope, no opcode), so the bytes are right — but the class-level remark explicitly billed as the contract "CRITICAL for matching Rust holtburger-protocol" states the opposite byte layout. Anyone re-implementing or reviewing against the in-source contract (the file's own stated purpose) would call correct output wrong, or "fix" it into divergence.
- **What must change:** Rewrite `:47-51` to: payload mode emits the subclass payload ONLY (no sequence/actionType for actions, no objectId/sequence/eventType for events, no opcode for top-level); full mode adds opcode + the respective envelope. Fix `:324` to "12 bytes … objectId + sequence + eventType" and delete the stale rewind sentence.

### F243 — chorizite-wire-pack-message — LOW: silent field-population hazards — out-of-range numerics wrap to the target width and unknown `fields` keys are ignored, both yielding plausible wrong bytes under success:true
- **Where:** `WorldBuilder.Terminal/CommandEngine.WireConformance.cs:571-578` (`(uint)UInt32Parse(node)` etc. — unchecked casts truncate, and `:663` `(ulong)l64` wraps negatives), `:548-553` (`PopulateFromJson` `continue`s past JSON keys that match no field — a typo'd field silently keeps its default).
- **What is imprecise:** For a conformance oracle, `{"fields":{"ObjectID":"0x1234"}}` (wrong case is fine, wrong spelling is not) packs ObjectId=0 with no complaint; `"Power": 4294967297` writes 1. The roundtrip/fixture diff usually catches it downstream, but the pack response itself gives no signal.
- **What must change:** Throw on unmatched field names (list valid names in the message), and range-check before each narrowing cast (`'{field}' value {v} out of range for {type}`).
